# shroom v1 — build decisions

> In-frame design calls made during the build (CLAUDE.md, Build rules). Each records what
> was chosen, why, and what was rejected, so Bagrat can review and reverse any of them at a
> checkpoint. Stack-level choices live in the stack decision (2026-07-20). Where the build
> departs from the spec, the entry goes to [the deviations](#deviations-from-the-spec)
> below, not to a B-row.

B1–B120 are indexed here one line each; their full rows (chosen, why, rejected) are in
[`history/decisions/B001-B060.md`](../history/decisions/B001-B060.md) and
[`history/decisions/B061-B120.md`](../history/decisions/B061-B120.md). From B121 on, the rows
are here in full, and a new decision is a new row at the end of that table.

## From B121 on

| # | Call | Chosen | Why | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B121 | A kept take of a camera, on the hosted Mac only (amends B55 a) | **`--drive take <out> camera-take`** (`app/test/take-drive.mjs camera-take`, the `take-camera` scenario): Camera as the source through the real flow — `config`, Return, the count, five seconds, the pause, Stop — and the take kept: `camera-take.mp4` and a frame from its middle beside the report, handed to the evidence. **Two fences instead of B55 a's “never a camera”**: before Return, the camera the core picked must be named `OBS Virtual Camera` (else refused, as B55 a refuses); after Stop, a frame of the take must show the test pattern's colour bars (`bars_seen`, five of six), else the take is deleted and the drive fails. Every other drive keeps B55 a as it was | B55 a's rule keeps unattended takes to the pattern because a camera is the room. The hosted Mac has no room: its only camera is OBS's virtual camera playing ffmpeg's `testsrc2`, set up by `dev/mac/camera.sh` (B120). The name alone would let an OBS showing anything through on another Mac, so the picture is read too, and a take that isn't the pattern doesn't survive the drive. `signals` still records the real camera only to discard it (B47) | Lifting B55 a for any camera in a harness run (the room, kept); fencing by the runner's environment (a dev app started with `open` doesn't inherit it); reading the camera's picture before Return through a second session on the camera (a second capture path; the take's own frames are the thing to judge) |
| B122 | The canvas's look in the build (DEVIATIONS #43–#45): one theme for every window, and the light theme derived from the dark canvas by one token map. **To ratify: Bagrat** | **(a) One theme.** `app/src/lib/theme.css` holds the canvas's tokens and the pieces every window shares — the grouped card of rows with hairlines, the six Scene button styles (`filled`, `plain`, `quiet`, `red-text`, `red-outline`, `link`), the segmented control, the switch, fields — and every window and the popover load it first; their own sheets use its names only. Rejected: a token block per window (the old `onboarding.css` and `Popover.svelte` each had one, and they had drifted — two blues, two reds). **(b) Light, derived, not drawn frame by frame.** The canvas is dark; light is the same structure through one map: window `#f6f7f9`, card `#ffffff` with a `#dfe3ea` edge (a light card needs its edge; a dark one sits on contrast), hairline `#dfe3ea`, text `#2e3440`, secondary `#4c566a` (Nord's `#7b8494` is only 3.5:1 on light, so it is kept for decor and greyed rows), Frost deepened to `#4c6f9a` for white text and links (`#5e81ac` is 4.0:1), red `#a3474f`, amber dot `#b4821f` on `#f6ecd2`, green `#5d8a45`. Two light frames are drawn on the canvas (the popover, the Library) to prove the map. Rejected: drawing every frame twice (forty more frames, and two sources of truth to drift). **(c) The popover's pickers are the canvas's own menus, not native `<select>`s** — Bagrat ruled out select boxes: a row (icon, the pick's name, a chevron) opens a menu of the Scene's choices, the “none” choice last under a hairline; ↓/↑/Return/Esc work in it and are its own (the popover's Return and Esc never see them); a click outside closes it; the window grows to hold an open menu (`popover_fit`). Camera only shows a crossed display (Bagrat's comment on the canvas, 2026-10-13: no display, only the camera, picked from the menu with the camera icon). A row is greyed only when nothing real can be picked — nothing live but No mic or Camera off (no camera at all, a denied microphone); one device is still a pick, so Camera only on a Mac with one camera keeps Show yourself live on it, its menu that one camera (Bagrat asked to keep the camera picker enabled; seen greyed on the hosted Mac's one camera, 2026-10-13). Rejected: greying any row with fewer than two choices (the first build) — a greyed camera name read as the camera being off while its picture recorded. Rejected: a transparent native `<select>` over the row (macOS's menu, but not the canvas's, and no hairline before the none choice). **(d) The count's scrim follows the Mac's appearance**: the spec's light scrim on a light Mac (N22), the canvas's Nord night scrim on a dark one, the digit 168 px semibold. **(e) The app icon** is drawn by `tools/render-app-icon.mjs` on macOS's grid (an 824-unit square, radius 185, on a 1024 canvas). **(f) A plan row is its title, its aside and its fact, split by the core** (`PlanRow.title`, `aside`, `fact`, cut from the row's own line — never new words): the canvas's title with a quiet line under it, and a done row's fact at its end. The line's brackets and dash stay in the row as text read aloud but not drawn. Rejected: the webview cutting the line at its punctuation (it guessed wrong on a fact with its own brackets — N42's “v” note). **(g) The setup window:** the mushroom + “shroom” header; while shroom works on a step, that row's arrow turns into the spinner (the Wiring and Joining frames); the welcome pair and its twins keep law 8's order — the filled action first — and the canvas was corrected to it (the spec wins); the dead invite's door is its line over an open paste field. **(h) The Library and Settings:** a row's Open page and Delete… take room only on hover or selection (always there, they clipped the state word at 680 px); the Settings tabs are a segmented control in a band under the native title bar (drawing in the title bar would take a shell change); Settings · Space's rows are 36 px, not the canvas's 40, so the tallest pane fits the fixed window; Disconnect this Mac… and Leave this space… are drawn red as the canvas has them, their Scene style unchanged. **(i) The page:** the control bar sits over the picture's foot and fades while the video plays and the pointer rests (a bar under the picture would make the layout jump as it hides); the speed button hides under a 360 px viewport (tiny embeds); the embed's mark rides in the control row and fades with it. |
| B123 | The capture crates' MIT notices ship in the app (legal audit, 2026-10-13) | `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` at the root reproduces the MIT notices of the git dependencies the binary links — `scap-screencapturekit` and `cap-camera-avfoundation` (rev `fb3f2438`) and `cidre` (rev `bf84b67079a8`) — and the release bundle (and, until B148 retired it, the preview's) carries it at `Contents/Resources/licenses/third-party/NOTICES.md`; `tools/release-build.sh` refuses a bundle without it | MIT's one condition is the notice in every copy, and a compiled app is a copy; before this the release shipped FFmpeg's notice only | **Proved by:** runner `the_release_bundle_carries_its_encoder_and_its_licence` (+ the notice resource, each git dependency named, both MIT texts whole); `the_preview_bundle_is_the_release_bundle_under_its_own_identifier` unchanged | An About-window licence screen (more UI for the same obligation); a generated notice for every crate (`cargo-about`, a machine change — open, see the audit report) |
| B124 | Every third party's licence ships with what shroom ships (legal audit, 2026-10-13; **amends B123**, page **VERSION 0.1.7**) | **(a) The repo:** a root `LICENSE` (MIT, the workspace's declared licence; the POC's own text). **(b) The app:** `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` is now generated by `tools/third-party-notices.py` — every crate `shroom-app` links on aarch64-apple-darwin (normal dependencies, transitively, from `cargo metadata --locked`; build and dev dependencies never reach the binary), the npm packages the webviews' bundle carries (`svelte`, `@tauri-apps/api` — what `vite build` puts in `app/dist`, read from its source maps) and hls.js: a table of component, version, licence taken and source (the MPL-2.0 crates' Source Code Form is where it points), then each distinct licence text once with every component under it. Of an `A OR B` the first of a fixed preference whose text the package ships is taken; a package that publishes no licence file gets the standard text (`tools/licence-texts/`, or MIT/BSD with its authors); a git dependency gets its repository's. CI's Tests job and `tools/release-build.sh` run `--check`, so a dependency change without a regenerated file fails before it ships. **(c) The page:** `worker/vendor/hls.min.js` is the pinned build behind one comment carrying hls.js's notice and the whole Apache-2.0 text, so every viewer's browser receives the licence with the code — a page change, so VERSION 0.1.7 (`tools/bump-version.sh`; 0.1.5 is released and 0.1.6 is the design canvas's page, B122) | MIT, BSD, ISC, Zlib, Apache-2.0 and Unicode-3.0 each ask for their notice in every copy, and the release carried FFmpeg's and three crates'; Apache-2.0 asks that recipients get the licence, and the page served hls.js bare; with no `LICENSE` the public repo granted nothing | **Proved by:** `tools/third-party-notices.py --check` (CI); runner `the_release_bundle_carries_its_encoder_and_its_licence` (+ the generated file, the git dependencies, tauri, svelte, @tauri-apps/api, hls.js listed); `worker/test/vendor.test.js` (the file pinned whole; after its comment, byte for byte the pinned 1.7.3 build that `node_modules` holds; the notice and the whole licence in the comment); `worker/test/page-version.test.js` (0.1.6's row) | `cargo-about` (a machine change, and an opaque config for what a 300-line script states); the licence at a separate URL on the page (a route, a second bucket asset, and the code still arriving bare); a hand-kept notices file (stale at the next `cargo update`) |
| B125 | The security audit's findings, closed (history/audits/2026-10-13-repo-audit.md §2; WORKER-INTERFACE §4.8, §4.9, §4.17, §4.18 amended) | **(M1) A deleted video stays deleted.** A recording's upload credential may write its meta for seven days (N32), so a tombstone it could rewrite was no tombstone: after a delete the Mac seals it — a device through `POST /_shroom/api/gone` (its maker or owner-class, only on a tombstone; idempotent), a token Mac by writing the key itself — into `.shroom/gone/<id>`, which no recording credential reaches. Every read of a record honors it first (`readMeta`, `metaFact`): removed whatever the meta says, and every mint on it but an owner-class `manage` is 410. An unsealed delete (a page from before this) is logged and still stands. *Follow-up (2026-10-14):* the owner title edit and the Library's body reads (every scope that lists) read `meta/<id>.json` directly and skipped the record, so a meta a stale credential rewrote after the seal listed as live and could be renamed; both now HEAD it too — a sealed id's title edit is 409, and it lists as a tombstone `{schema, id, state:"deleted", createdAt}` (WORKER-INTERFACE §4.10, §4.17, §4.20; page **VERSION 0.1.9**). The same day: `POST gone` on an id with neither a ledger entry nor a meta is 404 `absent` (any active device could seal an id nobody had recorded, and its maker's first mint was then 410; no real delete reaches it — a device's delete needs a `manage` mint, already 404 there); the app reads a listed tombstone as a deleted row (`shroom_upload::meta::listed` — a tombstone has no title, so it never parsed as a whole meta and a cached row stayed live); a token Mac's own listing lists `.shroom/gone/` beside `meta/`, a sealed id a deleted row with no read. **(M2) Only the release's own signer is installed.** The updater swaps a staged copy in only if it meets the replaced app's own designated requirement (`shroom_update::same_signer`: `codesign -d -r-` of the installed app, then `codesign --verify --deep --strict -R=` on the copy) — for a release, `identifier "am.shroom" and certificate leaf = H"<release pin>"`, the rule macOS keeps the app's permissions by; a copy that fails is deleted, never applied. The installer verifies every download's signature whole, and from the public releases pins the signer to `tools/release-identity.sha1` (its test holds the two equal); a local stand-in's (the VM, preview and checkpoint lanes, signed `shroom-dev`) is checked whole but not pinned. **(L1)** The cloud hook holds the live test token in a local and unsets it before `setup.sh`, whose `npm ci` runs dependencies' install scripts; `setup.sh` unsets it too. **(L2)** `/m/` serves only `.m3u8 .m4s .mp4 .jpg .vtt`; every media answer is `nosniff` with `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'`. **(L3)** A `refused` health report counts only from the recording's maker or owner-class (403 `not-yours`). **(L4)** 60 page keys a device-hour, counted beside the upload budget in its rate object (`k`); the token Mac's sweep already reaps unspent grants. **(L5)** vitest 3 → 5 (tinypool's prototype-pollution RCE) and an override past sharp 0.35.4 under miniflare; `npm audit` reads 0. **(L6)** CI's Advisories job (Linux): `cargo audit` (0 vulnerabilities today; 7 unmaintained/unsound notices in Tauri's tree, listed) and `npm audit --audit-level=high`. **Left as they are:** the per-isolate cookie-secret cache (a rotation lags an isolate's life — the rotation is a recovery step, not a race); the verifier's `!==` over a SHA-256 (a timing leak of a hash reveals nothing usable); no rate limit on peek/join/redeem (96-bit codes; the reads are the owner's, and Cloudflare's own limits sit in front); `site.yml`'s `npx wrangler@4.145.0` (version-pinned; a lockfile for one deploy tool is a follow-up) | The audit found two medium and six low findings and no critical one; each fix is the smallest that closes the path traced | **Proved by:** `worker/test/region6.test.js` (deletion records ×3, +3 in the follow-up: the title edit, the Library, an unrecorded id; `crates/shroom-upload` `a_listed_tombstone_reads_as_deleted`, `a_row_already_cached_goes_once_its_tombstone_lands`, `a_sealed_video_stays_deleted_whatever_its_record_says_now`, page key budget, the health rule; the key-confinement allowlists name `.shroom/gone/<own id>` and the caller's rate object), `worker/test/worker.test.js` (nosniff and sandbox; unknown kinds 404), `crates/shroom-update` `reads_the_designated_requirement`, `installer/test/install.test.mjs` (+2: a signature that doesn't hold is never installed; the public pin is the release's), CI's Advisories job; the Worker suite 450/450 (456/456 with the follow-up), workerd contract 14/14 | (M1) A short upload credential re-minted (a removed member's video on its way could no longer finish — N32); a Worker-written meta (every state change through the Worker). (M2) A signed manifest (a new key to keep); pinning the leaf in the app (the dev and preview lanes sign with their own identities). (L4) Listing and reaping keys in the Worker (a list on a hot route) |
| B126 | A release is a workflow run, not a typed tag (Bagrat, 2026-10-14: sessions cut releases themselves, through a workflow; amends B102) | **Actions → Release → Run workflow on `main` with Publish** releases the tree's version: the check job refuses unless the run is on `main`, `v<version>` isn't tagged yet, and CI's four jobs (Tests, Release build, Signing rehearsal, Advisories) are complete and green on that exact commit (the check-runs API, `checks: read`); the build is the release's as before; the publish job creates the release with `--target <sha>`, which makes the tag on the commit it built. A hand-pushed tag still publishes as before; unticked, Run workflow is still the rehearsal | The version, CI and the tag were three things to keep in step by hand, and the workflow didn't look at CI at all; now one click asks all three, and anyone (or any session) with the run button cuts the same release | **Proved by:** the 0.1.7 release, run this way (2026-10-14, run 38528266164: the check passed CI green on `8df01ed`, the publish job made `v0.1.7` there, and the public address serves the manifest and the zip it names) | A separate “cut” workflow pushing a tag (a tag pushed with the workflow's own token starts no other workflow, so it would have to call this one anyway); a release on every push to `main` (a release is a decision, not a side effect) |
| B127 | The pre-merge e2e: a branch installed, set up, recording and played on the test account (Bagrat, 2026-10-14: one full e2e run before merging any PR, its video shown to him) | **`.github/workflows/e2e-full.yml`** on a hosted Mac, through `cf-e2e`: the branch's dev build installed by the real one-line installer over `file://` (`dev/e2e/install.sh`); the run opened (`e2e-run open`: the lock, the pin, the sweep, a two-hour token with the product's two powers); the installed app's own wizard with that token (`--drive onboarding --e2e --token-file`); the heartbeat in the space it wired (`heartbeat.mjs --e2e`: one take, both engines, every frame and the sound, the owner's title, a member invited, joining, recording and removed, delete); the run closed (swept, token deleted). Verdict: the job's colour and an `e2e (test account)` status on the head. Evidence: the screen's recording, the browsers' views of the page (`page/`), the drives' reports, the app's log to the end of setup (the account id scrubbed). **Dev builds only, `--e2e`:** the cloud client is `Policy::Account` (the id from `~/.shroom-dev/e2e-account-id`), and the page, Worker, storage and poster fences admit a page under `shroom-e2e.workers.dev` — nothing else. **A timing bar missed on the runner** (repeated/skipped frames, the A/V offset, the keyed page) passes with a warning naming the figures, as `ci.yml` treats its record tests; any break in the path fails. **The lock:** a run token issued under 45 min ago is a run still going — waited for, never swept | A dev build already refuses every real space outside `shroom-test-…`, and the account is D3's; the release build has no harness, so the dev build is what's driven (Bagrat's pick); the runner is three virtual cores under its own build's load, so it reports timing and a Mac decides | Driving the release build from outside (accessibility clicks, a separate pattern player — Bagrat: dev build); the fake (proves no real wiring, page or playback); failing on timing bars from a hosted runner (red on load alone, never the product); sweeping without a lock (two sessions wipe each other's space) |
| B128 | WebKit's first callback, before its frame can be drawn, is no frame seen (amends B59 a) | In the heartbeat's measured plays (`tests/e2e/heartbeat.mjs`, step 4), the first `requestVideoFrameCallback` that draws nothing onto the canvas, before any frame was counted, is left out of `presented` and reported as `undrawnFirst`. Every other unreadable sample still fails the play, drawn or not, and each is described in the play's `unread` (drawn, luma, `readyState`, the callback's frame number and media time). | Three e2e-full runs failed on WebKit's frame 0 alone (38571262927, 38576110423, 38580960235), with the published media whole and Chromium reading every frame. A probe on a hosted Mac (PR #29, run 38583331393) played a counter-strip take through the real page in headless WebKit (native HLS), 16 times per layout. Frame 0 missed in 14 of 48 plays, at every column width (704 px: 5, 960 px in a 720-tall window: 1, 960 px fully in view: 8). Every miss was the same: one callback, nothing drawn, `readyState` 2, `presentedFrames` 1, media time 0. The next callback read 300, the take's first frame, as the clean plays begin. So no frame goes unread: WebKit calls back before the frame can be copied, more often when the whole player is in view. | Counting it (fails a run on a race inside WebKit, with nothing wrong in the page or the media); leaving out every sample where nothing was drawn (a later one could hide a frame a viewer missed); drawing again a moment later (the next callback already brings the same frame); a smaller window that pushes the player's foot out of view (the page's own short-window layout is what the heartbeat should play) |
| B129 | A full e2e request dropped from the queue says so (amends B127) | `e2e-full.yml` and `e2e.yml` keep the shared `cf-e2e` concurrency group, so one run is on the test account at a time. **`.github/workflows/e2e-dropped.yml`** runs on every `e2e-full.yml` run that ends `cancelled` (`workflow_run`, from main's copy). It sets that commit's `e2e (test account)` status to `error` with “Cancelled by another request — ask again”, or “Cancelled while running — ask again” when the run's own `pending` is the latest. A run that set its own final verdict is left alone. It reads only the run's id and commit, with no checkout. | GitHub keeps one *pending* run per concurrency group. A request that queues behind a running one cancels the one already waiting, before it starts a job, so it never sets a status. On 2026-10-14, run 38589644489 (`claude/heartbeat-in-vm-proposal`) was cancelled when `claude/obs-alert` queued behind `claude/zealous-keller-2tw5je`, and the branch's status never came. | **No group, or one per branch (`cf-e2e-${{ github.ref }}`), with the account lock doing the queueing.** The lock isn't built for that yet. (a) *The run closes* runs `if: always()` whenever the account token was written, so a run whose `open` gave up waiting would still sweep the account and delete every run token, the live run's included. (b) `open` reads the tokens, then mints, so two jobs reaching it within one 30 s poll both pass. (c) `E2E_WAIT_SECS` (20 min) covers about one run ahead, not a queue of several. (d) A run that held the account past `RUN_FRESH_SECS` (45 min) would be swept as a crashed one. Each Mac would also sit billed while it waits. That route needs the lock hardened first: close only after a successful open, delete only its own token, mint-then-check ordering, and a longer wait. **Re-queueing the dropped request automatically:** it could cancel the next waiting one, and so on in a loop |
| B130 | Runs queue on the test account: a ticket per run, its turn in issue order (amends B127's lock and B129's group) | Every run, first thing, mints its run token, its **ticket** (`tests/e2e-live`'s `open`). It holds the account only while no live ticket is older: issued first, or in the same second with a smaller id. Until then it waits (`E2E_WAIT_SECS`: 90 min on a hosted runner, 20 on a Mac), and gives up its place if the wait runs out. Its turn starts with the sweep. Its `close` sweeps only when it holds the account, then deletes **its own** ticket. A run stopped while waiting only leaves the queue (`e2e-run open` writes the ticket's id to `<token-file>.id` before any wait; the dev space keeps it in `~/.shroom-dev/e2e-ticket`). A ticket is live for `RUN_FRESH_SECS` = 150 min, the longest job that can hold one (`e2e-full.yml`'s `timeout-minutes: 150`). A test holds both workflows' timeouts and waits under it. Run tokens live 3 h. `e2e-run clear` (sweep + every ticket) is a person's way out when a lost runner wedges the queue. Each workflow's concurrency group is now the branch's own (`cf-e2e-full-<ref>`, `cf-e2e-suite-<ref>`), so requests from different branches all wait their turn. A newer request on the same branch still takes the place of a waiting one, and B129's status says so. | B129's watcher made a dropped request visible, but several sessions asking at once still lost requests (Bagrat, 2026-10-14, asked for real queueing). The old lock read the tokens and then minted, so two runs in one 30 s poll both got in. Its close swept and deleted every run token even for a run that never got its turn. A 45 min freshness would have swept a run still waiting. | **A heartbeat that refreshes the ticket** (Cloudflare tokens can't be re-dated without rewriting their policies; the job timeout bounds a ticket's life anyway). **A lock object in R2** (buckets are swept, and it needs a second store). **More test accounts for real parallel runs** (needs new accounts, claimed names and secrets — Bagrat's go; the product keeps one space per account, so one account can't hold two runs). **One shared group** (GitHub drops all but one waiting run: B129) |
| B131 | E2E runs side by side on the one test account, each in its own space (Bagrat's pick, 2026-10-14, after two requests were cancelled by the `cf-e2e` queue; **amends B127's lock and supersedes B130's queue**, and TESTING-PLAN D3's sweep) | **A space id per run, dev builds with `--e2e` only.** A run id is `<unix>-<hex4>`; `e2e-run open <token-file> <space-id-file>` makes it and writes it, before anything of the run exists, where the app reads it (`~/.shroom-dev/e2e-space-id`, beside the account id). It sets the label and the bucket as a pair — `v-<run>` over `shroom-space-<run>` (`wire::SpaceNames::of_run`), with no fallback label — so two runs never share a page, a video or a delete. The setup path is the product's: `Runtime::space_names()` feeds the survey (`sweep::survey`, `survey_own_among`, which reads a fallback against the space's own first label so a run never says a foreign `v`), wiring, the enroll and `space.json`; only the strings differ. The defaults stay `v` (then `shroom`, `shroom-v`) over `shroom-space` in every other build, and the release build can't take another (no harness; `space_names` needs `--e2e`). `--e2e` without a well-formed space id refuses to start. The e2e fence already admits one label under `shroom-e2e.workers.dev`; `v-<run>` passes it. **Scoped open and close** (`tests/e2e-live`): open pins, reaps, and mints a token named `shroom-e2e-run-<run>`; close deletes every Worker and bucket (emptied first) whose name ends in the run's id, and that token. Leftovers of a crashed run — any name ending in a run id at least 45 min old, and run tokens that old (by the id, else `issued_on`, else the old `<unix>` name) — are reaped at every open and close; a name without a run id is never a run's to take. The account-wide sweep, B130's tickets and turn order, `E2E_WAIT_SECS` and `e2e-run clear` are gone, and so are the concurrency groups of `e2e-full.yml` and `e2e.yml` (GitHub keeps one waiting run per group and cancels the next); nothing waits, so the job timeouts go back to 90 and 30 min. B129's `e2e-dropped.yml` stays: a run cancelled by hand still says so on its commit. The heartbeat's dev space (`shroom-dev dev-space`) is a scoped run too, under `shroom-test-dev-<run>` (the plain dev app's fence). **The trade-off:** no E2E deploys the literal `v` any more — the same code, a different string | Runs queued behind one another (B130's turn order, or before it the `cf-e2e` group, where a newer request silently cancelled the one waiting — twice that day); every run swept the whole account, so nothing else could go beside it. B130 rejected parallel runs because the product keeps one space per account — but only the product must: the run's space id is a test-build string, and the setup path is unchanged | **Proved by:** `crates/shroom-cloud/tests/run_space.rs` (the pair, the run id, a run set up beside another run's and the product's without seeing either); `tests/e2e-live/tests/harness_fake.rs` (a close takes its own space alone; a crashed run's space and token reaped by age, a live one's never); the app's `an_e2e_run_sets_up_its_own_space_and_every_other_build_the_products`; two E2E full runs requested seven seconds apart on `claude/e2e-run-spaces` (38601783525, 38601800527), their spaces live together for 4.5 min, both green — the pair done 12.5 min after the first request, where the queue would have taken about 20 | A pool of test accounts, one per lane (Bagrat sets each up — a card, a name, a token — and parallel runs cap at the pool's size); lock-only pipelining (the camera step moved before open and the queue dropped: about 1.8× the throughput, but runs on the account stay serial); a shared bucket with per-run labels (the runs' videos and deletes would mix) |
| B132 | The BYO-agent skill retired; shroom.am/agent is the agent's door (Bagrat, 2026-10-14, ruled to retire it; ARCHITECTURE §16 #40; **supersedes B81**, DEVIATIONS #50) | **`agent-skill/` is deleted** (its SKILL.md, `scripts/status.sh` and both tests; CI's step and the cloud skill's list drop them). A coding agent installs shroom from **`site/agent.md`** (served at shroom.am/agent): it asks, runs the one command on a yes, relays the installer word for word and stops at its last line — shroom's window does the rest. What the skill did that the page still covers: the ask-first rule, the token never in chat, an invite's own ask (added with this change), and the mid-take case — the installer refuses an open take itself (`install.sh`'s `MIDTAKE`) and the page says to relay a stop, not work around it. The skill's parting line isn't carried: at the installer's last line setup hasn't started, so “You're all set” would be false. **The status seam stays** (B29, B80): public, read by the installer and the drives. **Later (Bagrat's idea, not designed):** a doctor — a page served like `/install` that an agent reads to fix a shroom whose state is broken, reading the seam. | `site/test/agent.test.mjs` (the page's rules, its asks); `installer/test/install.test.mjs` (the mid-take refusal) | Keeping the skill and publishing it — from a marketplace listing inside `bagrat/shroom` (a push of `main` for every change) or served from shroom.am: it narrated what shroom's window already says, its tests bound it to every setup state the app has, and each status read was a permission prompt; with the marketplace gone nobody could reach it. Folding its per-state narration into /agent: the same coupling on a public page |

| B135 | Uninstall: the install line takes `--uninstall`, and Settings → General gets Uninstall shroom… (Bagrat, 2026-10-14, in the orchestrator chat: he asked for an uninstall in Settings that cleans up everything, the permissions included; on the boards — the canvas's UninstallSheet, UninstallCases, UninstallTerminal and the changed SettingsGeneral — he approved (a), (b) and (c); **reverses** Region 5's rejected “uninstall button”) | **This Mac only; the space is never touched** — no bucket, Worker, video, link or member entry (this Mac's device stays listed, as Disconnect leaves it: his (a)). Removed: `~/Applications/shroom.app`, the build's own folder (`~/.shroom`, `~/.shroom-dev`, `~/.shroom-preview`: token, device key, prefs, takes, logs), and what macOS keeps for its bundle id (`~/Library/WebKit/<id>`, `Caches/<id>`, `HTTPStorages/<id>`, `Saved Application State/<id>.savedState`, `defaults delete <id>` and its plist); copies saved to Movies stay. **The permissions**: `tccutil reset All <id>`, as the user, no admin — **while the app is still there and registered with LaunchServices** (`lsregister -f` first, `-u` after): tccutil resolves the id only through LaunchServices and otherwise refuses with -10814. shroom asks for no Accessibility, so there is none to reset. **The Terminal line** (`uninstall-sh`): `curl -fsSL https://shroom.am/install \| sh -s -- --uninstall` — the same one file, never a second address; a mid-take refusal with the installer's own words; recordings that never reached the space (a take not `uploaded` and not `tombstoned`) stop it, changing nothing (his (b)); a running shroom is quit as an update quits it. **The Settings control** (`uninstall-confirm`, Disconnect's guards: greyed mid-take, blocked while uploads drain or a saved take waits, Save them to Movies first for stranded takes): the app switches its login item off (`SMAppService` unregister), resets the permissions, quits, and leaves a helper that waits for its exit and removes the files — the updater's swap pattern (B31). | B136 | The app's updater asks for the manifest as `update.txt` (Bagrat, 2026-10-14: he approved the analytics proposal's split) | **Each release carries `update.txt`**, the same bytes as `manifest.txt` (`tools/package-release.sh` copies it; `release.yml` uploads all three and checks the copy is the manifest, before and after publishing). **The updater asks for `releases/latest/download/update.txt`**, and on a 404 for `manifest.txt` (`shroom_update::check`): a release from before 0.1.11, and the test harnesses' release folders, carry only the manifest. The installer is unchanged. GitHub counts downloads per file, so `manifest.txt` ≈ install-line runs, `update.txt` ≈ the app's checks (about six a day per running copy), and zip downloads − `manifest.txt` ≈ updates — with nothing new sent from anyone's Mac. | B162 | The setup window after the first stranger run (Bagrat, 2026-10-15, in a session: he approved items 1–4 and the four boards to build, WizardEmailFirst, WizardCardWatching, WizardTokenChecking and WizardOrder; N45) | **(a) The rows keep their numbers; only the drawn order swaps** (`derive::onboarding::DRAWN` = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 5; `PlanView.current` is the place drawn). **(b) Wiring runs under the chapter while the onboarding window shows** (`settle::due_wire`: `done` through `perm-priming`, the name row ticked, no space yet, never while the app is on its way out) — closing the window pauses it as before, and its row's dot turns (`Glyph::Working`). **(c) The chapter's chained prompts run once the name row ticks**, wired or not (`apply::os`). **(d) A relaunch mid-chapter with wiring unfinished re-probes from the token on** (no space on disk yet): the card row's spinner for the probe's second, then the chapter where it was, wiring picking up. **(e) A space found is joined first, as before** (`space-found` → `done`): the join is quick and its line is worth seeing. **(f) Settings · Space keeps its order**: the repair view of a space already set up, its microphone door under the last row. **(g) The quiet lines' state** lives in the onboarding window's fields (`signed_up`, `billing_opened`), clearing with the window; Session is at its 12-field cap | Bagrat's swap after the run: a new name's first certificate took ~3.5 min and wiring ~6, a wait that now overlaps the permissions instead of preceding them; the rest keeps every derived state and the window's lifetime as they were. | B164 | Copy thumbnail (spec N46; Bagrat, 2026-10-15, in the orchestrator chat, relayed by it: he liked B most and asked how to keep it current, added the loop, picked A for both and the name Copy thumbnail, asking that it act as a link too, then approved both and asked to see the player page as an image; proposal [`dev/proposals/thumbnail-share.md`](../dev/proposals/thumbnail-share.md), boards “Copy as a thumbnail — designs”, “B kept current”, “B + loop, kept current”) | **The owner page gets [ Copy thumbnail ] beside Copy link, shown once the video plays; the Library row's menu gets it under Copy link.** The clipboard carries HTML (a linked picture of the video) and the plain link as text — never an image file. **The picture is B + loop, kept current:** a 3 s silent loop of the teaser's start, 600 wide at 8 fps, the title on a soft band at its foot and the length in a corner (the first frame doubles as the still Outlook shows); before the teaser exists, the same as a 1200-wide JPEG still. Untitled (`titled` false): no band. **Made where the title is set:** after a title save the page draws the still and then the loop in the browser (canvas + a vendored GIF encoder, unchanged pixels left out of each frame) and stores each with `POST /_shroom/thumb/<id>`; a video with no picture for its title gets one on its first copy. **The new cookie power** (WORKER-INTERFACE §3.5, §4.1, §4.17): whoever may rename a video may store its picture — a GIF ≤ 600 wide and ≤ 800 KiB or a JPEG ≤ 1200 wide and ≤ 300 KiB, checked by its first bytes, ≤ 40 per video, named by its SHA-256, accepted only for the title the record has. The record's `thumb` points at the newest for the current title; earlier files stay, so a sent paste keeps its picture; a delete removes them with the video, a seal stops `/m/` serving them. `/m/` serves `gif`; status says `teaser`. **The viewer's page too** (Bagrat, the same day, settling Region 4's open question: the buttons on the viewer's page as well, with Download there too): `live` shows Copy link and Copy thumbnail beside Download; a viewer stores nothing, so theirs wraps the newest picture for the title, else the first frame (`poster.jpg` — a picture drawn in a viewer's browser couldn't be pasted without being stored, and inline `data:` pictures are stripped by mail and editors). Both copy buttons carry their words as a tooltip. **The Library** (this PR): a live row's menu has Copy thumbnail under Copy link; the app asks the page's status for the newest picture (`thumb`) and puts the same HTML and the plain link on the pasteboard (`public.html` + text, never an image file), the first frame when there is none. **Dropped** (Bagrat, 2026-10-15: no backward compatibility needed): the Library making the picture itself on a first copy; an older video copied only from the Library keeps the first frame (linked) until its page is opened once | Basecamp and most apps embed only a fixed list of providers; a pasted linked picture is what Loom's email embed does (probe: Trix keeps a remote image's link, measured; a PNG on the clipboard makes Trix upload a detached file, measured). Drawing at title time keeps the picture current without the Worker drawing anything, and content-addressed files never go stale in Gmail's proxy or a re-hosting app. Measured: a canvas still ~35 ms and ~115 KB; the 3 s loop 1.1–2.6 s in headless Chromium (software decode) and 80–125 KB for a typical screen, up to ~770 KB when the whole screen moves | The Worker drawing it on request (satori + resvg-wasm: ~150–750 ms CPU a picture against the free plan's 10 ms, +~1 MB to a 38 KB Worker); drawing at publish and redrawing on rename at one address (the app hears of a page rename only when its Library opens, so title-then-copy races it; an overwritten picture goes stale in caches); a title baked in at go-live (it is usually still the placeholder); the plain poster (reads as a screenshot); the app making the first loop in Rust (the bundled ffmpeg has no GIF encoder — the owner page makes it once the teaser lands, or the first copy does) |
| B165 | Copy thumbnail after Bagrat tried it on 0.1.15 (2026-10-15, in the orchestrator chat, relayed by it: copying worked, but right after upload it gave a JPEG and only later a GIF; the GIF's quality was low and neither showed the length; after a rename the new copy had the new title and, somehow, the length too) | **(a) The first pictures carry the length.** The owner page opens at stop, while the video uploads, when the record has no `durationSec` yet; the still and the loop it drew at the go-live had no length, and only a later page load (a rename after reopening) had it. The status now says `dur`, and the page sets the length before it draws (the gallery face `copy-thumbnail-late` reads the stored still's corner). **(b) The loop looks like the screen.** 640 wide (the teaser's own width) at 10 fps for 3 s; its palette is cut from the frames' colours (median cut on 5 bits a channel, each colour weighted by the square root of its count, so a small coloured text keeps its colour) with a 4×4 ordered dither (the band's shade no longer steps; steady across frames, so unchanged pixels are still left out). The Worker's GIF width cap is 640. Measured on a UI-like clip (the Library's rows, a popover sliding in, a cursor, typed text): 103 KB typical, 523 KB with the whole screen scrolling, ~0.3 s to encode; the 0.1.15 encoder banded and tinted the whole picture | Bagrat's report; images in [`dev/proposals/thumbnail-share/`](../dev/proposals/thumbnail-share/) (`gif-before-after*.png`) | For (b): Floyd–Steinberg dithering (noisy, and 275–355 KB on the typical clip: its error spreads change unchanged pixels, so they can no longer be left out); 720 wide (an upscale of a 640-wide teaser); the 0.1.15 palette (the commonest colours on 4 bits a channel); an unweighted median cut (it loses a small coloured text to the band's many shades) |
| B166 | Copy thumbnail waits until its picture is ready (spec N46, amended; Bagrat, 2026-10-15, in the orchestrator chat, relayed by it — on 0.1.15: keep the button disabled until the picture is ready; on the board “Copy thumbnail waits until the moving picture is ready”: disabled until ready, with padding between the tooltips and the button, the wording OK) | **The owner page's Copy thumbnail is greyed until the loop for the current title is stored** (`aria-disabled`, the icon dimmed, a click copies nothing), its tooltip “Preparing the thumbnail…”; then it wakes and its tooltip is its name. A new title greys it again until that title's loop is stored. **When no loop can be made** it wakes with the still: the upload finished with no teaser (the page renders it awake when the record already says so), drawing or storing the loop failed, or 20 s passed after the still with the teaser present (a guard against a video that never loads). **The viewer's page shows it only once a picture made for the current title exists** (it no longer falls back to the first frame). The Library row's menu keeps it enabled. **Tooltips are drawn by the page** (`data-tip`, a CSS `::after`) 8px below their button, right edges aligned, after a 0.3 s hover or on keyboard focus — Copy link, Copy thumbnail, the preparing line and Download (on its wrapper); the browser's own `title` tooltip can't be moved off the button. 0.1.16 (unreleased) carries it: its page-version row is re-recorded | Bagrat's report (a copy right after upload gave a still, a later one the loop) and his ruling; board [`dev/proposals/thumbnail-share/thumb-wait-board.png`](../dev/proposals/thumbnail-share/thumb-wait-board.png); the wait is the upload plus ~1 s | Enabled at once, copying the still until the loop exists (a paste made early stays a still forever); a timer instead of the stored loop (wakes too early on a slow upload, too late on a fast one); the button hidden instead of greyed on the owner page (it would jump in under the pointer); the native `title` tooltip (no gap can be set) |

The popover's panel now has `hasShadow` (cast from what the page draws, so it follows the curve), re-cast on every show and every height fit (`invalidateShadow`); `.pop` keeps its hairline edge and loses its shadow. The count covers the display and casts none; the Library, Settings and setup windows are macOS's titled windows and were already right (seen on the hosted Mac) | Before/after on the hosted Mac: [`dev/proposals/popover-corners/`](../dev/proposals/popover-corners/) | A panel larger than the card with a margin for the CSS shadow (the margin is panel too: it would take clicks meant for what's beneath, and move the popover's anchor off the mushroom); masking the panel's content view to the radius (the CSS shadow would still be cut, now by a curve, and lost) |

| B172 | The Mac's sound in a display take (N47; Bagrat, 2026-10-16: build it now, on by default, with no setting and no UI, to keep the work minimal) | **ScreenCaptureKit's own audio on the screen's stream** (`capturesAudio`, `excludesCurrentProcessAudio` so shroom's sounds stay out, mono at 48 kHz). The screen source hands it to a `SystemAudio` handle (`shroom-record`'s `mix`), and the take's audio source is `Mixed`. **(a) Placed by when it was heard.** ScreenCaptureKit hands its sound over 58–71 ms after it was heard, as measured on the hosted Mac from each buffer's presentation time. A mix made as the voice arrives left the Mac's sound 100–165 ms behind the picture. So the mix runs `LATENCY` (120 ms) behind. The voice waits in a delay line of exactly that length, and each block that leaves it gets the Mac's sound heard at the same instants added, through a soft limiter. The source declares the latency (`AudioSource::latency`), and the take's writer places the track that much earlier (B58's alignment, with the latency taken off). At the take's end the writer flushes what the mix holds, so the track stays as long as the take. A voice alone comes out sample for sample as it went in. The Mac's sound is resampled to the mic's rate (linear), and what arrives too late for its slot becomes silence, counted. **(b) With no microphone**, the Mac's sound alone is let out as it falls `LATENCY` behind, at 48 kHz, so a take that used to be video-only now carries a track. **(c) A lost mic** stops the mix with it until Resume. The resumed take without a mic records the Mac's sound alone, resampled to the recording's rate by the encoder as today (B47). **(d) Camera as the source** records the mic only, a take with the mic off keeps the Mac's sound, and mic-lost's line becomes “Resume continues without your microphone.” (Bagrat, 2026-10-16, answering the three questions put to him). **(e) The drives** mix the beep with the runner's sound, so the heartbeat and e2e cross the same path. The hosted-Mac scenario `system-audio` proves the three things. Two other apps play during take 0: the dev app's `--play-out` burst, half a second after each beep, and Apple's `afplay` tone. The check's script starts both, never shroom, because `excludesCurrentProcessAudio` also leaves out shroom's own children (probed). Take 0 must carry the voice and both apps, each against the picture. shroom's own tone must be in neither take. Take 1 must be the beep alone. | ScreenCaptureKit gives the Mac's sound with no driver and no new permission on macOS 13+ (RESEARCH §5, the floor is 14). Keeping the mic as the clock leaves the voice's path, and every check on it, exactly as it was. Mono matches the track as it is | A virtual output driver (BlackHole-style: an install, a permission, the POC's walls); mixing in ffmpeg as a second audio input (a second fifo, a second alignment, the POC's two-input deadlocks); a stereo track; ducking, levels or a volume per source; mixing as the voice arrives through a jitter buffer (built first: the Mac's sound trailed the picture by ~130 ms on the hosted Mac) |
| B173 | The microphone opens with the count; a Bluetooth one is held through a pause (Bagrat, 2026-10-15, in the orchestrator chat, after the AirPods report: the mic-in-use dot showing from the count is fine, and option c for Resume) | **(a)** When the 3-2-1 starts (Start, or Restart's count), the core emits `RecCmd::Warm` with the microphone the count will fire capture with. The recorder opens it then (`shroom-record::warm::WarmMic`), listening into nothing. At zero the take binds to the stream that's already running. A cancelled count emits `RecCmd::Cool`, which closes it. What the mic heard before the take began never reaches it (the take's sink is bound at its start, and `trim_to_take` still cuts to the take, B58). **(b)** A **Bluetooth** microphone (CoreAudio transport `blue`/`blea`) is held open through a pause, so Resume is heard at once. Any other microphone closes at the pause as before (D:B58 c). A held mic that reports itself lost is reopened, or replaced by the fallback the core names (N39). | The report, 2026-10-15, with AirPods on 0.1.15: the first 2.08 s of the take were exact digital zeros, then sound, in step from there. The track was 24 kHz mono (AirPods' call profile), the full length of the video, with no other gap. A headset switches from its listening profile to its call profile when its mic opens and delivers nothing for about two seconds. The mic opened at the count's zero, so that wake was every take's head, and every resumed take's too. With B172 the Mac's sound rides the voice's blocks, so it was cut from the same seconds. The rate was never the problem: each take keeps the rate the device reports. A wired or built-in mic delivers within about 100 ms, so it isn't held through a pause | Filling the gap with silence (that's what was shipped; the words are still lost); a wait state before the count (a new surface for a two-second wake the count already covers); holding every microphone through a pause (the mic-in-use dot stays on while paused, for a wake only headsets need); leaving Resume cold (a silent two-second head on every resumed take with AirPods) |
| B174 | The Mac's sound is read on, never cut, to keep it in step with the voice (in-frame bug fix, **refines B172 (a)**; Bagrat on 0.1.16, 2026-10-16: the Mac's sound glitched and cut out) | **The voice keeps its own time.** Its time is its sample count from its first block, eased (over ~0.5 s) towards when its blocks arrive. A jump of more than 50 ms (a stall) is taken at once. **The Mac's sound is read on** from where the last block stopped. While it is more than 1 ms off the voice's time, it is read up to 0.2 % faster or slower (3.5 cents, linear interpolation) until they meet. The Mac's sound is placed by its timestamps, which hold however its buffers are delivered: on the hosted Mac, traced, they matched its sample count to the millisecond while its buffers came in bursts (the first four at once, 120 ms late). A gap of up to 20 ms in them is the sound card's clock against the host's: the buffer follows them, and the read catches up by stretching. A voice block far off its eased time is a burst and passes; only blocks far off for 250 ms are a stall, taken as its new time. The drives' beep, the runner's microphone, now paces its samples by the steady clock, as a device's crystal does, sounding the wall clock's tick. **Placed by timestamps again** only when it ran out, was never placed, or is more than 50 ms off, as B172 placed every block. Seam tests in `shroom-record`'s `mix`: 20 ms blocks up to 15 ms late, clocks ±100 ppm apart for 90 s, and bursts (the Mac's first four buffers at once, then clumps; the voice stalling 80 ms each second). Each runs with no splice and stays in step within 12 ms. | A real take on 0.1.16 (AirPods, 24 kHz, 20 ms blocks) had 197 splices in the 13 s the Mac played: 185 sat in the same 1 ms of the 20 ms block, the mic's block edge. B172 placed each block by when it arrived. A callback's few ms of lateness, different every block, skipped the Mac's sound or put silence in at every edge, 16 times a second. The voice was clean, since it passes through untouched | Placing each block by when it arrived (B172 as built: the splices); a cut whenever they drift apart past a bound (a click every few minutes from clock drift alone); resampling the voice instead (the voice's path and every check on it stay sample for sample, B172) |
| B175 | Install via Homebrew: shroom's own tap, rendered per release (N49; Bagrat, 2026-10-16: a required item) | **`brew install --cask bagrat/shroom/shroom`, from the tap `bagrat/homebrew-shroom`.** Its `Casks/shroom.rb` is generated: `installer/homebrew/render-cask.sh` fills `installer/homebrew/shroom.rb` with the version and SHA-256 the latest release's `manifest.txt` names, under the same shape checks as the install line. The tap's own workflow (source: `installer/homebrew/tap/`) renders it from `main` every hour and on demand, and commits only a change, with no secret. RELEASING's last step runs it by hand. The cask: **(a)** the release's zip at the address the install line uses, Apple silicon and macOS 14 only, `auto_updates true` so `brew upgrade` leaves updates to the app (B31). **(b)** Placed at `~/Applications/shroom.app`, where the install line and the updater keep it: one copy, no admin password, always writable for the swap. A copy the install line placed is adopted with `--adopt`. **(c)** Before anything is placed, a `preflight_steps` `run` step holds the staged copy (`{{staged_path}}/shroom.app`) to the installer's pin (`am.shroom`, the release certificate's leaf, B125). A copy that fails is never placed and the install fails. Only then is Homebrew's quarantine mark lifted from it: the release is self-signed, not notarized, so a marked copy is refused at first open, and the install line's curl never marks it. Homebrew 7 deprecates Ruby `postflight` blocks for these structured steps, which run in a sandbox whose `$HOME` is a temporary folder. A step after the move can't see `~/Applications`, and the staged copy is in the Caskroom, which the sandbox may write. **(d)** `uninstall` quits it; `--zap` removes what `install.sh --uninstall` removes beside the app (B135). The grants stay, as after dragging an app to the Bin: `tccutil` finds an app only through LaunchServices, and the app is gone by then. `installer/test/cask.test.mjs` holds the pin, the floor, the place and the zap list to the installer's. The hosted-Mac scenario `brew-cask` installs the real release through a local tap and checks each of these. | Homebrew's official cask list refuses an app that fails Gatekeeper (notarization), so it can't carry a self-signed release, and a bare `brew install --cask shroom` can't work without it. Since Homebrew 5, `--no-quarantine` is gone, so the cask has to lift the mark itself. On the hosted Mac (Homebrew 7.0.9, 2026-10-16), against release 0.1.16 through a local tap: installed into `~/Applications` with no quarantine mark and the release's signer, and it opened; a cask whose pin was wrong placed nothing; a copy the install line placed was adopted; `--zap` left nothing; `brew style` clean; livecheck reads the manifest. A `run` step after the move (first tried) failed on the genuine release, because it ran against the sandbox's home. | The official homebrew/cask (needs notarization, a paid Apple account: B125's posture); `/Applications` as Homebrew's default (a second copy beside an install-line one, two `shroom://` handlers, and an admin-only folder the updater may not write); `version :latest` with `sha256 :no_check` (no fingerprint at all); the release workflow pushing the bump with a deploy key (a new credential, kept as an option); asking people to pass `--no-quarantine` (removed from Homebrew); a Ruby `postflight` block (worked, but deprecated); a `postflight_steps` check of the placed copy (the sandbox's home isn't the person's); registering the app with Launch Services at install (`lsregister -f`), so `open -a shroom` and `shroom://` work the moment `brew install` returns — on the hosted Mac (Homebrew 7.0.9, 2026-10-16, run [38879024362](https://github.com/bagrat/shroom/actions/runs/38879024362), `dev/mac/scenarios/brew-lsregister-probe.sh`) `lsregister` exits 1 inside every `postflight_steps`/`preflight_steps` run step (after the move, with `{{appdir}}`, which reads `/Applications`, and on the staged copy), and the step can't read the person's home from the directory service; only the deprecated Ruby `postflight` worked, and it prints a deprecation warning to every person who installs, so the lines open shroom by its path (`open ~/Applications/shroom.app`), which works at once (`brew-join-probe`, run [38876435372](https://github.com/bagrat/shroom/actions/runs/38876435372)), while `open -a shroom` by name failed in 3 of 4 fresh installs (Bagrat, 2026-10-16: fall back to the path form if Homebrew 7 blocks the registration) |
| B176 | A join link handed over by `open -a` is an arrival (N50, ratified by Bagrat 2026-10-16; the Homebrew join line) | **`open -a shroom "https://<space>/join/<code>"` opens the invite.** macOS hands the link to the app as an open-URL event at cold launch (before launch finishes) and while it runs; the app's `RunEvent::Opened` used to keep only `shroom:` URLs, so a web address was dropped and the welcome opened. Now `shroom_cloud::member::takes_opened` keeps a `shroom:` URL as before and an `https://` one only when `parse_link` reads it as a join link (host with a dot, `/join/` and a 16-character code); every other URL is ignored unread. A kept link goes through the same `member::deep_link` as Open shroom: the owner-only `~/.shroom/invite`, the stash, then `MemberNews::Arrived` — the invite window, never a silent join, and held while a take is open (Law 4). The bundle claims no web addresses (`CFBundleURLTypes` lists only `shroom`), so the browser keeps every https link and no website can route one here; the link is in the shell's history and `open`'s own arguments, never in the app's. | On the hosted Mac (macOS 26.6.2, 0.1.16 from the tap; runs [38875304073](https://github.com/bagrat/shroom/actions/runs/38875304073), [38876435372](https://github.com/bagrat/shroom/actions/runs/38876435372), `dev/mac/scenarios/brew-join-probe.sh`): a throwaway app with no URL types got the https link in `application:openURLs:` before `didFinishLaunching`, and again while running, with Safari still the https handler; 0.1.16 dropped it and showed the welcome; `shroom://join/…` the same way reached the invite window (`join-waiting`, then `join-space-missing` for the invented space). `ps` showed only the binary on the app's command line. **Proved by:** `crates/shroom-cloud` `an_opened_url_is_taken_only_as_a_join_link`; the hosted-Mac scenario `open-join-link` on the dev build ([run 38880095670](https://github.com/bagrat/shroom/actions/runs/38880095670)): the link arrived about 3 s into a cold launch and opened the invite window, arrived again while running, `https://shroom.am/` left no arrival and nothing read, and the app's command line held only `--home`. | Claiming `http`/`https` in the bundle (any website could hand shroom a link; the browser would stop being the person's); keeping the install line's file hand-off as the only road for Homebrew (`brew` has no `--join`, and a cask can't take arguments); taking any https URL and letting `deep_link` sort it (a stray web address would be stashed and logged as a non-join link) |
| B177 | The page plays the single-file copy once it's up (Bagrat, 2026-10-16: look into raising how many views an owner's free Worker can serve; then, the same day, build it for 0.1.17) | **Once `media.mp4` is set and the stream is one picture, the player plays `/m/<id>/video.mp4` as a file; otherwise the stream, as before.** The recipient's page, the owner page and the embed choose the same way (`sourceOf`). One picture means the stream's playlist (`rec/<id>/v0/stream.m3u8`) names one `EXT-X-MAP`. A later take whose picture differs in size has its own init (B58 a), and the copy, joined by `-c copy`, can't carry that change. The playlist is read beside the record, so the choice costs no round (the latency test holds), and a missing or unreadable playlist means the stream. **Before the first play, hls.js buffers one 6 s piece** (`maxBufferLength` and `maxMaxBufferLength` 6), and takes its defaults (30, 600) at the first play. Native HLS keeps `preload="metadata"`. **The choice is made once, when the page loads** (Bagrat, 2026-10-16: no switch during playback, keep it simple). A viewer who opens the page while only the stream is up watches the stream to the end, and the next load plays the copy. No link, path, gate or word changes: `/m/` already served `video.mp4` under the same removal check, and the recording has one quality, so no adaptive switching is lost. | Every request a viewer makes runs the owner's Worker, and the Workers Free plan allows 100,000 a day (Error 1027 past that). `worker/dev/view-cost.mjs` counts what reaches the Worker per view: the real `worker.js` with a 5-minute 1080p recording at 3,500 kbps, in headless Chromium (hls.js) and WebKit. **Before:** a full watch 56 (page, poster, hls.js, 2 playlists, init, 50 pieces), ~1,800 full views a day; opened but never played, 28 (hls.js buffered ~2 minutes before any play). **After:** with the copy up, a full watch at real speed 4 (Chromium) to 8 (headless WebKit), 6–10 with two seeks, 3 if never played: ~12,000–25,000 full views a day from Chromium-family browsers. **Real Safari** on the hosted Mac (the `view-cost` scenario, safaridriver, real speed) reads a file in ~3 MB ranges, so it saves far less: the copy of a 3-minute video cost 29 for a full watch and 26 with two seeks, ~47 for 5 minutes, against 58 for the stream (run 38878595183). In Safari the cost follows the bytes, about one request per 3 MB, whatever the format. At 8× Safari asks for far more (1,389 for the stream, trick play: run 38877481672), so the scenario plays at real speed. A mix of half Safari and half Chromium is ~3,700 full views a day. Before the copy is up, the stream's never-played view costs 8. Each `/m/` request also costs 3 R2 reads (record, seal, bytes), so R2's free 10M a month went from ~2,000 to ~13,000 full views a day, billed past that rather than refused. `npm run drive:page` checks that the copy plays and that no piece of the stream is asked for. | **Workers Cache** (2026): a hit skips the code but is billed as a request (Cloudflare's pricing footnote), so it still counts against the 100,000. **The Cache API and Cache Rules**: the Cache API runs inside the Worker, and Cache Rules don't apply in front of a Worker on its own route. **A public bucket (r2.dev)**: rate-limited and "non-production" by Cloudflare's docs. It would expose the whole bucket, `.shroom/` included, unless a second bucket held the media, and a removed video's bytes would stop answering 404. **R2 on a custom domain**: needs the owner's own domain on Cloudflare, a step the wizard doesn't have. **10 s pieces**: 36 requests (~2,800 a day), at the cost of a slower start and coarser seeks. **Workers Paid** ($5 a month, 10M requests): the owner's choice, nothing to build. **The copy when the takes differ in size**: it would play wrong after the change. |

| B195 | **The editor: trim, cut, undo, and edits the link plays** (spec N63, **ratified by Bagrat as built, 2026-10-18**; Bagrat asked for it 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: build the editor now, its look and its workings, since it needs no AI, building on the voice-cut proposal's edit list and its canvas boards; under his standing ruling for this work, the build's calls are made on judgment and listed here for him to overturn) | **(a) A window of its own, one at a time,** opened by Edit… in a live Library row's menu (after Open page; greyed offline and mid-take, as Rename). It is built to the canvas's “Editor — trim” and two boards this build added, “Editor — a cut and undo” (Undo · Redo, Put back) and “Editor — getting ready and saving”. **(b) The edit list is the voice-cut proposal's** (`crates/shroom-edit`: `EditList`, `Cut`, `keep()`, from PR #59, unchanged in shape), so the spoken-cue layer adds its suggestions to the same list later. This build adds to it the edited→source map, input checks (clamped to the recording, never rejected for touching an end or overlapping another cut; a hand cut is at least 0.1 s) and the editing operations with their Undo history. **(c) Saved inline in the record, `meta.edits`,** by the same read-modify-write under `If-Match` as a rename, over the same door (the token Mac's own pair, other Macs a 15-minute `manage` credential). The Worker reads the record anyway, so the page costs no extra read, and the page gets only the ranges that play: no transcript text a later voice cut carries ever reaches a viewer. **(d) The share page and the embed apply it as they play,** over the single-file copy and over HLS alike: they start at the trim's in, skip each cut, end at the trim's out, and show the edited length and time; `og:video:duration` and the owner page's length are the edited one. The link never changes. **(e) The editor plays and draws from a file on this Mac:** the take's own copy when it was recorded here (takes are kept after upload), else the video fetched from the space first. The waveform is read from that file's sound in Rust. **(f) A permanent copy, made on the Mac:** after a save, the app cuts the edit out of the original with its own encoder and puts it up beside the original (`rec/<id>/edit-<rev>.mp4`), naming it in `meta.edits.copy`; Download and the page use it once it matches the saved edit. The original stays until B197 deletes it, so Revert works from any Mac meanwhile. **(g) A cut's edges drag** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: he asked whether a cut's edges could be moved to adjust it, then chose to build it as the canvas board “Editor — adjusting a cut” draws it). Either edge of a red cut or a dashed suggestion: the pointer turns ↔ over it, the edge follows the drag with the cut's length live in its chip and the picture at the edge, and it stops at the next cut or suggestion, the trim's end, a tenth of a second from its other edge, or where a second would be all that's left. Letting go is one step (`Op::Resize`, `edit-resize`): Undo puts the edge back. A suggestion stays offered, and Cut it too takes it as it now is; the cut keeps its id and words, marked `adjusted` for the voice-cut layer. No new words. | Bagrat's ask. An edit list keeps every change undoable and costs nothing to save, and the link already plays from one record; a re-encode first would make every save wait minutes. The Worker can't run an encoder; the Mac already bundles one. | **A web editor on the page** (the owner's browser has no take, no encoder, and can't sign writes to the space). **`rec/<id>/edits.json` beside the record** (the proposal's first idea: one more read per view and two objects to keep in step). **Re-encoding before the link plays the edit** (minutes of waiting for a trim). **Replacing the original** (Revert would need the Mac that recorded it). **Saving every change as it's made** (half-made edits would reach viewers); **a cut dragged into its neighbour merging with it** (Put back would then take back both, and a merge can't be told from the drag that made it) |
