| B197 | **After an edit: the final video, then “Delete the original?”, and the original gone within two days** (spec N63, amended, **ratified by Bagrat as built, 2026-10-18**; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: keeping the unedited version for 30 days is too much, deleting it soon should be encouraged; once an edit is saved, offer to delete the original, Yes, delete or keep for 2 days; Revert only while it is kept, with the time it has left shown by Revert; the two-day removal must happen even if the Mac is closed then; the words are his to approve) | **(a) Done saves, then the window makes the final video** (B195 (f)'s permanent copy) and shows its progress; it can be closed and the lane finishes. **(b) Once the copy is up it asks “Delete the original?”**: Yes, delete (filled and leading, as he asked, but a click only: Return does nothing there, law 9; DEVIATIONS #55, ratified 2026-10-18) or Keep for 2 days; Close, ✕ or Esc at any point after the save is Keep for 2 days. The copy's landing writes `meta.edits.keep_until` (now + 2 days); the answer only shortens it. **(c) “The edit becomes the video” (the bake), on Yes, delete or once `edits.keep_until` has passed:** one read-modify-write points `meta.media` at the copy's own files (`mp4`: `rec/<id>/edit-<rev>.mp4`, its poster and teaser, `hls: null`), sets `durationSec` to the copy's length, maps `markers` through the edit (a marker inside a cut goes), drops `edits` (`keep_until` with it), and appends `{at, rev, keep}` to `bakedFrom`: when, the baked edit's revision, and the ranges that played on the clock before it (the last 20 bakes kept). Data kept on an older clock (a transcript) maps through every later bake in order, and a later edit's revision starts past every baked `rev`, so its copy never takes a file name the video plays from; then the original's files (`master.m3u8`, `v0/*`, `video.mp4`, `poster.jpg`, `teaser.mp4`, or an earlier bake's `edit-*` files) are deleted. Nothing is renamed, so any of the space's Macs can do it without the file in hand, and a baked video is an ordinary one-file recording that can be edited again. The page, Download and the Library follow `media`'s keys. **(d) Who deletes after two days: the Macs, not the Worker.** The token Mac's cleanup sweep (B91: 10 minutes after the first sound pass, then every 6 hours awake) bakes every record in the space whose `keep_until` has passed and whose copy is current; every Mac also bakes its own such videos when it starts and every 6 hours. A Mac that's closed then does it when it next runs. **(e) Revert while it's kept:** the editor shows “The original is kept one more day” (or “two more days”, and by the hour under a day, never more than is left: B199 (n)) by Revert to original, and reverting saves the identity edit, which drops the copy and `keep_until`. Once baked, the edit is final. | His ruling. A kept original doubles a video's storage; two days covers a second look. Making the bake a pointer move plus deletes keeps it safe to repeat (each step is idempotent, the record first) and lets any Mac finish it. | **A Worker cron** (`scheduled()` listing the space's records daily): exact to the hour even with every Mac closed, but it needs schedules in the deploy, a new Worker power and its own tests, for a two-day deadline that the next launch meets. **Overwriting `video.mp4` with the copy** (a re-upload, or an S3 copy the per-recording credential may not allow; and the HLS stream would still have to go). **30 days** (his no). **Asking in the Library** (no one sees it until they open it) |
| B198 | **The editor build's calls on the page and in the Worker: a viewer gets the edit, and never what it leaves out** (spec N63, **ratified by Bagrat as built, 2026-10-18**; the calls B195 and B197 left to the build, made on judgment under Bagrat's standing ruling for this work (B195) and listed here for him to overturn; with the fixes from the build's review, 2026-10-18) | **(a) Download waits for the copy.** While an edit waits for the Mac's permanent copy, Download is hidden and `/d/<id>` answers 404 (`viewOf().download`); once the copy is current, Download hands it over, named after the title. **(b) Step 3: the original out of reach.** While a copy is current, `/m/<id>/` serves the copy's own files and none of the original's video: its stream, `video.mp4` and teaser answer 404 to GET, HEAD and Range alike (`servedOnly`); Copy thumbnail's pictures as (h). A newer save, which makes the copy stale, or a revert opens them again until the next copy lands. The Macs read originals over S3, never through `/m/`. **(c) A player follows what plays now.** A page opened before the copy landed asks the status when its media fails (`fail()`: an error, or a piece answering 404, without waiting out hls.js's half-minute of retries) and plays the copy from the same edited moment, playing on if it was. A baked record's status names its one file, so a player opened before the bake moves to it the same way. A `shroom:keep` event (agreed with the voice-cut build, #59) hands the player new ranges on the recording's clock, made safe as `editsOf` makes an edit; it does nothing while a copy plays, which is the edit already. **(d) What the Worker reads of an edit.** `editsOf` ports `EditList::checked` and `keep`, clamped to the record's `durationSec` and held with the player's own arithmetic to `crates/shroom-edit/tests/keep-vectors.json`; it reads at most 500 applied cuts (`EDIT_CUTS_MAX`). A cut it doesn't read (past the cap, or one it can't make sense of) never becomes played time, and an edit that leaves nothing playing fails closed: nothing of the original plays, rather than all of it. **(e) The sound dips around each skip.** The page skips a cut by seeking; the player eases the video's level to nothing over the 50 ms before each skip and back over the 50 ms after it (`shroomGain`), the trim's own ends and a page with no edit at full level. iOS ignores a page's volume, so on an iPhone it's a plain skip. The editor's preview dips the same way (`app/src/editor/keep.ts`), and the copy fades its joins (B199 (d)). **(f) Edited time everywhere:** the player's time, seek bar, ←/→ and Home/End; the page's, the embed's and the owner page's length; the status's `dur`, Copy thumbnail's length and the unfurl's `og:video:duration`. **(g) Only the ranges leave the Worker.** Pages, the embed and the status carry the ranges that play (`keep`) and the current copy's names, each checked as a name of its kind beside the recording: never the list, its cuts, `bakedFrom`, or the transcript text a voice cut carries (`leads`, `removed`, `resumes`, `why`). **(h) No picture of what the edit leaves out.** Copy thumbnail's pictures are drawn from the video's first 3 seconds and stamped with what they were drawn from (`meta.thumb.from`). After an edit `meta.thumb` is no longer current, so no earlier picture is handed out again (the status's `thumb`, Copy thumbnail): the owner's page draws a fresh one, with the edited length. Earlier pictures stay served, so a paste already sent keeps its picture (B164), unless the edit removes something in those first 3 seconds: then only the current picture, drawn from what the video now starts with, is served, and the bake deletes the others. While an edit waits for its copy, the unfurl names no picture or teaser of the original (`og:image`, `og:video`, the twitter tags, oEmbed's thumbnail); the copy's own come with it. Tests: `worker/test/edits.test.js`, `workerd/contract.workerd.mjs`; `worker/dev/page-drive.mjs` plays an edited record in Chromium and WebKit, sampled so a cut moment never shows. | The link never changes and plays the edit from the save on (B195), but until the Mac's copy lands the page has only the original. So everything a viewer can take away (a file, an address, a picture, a card) is the edit's or nothing: a cut part that leaves as a download or in a card can't be called back, and platforms keep cards for days. Once the copy is up, the original's addresses are the one way left to what was cut, and a 404 closes it with no new secret. The cap bounds what one record costs a view, far past what a hand draws; failing closed keeps a bad or oversized record from playing what it cuts. A seek from one sound to the next clicks (Bagrat heard it, 2026-10-18; PR #59 measured a hard cut at a median 0.87 against the room's own texture, a 60 ms fade at 0.22), and 50 ms each side is too short to lose a word. Copy thumbnail's pictures come from the first 3 seconds, so only an edit there makes an earlier one show what was cut; elsewhere a paste already sent keeps its picture. Transcript text is the speaker's words, often the very ones cut | Download of the original while the copy is made (it holds the cut parts); cutting on the fly in the Worker or at `/d/` (no encoder there, and a cut without one lands on the recording's 6 s keyframes, so seconds of a cut part would play); signed, expiring media addresses in place of step 3's 404s (a key per view and a new secret, where the record already says what plays); keeping the original reachable after the copy lands (anyone holding the stream's address would still reach what was cut); dropping the cuts past the cap, as the first build did, or playing the whole original when an edit leaves nothing (both play what was cut); reloading the page when a copy or a bake lands (the viewer loses their place); deleting every earlier picture at an edit or a bake (a paste already sent would lose its picture even when nothing it shows was cut, B164); keeping the earlier picture current after an edit (its length would be the original's); the original's poster and teaser in a card while the copy is made (a trimmed-off start or a cut moment in a card); no dip (the click), or a longer one (it eats the first and last sounds of what's kept) |
| B199 | **The editor build's calls on the Mac: its media, its lanes, the final video and the upkeep** (spec N63, **ratified by Bagrat as built, 2026-10-18**; the calls B195 and B197 left to the build, made on judgment under Bagrat's standing ruling for this work (B195) and listed here for him to overturn; with the fixes from the build's review, 2026-10-18) | **(a) The editor's media: `shroom-media://`.** The webview plays `shroom-media://localhost/<rec>/video` and draws from `…/peaks` (`runtime/media.rs`): only the one video and the peaks the lane registered, to the editor's webview alone (`main.rs`), the video by byte ranges (206 with `Content-Range`, at most 2 MiB a piece, off the main thread), since WebKit's player asks for ranges. No path from a request reaches the disk: it names a recording and a kind, and the registration says which file. **(b) The lane's threads** (`runtime/editor.rs`): Save and Cancel on `shroom-editor` itself; each open on a thread of its own (`-open`), so ✕ or Esc stops a fetch mid-way and nothing is said after; the final videos one at a time on `-copy`; Yes, delete's bake on `-bake`; the upkeep on `-upkeep`. None waits on another. **The save guard:** the open remembers what the record played (`media.mp4`, and how many bakes `bakedFrom` holds); a save over a record that has moved on since (another Mac's bake) is refused before anything is written, its list made on a clock the record no longer plays. **(c) The encode.** Each kept range is re-encoded with VideoToolbox at the source's own picture bitrate (held to 500–20,000 kbps), the sound AAC 128k; where VideoToolbox won't encode (a virtual Mac's can refuse), x264 is tried if the encoder at hand has it, which the release's doesn't. The copy's poster and teaser are made from the copy itself. **(d) The cut and the join.** One part per kept range (`-ss <start> -i <src> -t <len>`, frame-exact), joined by the concat demuxer with stream copy and `-copyts`, so a part's AAC priming neither puts the picture ~23 ms late behind an empty edit (WebKit then shows no first frame) nor lengthens the sound at each join. At each join the sound fades 30 ms out and 30 ms in (`afade`, which the bundled ffmpeg gains, from 0.1.25 on); a part too short for both fades gets neither, and an encoder without `afade` cuts hard. The window's bar: the cut fills it to 80%, the three files sent take it to 95, 97 and 99, naming them in the record to 100; at most every 250 ms, forward only. **(e) The window** opens at the board's 1100×720, no bigger than the screen it opens on and never below 900×640 (a 1024-wide screen, the hosted Mac's, opens it at 1024×720). **(f) What the Mac keeps.** A video fetched from the space stays in `~/.shroom/edits/<rec>/` (`source.mp4`, `source.key` naming the file it came from, `peaks.u8`; folder 700, files 600) for the next edit. An open takes it only while the record still plays that file, and a take's own `preview.mp4` only while the record plays the original, each only when its length is the record's (within a second). The upkeep drops a folder whose fetched video the record no longer plays (a bake on another Mac), whose recording the space no longer lists, or that's gone a week unused (never the open one); Disconnect and Leave drop them all. **(g) The upkeep reads the space.** At launch and every 6 hours awake (never over a take, never offline, the editor closed and its lane idle), it lists the space's records as they are now (the token Mac `meta/` over its pair, any other Mac the Library API), never only `library.json`, which hears of an edit only when the Library refreshes. It bakes the due ones among this Mac's videos (every video on an owner-class Mac, a member's own) and makes the copies they lack where the video is on this Mac. **(h) The waveform** is read in Rust with symphonia: the default sound track's loudest sample in every 20 ms (50 a second) over every channel, a byte each, the length the picture's (`shroom_encode::peaks`); it's served whole as `…/peaks`. **The review's fixes:** **(i)** On this Mac, the cleanup sweep and the upkeep never bake the video the editor has open; another Mac's bake under an open editor is the save guard's. **(j)** A video deleted while the editor is open on it closes the window. **(k)** A save the space refuses (the video changed since the window opened: another Mac made the edit the video, or it was deleted) gets its own amber line in the footer's place, “This video changed since you opened it, so this edit can't be saved.”, as the canvas board “Editor — getting ready and saving” draws it: Done gives way to Close (Return, Esc and ✕ close too), and nothing is saved. **(l)** Quit from the Dock, the popover or the mushroom's right-click menu, and Update, save the editor's changes first, as ⌘Q, Done and ✕ do; the final video is then made later, by the upkeep where the video is on this Mac. **(m)** A copy that was dropped because the record moved on never asks “Delete the original?”. **(n)** The time left by Revert is never overstated, and its numbers are words as law 16 has them in a sentence: “The original is kept two more days” from 47 hours left (the first hour after the question said 2 days), “one more day” from 24 to 47, then “{n} more hours” (two to nine in words, 10 to 23 in figures, rounded down), “one more hour” and “less than an hour”. **(o)** A cut or trim that changes nothing that plays (inside a cut, an end moved within one, putting back a cut another covers) is refused, and `shroom_edit`'s `check()` says why; the timeline offers Cut only for what of a part plays, by that length. **(p)** A bake interrupted after its record write (the original's files not all deleted) is finished by the next cleanup sweep. **(q)** The uploader never sends the original's files again over a baked video, a pass run again from the start included. **(r)** The Library's Copy thumbnail, for a video with no current picture, falls back to the page's own poster (the picture the page shows now, never a fixed `poster.jpg`), and to the plain link when there's no picture yet; a Library row's poster is the copy's own once it lands. **(s) No bake while the space's page is older than this app's.** A page from before the editor plays only the original's stream, so after a bake its share page and embed would read the video as unavailable; Yes, delete, the upkeep and the sweep first ask the page its version (`/_shroom/ping`) and wait while it's older, the original kept meanwhile (the next pass bakes it once the page is refreshed and its two days are up). **(t) The run log says each step** (`runtime/editor.rs`'s `said`, for Report a problem; the orchestrator's ask for the release, 2026-10-18): the record as the editor opens on it (its revision, what it keeps, its final video current or not, until when the original is kept); each save (revision, kept ranges, seconds kept and cut, the ends, cuts by hand and suggested) and its time; the final video's cut (encoder, parts, seconds, MB, ms) and its naming (MB sent), or why it was dropped; each bake (revision, the original's files deleted) and why one waits; the upkeep's and the sweep's counts. The core's line for each step already names the decision (`core open-editor`, `editor-saving`, `editor-copied`, `original-deleted`…). Ids cut to four, never a title, a key, a link or a word said; a report keeps each line whole. **(u)** The upkeep and the sweep bake only a copy past its two days, checked again inside the write: a newer revision whose copy landed while a pass ran keeps its own two days; Yes, delete bakes at once. Tests: `crates/shroom-encode/tests/cut.rs` (a steady tone's join measured with and without the fades) and `peaks.rs`; `crates/shroom-upload/tests/edits.rs`, `bake.rs`, `library.rs`; `crates/shroom-core/tests/editor.rs`; `runtime/editor.rs`'s and `media.rs`'s units; the editor drive on the hosted Mac (`--drive editor`, `app/test/editor-drive.mjs`). | The editor plays and draws from a file on this Mac (B195 (e)), and WebKit plays a video only from an address that answers byte ranges. A lane per kind of work keeps the window answering while a fetch, a cut or a pass takes minutes. The release's encoder is LGPL, with VideoToolbox and the concat demuxer but no concat filter (B82): re-encoding each kept range is frame-exact and needs nothing new but `afade`. An unfaded join clicks (PR #59's numbers, B198). The upkeep is how the two days hold with no Worker cron (B197 (d)), so it has to see every edit, not only the ones the Library has read. Each of the review's fixes closes a way an edit could be lost, made twice, laid over the wrong clock, or its time said wrong | Tauri's `asset:` protocol (files by a path the webview names, inside a scope that would have to cover `~/.shroom`); a `blob:` address (the whole recording in the webview's memory); a local HTTP server (a port any process on the Mac can reach); one thread for the whole lane (a fetch or a cut would hold Save and ✕); a save over a bake, the last writer winning (a list made on the old clock cuts the wrong seconds of the baked video); bundling x264 (GPL: the bundled encoder is never `--enable-gpl`, B82); stream copy without re-encoding (cuts land on the recording's 6 s keyframes); the concat filter or a filter graph (not in the release's encoder); shifting each part to start at zero (the picture late, the sound longer at every join); a fixed 1100×720 window (bigger than a 1024-wide screen); fetching the video at every open (minutes and bandwidth again for a long recording); the Library's cache for the upkeep (an edit saved and the app quit before the Library refreshed was missed by every later pass); peaks decoded in the webview (an hour's sound is about 1.4 GB of samples there) or by the bundled ffmpeg (it writes no raw sound); greying Edit… while the upkeep runs (a pass can cut copies for minutes, and the token Mac's sweep reaches other Macs' videos, which no greying here can); the save-failed line for a refused save (“Check your connection” would be false, and Done could never succeed); asking before Quit or Update (✕ is already Done, N63, and a question would stand between the person and what they asked for); asking “Delete the original?” over a dropped copy (Yes, delete would bake nothing, or a newer copy no one has seen); keeping a change that plays the same (Done saved it and the final video was made again, minutes of encoding for nothing); a Worker cron to finish an interrupted bake (B197 (d)'s no: a new Worker power, for what the next sweep does); sending the original's files and then deleting them, as the first build did (an upload for nothing, and the original's bytes back in the space meanwhile); the original's `poster.jpg` as the Library's fallback picture (a first frame an edit may have trimmed off); baking whatever the page's version (an old page reads a baked video as unavailable) or raising `IFACE_MIN` for the editor (every Mac of a space whose owner hasn't updated would read its needs-update line over everything, not only editing); logging every edit step's times or the words a cut removes (the core's line per step names it already; the words are the speaker's) |
| B205 | **Names, chapters and captions: Whisper hears each minute, one model names, rules place the chapters** (spec N66, provisional; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: build B171's plan now, decide the release later; under his standing ruling for this work the build's calls are made on judgment and listed here for him to overturn) | **(a) Hearing:** Cloudflare's `@cf/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo` on each ~60 s window of a take: ten 6 s pieces, each window after the first led by the last piece of the window before. A word is kept from the window that heard it whole: from 1.5 s before a window's own first piece to 1.5 s before the piece after its last. The audio is read out of the pieces themselves (the sound track's samples rewrapped as ADTS, the video never read; ranged reads for a big piece) and sent as a stream, so no window needs base64 or a second copy. Each call asks Whisper with its voice-activity filter on and the voice cuts' prime (`AI_HEAR_PRIME`: their cue and the hesitations their planner reads), and a word without a letter or a digit is dropped: unfiltered, Whisper wrote “Thank you. Thank you.” into a silent minute and two minutes of made-up music and “. .” into a room's noise; filtered, nothing, and speech kept its words and times (239 → 240 words, shifts within ±0.03 s), measured on the test account with the sound sent as the step sends it (2026-10-18, `dev/proposals/ai-titles-chapters/poc/results/prime-2026-10-18.json`; the cue itself measured by the voice cuts' tester, 34 of 34 heard primed and filtered). Rejected: the cue alone as the prime (written into silence and music unfiltered), and no prime (fewer cues heard). **(b) The clock:** words carry seconds on the recording's joined clock, the clock of `markers` and the editor's `meta.edits`: each piece's time inside its take from its own sound track, and a take after a pause starts where the one before ended. **(c) Naming:** one call to `@cf/openai/gpt-oss-120b` (falling back to `@cf/google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` on a refusal or an empty answer) gives the title and the chapters as JSON; the words that play go in, one line per sentence with its start. **(d) Rules held in code, whatever the model says:** a title on one line, no wrapping quotes or full stop, at most 60 characters, its first letter a capital; chapters snapped to the nearest sentence start, the first at 0:00, none under 20 s (a short one gives its place to the one after it), at most 12 (the shortest fold into the one before), only on a video of 90 s or more and only two or more. **(e) Captions** are written from the same words, never a second hearing: a cue ends at a sentence's end, a pause over 1 s, 6 s, or two lines of 42 characters. A lone hesitation (um, uh, er, erm, hmm) is left out of them, its sentence's end kept; the words keep it, for voice cuts. **(f) The early title** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: the title must be there when the page opens): once the first minute of a take is heard while it records (its first window, with 20 words or more), one title-only call on those words, a “shroom cut” slip and the sentence it breaks off left out, makes the record's title, marked `named.early`; the full name and chapters at done take its place, never over a title typed meanwhile. It runs before any more hearing (the free day favours it), once a take: a model that fails it is let go, a used-up day or a busy model waits like any step. A take under a minute, or without speech in its first minute, gets its name at Stop. The owner's page shows it in the title field and swaps in the full name in place, quietly; a viewer who opened early sees the full name on their next visit. Discard and Restart remove the take's record, and the early title with it. | Measured on the test account (dev/proposals/ai-titles-chapters.md §4–§5): a real 35:39 recording cost 3,072 neurons an hour to hear and ~590 to name, Cloudflare's meter matching the computed figures to the neuron; gpt-oss-120b put every chapter start within seconds of the book's own and gave the best titles in every take; captions add nothing. A stream needs no base64 (the probe: raw bytes are refused, a stream is heard). The early title, measured (`worker/dev/ai-early.mjs`, results in dev/proposals/ai-titles-chapters/poc/results/early-title-2026-10-18.json): on the public how-to video and two invented talks, a title from the first 45–60 s was the full one's or close to it in all three (“Setting up Shroom screen recorder” for “Setting up and using Shroom”); each early call cost 42–93 neurons, a take's name ~5–10% more. | **Deepgram nova-3** (ten times the price; the free day would cover about 20 minutes). **Clef or Clef-flash placing the chapter cuts** (fast, but it missed topic changes on real speech). **A whole take in one call** (the free plan's 10 ms of CPU and 1,000 storage calls an invocation). **Hearing on the Mac** (macOS 26 only; kept as a later saving of ~85% of the cost). **A second hearing for captions** | **The title only at Stop** (the page would open dated and change 10–20 s later, Bagrat's no). **An early title at 30 s** (as good in these talks, but it would need a half window and a signal of its own; at a minute it rides the first window).
| B206 | **The space's own page does it, asked by the Macs each minute; a schedule finishes what's left** (spec N66, provisional; as B205) | **(a) Where:** the space's page (the Worker) with an `ai` binding, on the owner's own account; turning naming on re-wires the page with that binding and a Cron Trigger each minute, from the Mac that holds the setup key (only that Mac can deploy), and off re-wires it without them (`wire::set_ai`; never over a page newer than the app's). A refresh after an update and a re-wiring keep naming as the live page has it (the same read of the Worker's settings that tells ours from someone else's), and the page's ping adds `"ai": true` when it carries the AI, which the setup Mac's page check reads for the switch. A page without the binding answers the naming routes `{"ai": false}` at once and its pages look exactly as before. **(b) Who asks:** each Mac's uploader, as it uploads a take, posts `POST /_shroom/api/ai {rec, through}` every ten pieces up (unbroken from the first), and `{rec, done: true}` once the take is live, keeping an `ai-owed` marker beside the take until the space answers at all (said again from launch, then every ten minutes). The words go through a lane of their own, so a step never holds the upload. The device key is the door; the setup Mac, which may hold none, signs with the parent proof `enroll` uses. Only the take's maker or the owner may ask. **(c) Small steps:** one call takes one step — hears one window, in order, or names the done take — and answers `more` when another step can be taken now; the Mac asks again while it says so (three times for a minute, twelve for a done take). A step a person or the schedule takes holds the take's mark under `.shroom/ai/later/` as a two-minute lease, so the schedule and a Mac never take the same step; whatever is left waits there, and the schedule gives the oldest due mark one step a minute. A step that keeps failing for another reason is tried again a little later each time, and after five tries the take is let go (no name, no captions; the owner page stops waiting). **(d) Stored beside the take:** the transcript grows a window at a time at `rec/<id>/ai/transcript.json` = `{v:1, model, language, duration, heard_through, done, windows, clock, words:[[text, start, end],…], tail}` — the words final up to `heard_through`, the last 1.5 s held in `tail` until the next window hears them whole — which the voice-cut build reads (N65). The record keeps what the page needs, so the page reads nothing more: the title (while untitled), `named {by:"ai", model, at, rev, clock, words, title, chapters:[[start, title],…], language}` (title null when fewer than twelve words were said) and `captions {language}`, written with the same `If-Match` as a rename. **(e) Turning it on** *(superseded by B235, 2026-10-25: on by default, said once)* (Bagrat, 2026-10-18: off by default, but very visible; one switch for every AI feature; the turn-on boards approved as drawn, the words his to tweak later): on the Mac that holds the setup key, while the space's AI is off, the popover's lowest banner (under a saved take, the space, recording off and an update) says what shroom can do and offers **Turn on…** and **Not now**. Turn on… opens a short confirm in the popover with the one cost line (“…free for about 2½ hours of new video a day; more is about 4¢ per video hour on Cloudflare’s $5 plan”, the price per video hour so it reads as hours of video, not of the day), no Return default (law 9); its Turn on re-wires the page as the switch does, the banner saying “Turning on — a few seconds.” meanwhile, and the status line says once that it's on. A Turn on that didn't go through says so in the banner (law 12) and is tried again quietly, ten minutes then doubling to an hour, until it goes through or Not now. **Not now hides the banner for good on that Mac** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18); so does AI on from anywhere. The switch in Settings · Space, “AI for new videos”, stays the home, voice cuts included. Members' Macs and the space's other Macs never show it (they can't re-wire the page and never see a cost); the Library's card is gone; nothing on the tray. **(f) What a report shows of it** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, for every build: enough in the Mac's own log that a report shows what went wrong): each answer to a Mac's signal carries `note`, one short line (the window heard with its time and word count, the early title and its model, the name with its model, time, chapters and tokens and, when the second model answered, the first's code; or why it waits, with Workers AI's code alone), and the Mac logs it with the signal (`naming <take>: Done → On · …`). What the schedule does on its own reaches the Mac as the record: `named` keeps `ms`, `tokens` and `fallback`, a take let go keeps `code`, the free day's `naming` keeps `code`, and a Library refresh logs one line per take whose AI state changed (`naming <id>: named by gpt-oss-120b in 1530 ms: 4 chapters from 238 words, 900+80 tokens`). Turning the AI on or off logs how long the page took. No line holds a word said, a title, a key or a link; each passes the report's cleaning as it is. About a line a recorded minute and two a take: a day of ten five-minute takes is some 70 lines of the report's 200. | The space already holds the pieces and the Worker can read them; no Mac and no member holds an AI key, and a member's take is named on the owner's account without the member seeing a cost (B171). The Mac knows when pieces land; the Worker doesn't (uploads go straight to storage). The schedule covers a Mac that went to sleep or offline. **Measured at Cloudflare's edge** (2026-10-18, `worker/dev/ai-cpu.mjs`, one invocation a minute on the test account): the ping 1.7 ms of CPU, a page 4.4 ms (10 ms with chapters and captions), the captions file 9.5 ms, naming 12 ms, hearing a minute 20–61 ms — over the free plan's 10 ms an invocation, which Cloudflare enforces with some give for overruns that aren't constant. The test account is on the free plan (a deploy asking for a CPU limit is refused, 100328), and every step ran there: this build's two takes heard, named and captioned, one finished by the schedule alone once its trigger reached the network about five minutes in (`worker/dev/ai-live.mjs`), and the 36-minute take's 36 windows (B205). Reading a window's sound is most of a hearing step (about 1 ms of CPU a ranged read, 22 reads, `ai-cpu` split by part); a Mac-side audio file per minute (one read) is the saving kept in hand should Cloudflare's give tighten. The banner: Bagrat wanted AI off by default (not to spend the free allowance unasked) yet seen, and the update's banner is the popover's one proven place for an offer; the cost goes one tap away so the banner stays an invitation. | B207 | **The free day: when the 10,000 daily neurons run out, the rest waits for 00:00 UTC, with no error anywhere** (spec N66, N67, provisional; as B205; the orchestrator relayed Bagrat's ask the same day: build for the free plan as the default and never show an error) | Workers AI refuses a call once the account's free daily allocation is spent on the free plan (error 3036 by the docs, and 4006 as the test account answered at its cap on 2026-10-18; either code, or the words “daily free allocation”, reads as the free day; “Account limited”, Cloudflare's errors page). | B208 | **Edits never re-hear: chapters and captions follow the saved edit on every view; the name is made again only when an edit changes what's said** (spec N66 (5), provisional; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator, asked the build to think through edits and captions and decide) | **(a) One clock:** the transcript and the chapters stay on the recording's own clock, the clock of the editor's edit list (`meta.edits`, N63, when it lands). **(b) Mapped on every view:** the page maps each chapter's start through the edit (a start inside a cut moves to where the cut ends; a chapter left with under 20 s folds into its neighbour; fewer than two left, none), and captions are written from the words that survive it (a word is cut when most of it is; a cue never spans a cut; cues are on the clock of what the page plays, the original with its cuts skipped or the editor's permanent copy). Re-hearing would pay ~85% of the cost again for nothing. | **Hearing again after an edit.** **Writing edited caption and chapter files at save** (a save from another Mac or the command line would leave them stale). **Renaming on every save** (titles changing under people, for small trims) |
| B209 | **The page: chapters as sections of the seek bar, a Captions button, the named title** (spec N67, provisional; Bagrat's ruling on chapters, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator, after the canvas boards; the rest on judgment, as B205) | **(a) Chapters:** the bar is drawn as one section per chapter with 2 px gaps (a layer behind the native slider, which keeps its keyboard and screen-reader behaviour); hover shows a tip with the time and chapter at the pointer; a touch shows it above the thumb while dragging and for 1.5 s after, the thumb grown while the finger is on it and the bar's touch area 44 px tall; the current chapter's name follows the time where the row has room for it (480 px and wider: a phone's row has none beside its five controls, so there the tip names it; Bagrat kept it so, 2026-10-18, over a line under the controls or no sound button on phones), and the slider's spoken value adds it everywhere. The chapters come in with the page (no extra request), already on the bar's clock (the edited one, once N63 lands). **(b) Captions:** a native `<track>` from `GET /_shroom/cc/<id>` (WebVTT, written per view from the words and the edit), and a Captions button in the player's row, off by default, remembered on that browser. **(c) The title:** the named title is the record's `title`, so the page, its tab, unfurls, oEmbed, the download's name and the Library show it with no change; the owner's page shows it in the title field — as the field's placeholder, so it stays the space's until a title is typed — with the line “Named from what you said. Click the title to change it.” until it is edited (removed: B241). An owner's page opened before the name lands asks for it now and then (`GET /_shroom/named/<id>`, the maker's or an owner's browser only, for a recent untitled take) and shows it when it comes, or the free day's line. Copy thumbnail draws a typed title only, as before: a picture copied into an email can't be renamed afterwards, so a name nobody has looked at never goes on one. **(d) The embed** plays the same chapters and captions. **(e) The page version** moves to 0.1.25 (B61). | Bagrat's ruling and boards for (a); the rest keeps the page as it is for spaces without naming. | **A chapter list under the player** (option A on the canvas; not chosen). **Captions on by default** (most people watch with sound; the button is one click). **A separate title field for the AI's name** (every title reader would need to learn it) |
| B210 | **The page counts plays, in the space's own bucket, with nothing that names a viewer** (spec N68, ratified as built by Bagrat on 2026-10-18; Bagrat asked for cleanup suggestions 2026-10-18 and the same day picked, relayed by the orchestrator, plays **on by default** with the switch in Settings, and **plays and unique-ish viewers** both counted) | **The beacon.** `VIEW_JS` runs on a viewer's page and the embed, never the owner page. It sends `sendBeacon` at the first play and at each tab hide, with the share watched (the union of `video.played` over the length) and a random token held only in page memory. **The record**, `.shroom/views/<id>.json` by CAS: plays, unique-ish viewers, ends by tenths watched, seconds watched, plays per day (400 days), first and last played. A viewer is the first 8 hex of SHA-256(a salt replaced daily, the address, the user-agent, the id), kept for the day only. `views/since.json` says when counting began. **The route** `POST /_shroom/view/<id>`: ≤ 512 bytes; a named other `Origin` or `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` → 403; WebKit's `Origin: null` from a page being left is taken; else 204. It writes only for a live video while `plays.json` is on, and a seal deletes the video's record. **The switch**, owner-class: `GET\|POST /_shroom/api/plays` writes `.shroom/plays.json` `{on, at}`. Off keeps what was counted. WORKER-INTERFACE §4.21 | No cookie and no device storage keeps the viewer out of the consent question (EDPB 2/2023 treats localStorage as a cookie). The address and the user-agent are read once a day for one hash, under a salt that's forgotten. **R2**: the bucket and the token the space already has, so no new service, binding or permission and no wiring change. The free tier covers about 500,000 plays a month. The counts live and go with the videos. Counting media requests says little about how far someone watched: the player now plays the single-file copy, in ranges the browser chooses (B177). **Proved by:** `worker/test/views.test.js` (26); `worker/dev/views-drive.mjs` (Chromium and WebKit: a bounce, a half and a full watch, the owner's own: 12/12; it found WebKit's `Origin: null`); `worker/dev/views-live.mjs` on the test account (2026-10-18: plays 2, viewers 2, both ends in the last tenth, the owner page carrying no beacon; everything it made deleted) | A Durable Object (exact, but a migration in wiring's script PUT and a second kind of state); D1 (a new token permission); Workers KV (1,000 writes a day, eventually consistent); Analytics Engine (keeps data three months, so it forgets a video's last play, and reading it needs Account Analytics Read); counting `/m/` requests (above); keeping no viewer hash (Bagrat chose viewers) |
| B211 | **A video is suggested when its plays have faded *and* it takes real room** (spec N68; the 1¢ size gate ratified by Bagrat, 2026-10-18; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: “fading and cost together”, and the rest to the build's judgment) | `fading-cost`, the Worker's default (`cleanupScore`). **Guards:** never one marked Keep (the record's `keep`), under 14 days old, played in the last 14 days, or before counting has watched it (`counting-too-new`). **Both must hold:** heat < 0.25, where heat is plays halving every 30 days × (0.3 + 0.7 × the median share watched), less than one full watch two months ago; **and** at least 1¢ a month at list price (≈ 0.67 GB, about 12 minutes at Normal). It must also be ≥ 30 days old and quiet ≥ 30 days. **Order:** cost ÷ (heat + 0.05), costliest per remaining play first (GreedyDual-Size's shape). **The reason**, a row fact (`copy::cleanup_reason`): “Watched 4 times, the last 10 months ago” · “Watched once, 5 months ago” · “Watched twice, stopped early, the last 4 months ago” (median under a quarter) · “Nobody has watched it”; days under 60, then months under 24, then years. `idle`, `fading`, `cost` and `gravity` stay in the Worker for comparison (`history/proposals/cleanup-suggestions.md`) | “AND” is the only combination whose reason a person can check in one line; a weighted sum would need its own explanation. 1¢ gives fewer, surer suggestions in a list that leads to deleting things (2 of the proposal's 12, 4 at half a cent). It's one constant, `FADING_COST.usdAtLeast`, to revisit with real counts. **Proved by:** `views.test.js` (the formulas, the route's scopes and paging), `crates/shroom-core/tests/cleanup.rs` (`reasons_read_as_row_facts`), `worker/dev/cleanup-sample.mjs` | A weighted sum of age, plays and size; `idle` alone (the proposal's first pick; past hits go too soon); `cost` alone (never suggests what isn't big); HN-style `gravity` (opaque); half a cent |
| B212 | **The money line is honest about Cloudflare's free 10 GB, and sizes are the bucket's** | **Sizes** are the record's `bytes`, which the Mac writes when its upload drains (`shroom_upload::upload::finish` lists `rec/<id>/` once and sums it; a listing that fails leaves it out). Otherwise the Worker estimates the length × 10 GB / 3 h (the FAQ's “about three hours in the free tier”). **The view's line** (`derive/library.rs:intro`): past the free 10 GB, “…Deleting all six frees 11 GB — about 17¢ a month off your Cloudflare bill.” (the cents are only what lies past 10 GB, and never under 1¢ once there is something); inside it, “…so this saves nothing yet.”; when deleting them would bring the space back under 10 GB, “…and brings your space back inside Cloudflare's free 10 GB.”; two read “Deleting both”, one “Deleting it”. A member's Mac says the size only: the bill is the owner's. **Space used** (Settings · General, owner-class): “34 GB — about 36¢ a month past Cloudflare's free 10 GB” · “8.2 GB — inside Cloudflare's free 10 GB” (money: cents under a dollar, “$1.87” above). **Inside the free 10 GB the suggestions still show:** the cost gate reads the list price, the room a video takes, not the bill. **Each row's cost** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18: only past the free tier): past the free 10 GB a row reads its size at the list price, “5¢ a month”, at least 1¢ — also when the space is only partly past, where the intro still says the exact saving; inside it, and on a member's Mac, no row says money | At R2's $0.015 per GB-month the saving is cents; saying “0¢” or a list price would mislead. The line worth saying is getting back inside the free space. Gating on the bill would suggest nothing until a space passed 10 GB, then everything at once. **Proved by:** `cleanup.rs` (`the_money_line_is_honest_about_the_free_ten_gigabytes`, Space used's three shapes, and each row's cost past, partly past and inside the free 10 GB and on a member's Mac), `crates/shroom-upload/tests/progress.rs` (the drained record's `bytes` is the bucket's sum) | Cents always; GB only; hiding the suggestions inside the free tier; estimating every size from its length; a row priced at only its share past the free 10 GB (one row's cost would hang on the others') |
| B213 | **The Library says so in one line, and Suggested to clean up is its own view** (spec N68; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed: **one tap with Undo**) | **The line** (`LibraryBody.cleanup_line`) sits above the rows while Suggest videos to clean up is on and there's something to suggest: “Six videos might be worth deleting — they take 11 GB.” with Review… and **Not now** (quiet text). Not now remembers the suggestions' ids (`prefs.cleanup_dismissed`) until they change. **The view** (`library-cleanup`, a face of `library`) stands in place of the list: ‹ Library, the title, the money line (B212), and one card of suggestions. Each row shows its poster, title, then when it was made · its size · why (B211), with Open page, Keep and **Delete in red text** (law 9). Under the card: the line on Keep, and Delete all… (red text, from two up: the several-videos sheet). **Delete is one tap:** the row leaves at once, from the list behind too, and an Undo line stays 5 s (`UNDO_MS`) before the delete is sent, because a sealed delete can't be taken back (B125). A second tap sends the first; closing the window sends what waits (the tap meant delete). Offline, Keep and Delete grey. **Keep** sets the record's `keep` under If-Match, and the row leaves for good. **Reading:** on each entry of the Library (with the switch on) and of Settings · General, owner-class Macs read the whole space's and members their own. Never under an uninstall or a disconnect sheet. A failed read retries on the Library's backoff. **No Return default and no Esc in the view** (a window's rule): ‹ Library is the way back. **Not now in place of a ✕:** the gallery holds every visible word to the Scene's, and a word says more than a glyph. **Your videos** (Settings · General, under the help line): Count plays (the space's switch; greyed off owner-class Macs, and until the space has said), Suggest videos to clean up (this Mac's pref, on), Space used. A switch the space didn't take says “Couldn't change that just now — try again in a moment.” (law 12) | Two clicks to the list, one to act: the cleanup job without a mode to learn. Undo instead of a sheet per video: Bagrat's pick, and the sheet stays for “all”. **Proved by:** `crates/shroom-core/tests/cleanup.rs` (13 walkthroughs: the line and the view, Undo's timing, a second tap and a closed window, Keep and a failed one, Delete all, Not now, the money line, offline, reading by scope and switch, Settings' switches, a member's Mac, the deadlines); region5's inventory and copy registry; the gallery (`library-cleanup*`, `general-your-videos*`: 6872/6872) | A ✕ glyph (the canvas's first draw); a confirm sheet per video; Esc as Back in the view (a window has no Esc, law 9 is sheets'); reading the suggestions on every launch |
| B214 | **The Mac reads the suggestions and sets the switch under its device key; Keep is a record rewrite** | `shroom_cloud::member::Space::cleanup` (paged) and `::plays`; `CleanupPage`/`CleanupItem` read `counting`, `bytes`, `plays`, `quietDays`, `medianWatched`, `candidate`, `score` and `reason.code`. The Library lane (`runtime/library.rs`): `LibCmd::Suggest` pages to its end (≤ 500 pages) and posts `Suggested` with the candidates in score order and the bytes of every item read. `LibCmd::Plays` posts `Plays`. Both go through `worker_door`, the device key every enrolled Mac holds, the token Mac too. `LibCmd::Keep` uses `shroom_upload::library::keep` over the token Mac's pair, or a `manage` mint elsewhere, as a rename does. | B215 | **The `shroom` command's door: this Mac's device key, never the token; the command follows the app it ships in** (spec N62, ratified 2026-10-18; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: shroom usable from the command line whether the app runs or not, then built in full) | `crates/shroom-cli`, a binary `shroom`. **Its folder** is the one of the app it ships in: from its own path (`…/X.app/Contents/MacOS/shroom-cli`, links resolved), `Contents/Info.plist`'s `CFBundleIdentifier` picks the folder by the app's own rule (`am.shroom` → `~/.shroom`, `am.shroom.dev` → `~/.shroom-dev`, `runtime/home.rs`), and that identifier is the app it opens (`open -g -b`); off a bundle (a `cargo` build) it is `~/.shroom` and `am.shroom`. It reads `space.json`, `device.key`, `status.json` and `library.json` there, writes nothing, and never reads `token.json`. **Every call** goes to the space's own page under the device key, on the routes the app's Library and Members use (WORKER-INTERFACE §4: `library`, `mint` for a manage credential, `gone`, `invites`, `members`), so a member's Mac can do from its command exactly what its role lets it do in the app. A delete, a member's removal and a batch invite change nothing without `--yes`, and title words never name a video to delete; while a take is open (`status.json`, its pid alive and named shroom) rename, delete, invite and removal refuse with `take_open` (law 4). Offline, `list` answers from the app's cached Library (`"cached": true`). **Cleanup candidates** are the page's own suggestions (`POST /_shroom/api/cleanup`, paged by `after`; read with the cleanup work's own client and structs, #57's, plus the facts the command shows) where the page makes them, with plays, when last played, quiet days (the page's fractions, shown whole) and a reason, and whether the space counts plays (`counting`); otherwise (404) the videos older than `--older-than` days (90); the output has the same fields either way, unknown ones `null`. **Its answers:** words for a person, or with `--json` one object, `{"ok":true,…}` or `{"ok":false,"error":<code>,"message":…}`, with fixed exit codes 0–11 (`site/cli.md`'s table, held equal to `out.rs` by `site/test/cli.test.mjs`). Every line it says is in `src/say.rs`, ratified by Bagrat as built with N62 (2026-10-18); a test holds the other files to it. **Its own log** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: enough on this Mac after the release to report a problem): `logs/cli.log`, one line a run — UTC time, the verb (and `members remove`, `invite cancel`, `record start`'s word), the options by name, `ok` or the code and exit, what the answer counted (its lists' lengths, `counts`, the take, an id as `AbCd…`), the space's refusal (status and class) or the app's code, and the time it took; never a title, a name, a link, a search or a path. 600, rolled at 64 KB to `cli.log.1`, written only where shroom's folder exists; Each line starts with the stamp `run.log`'s lines have (UTC to the second, the log baseline), and Report a problem and Copy diagnostics carry its last 30 among the app's lines by that time, the whole within the report's 1,000. Rejected: writing into `run.log` (the app is that folder's one writer, and its roll would race the command's); no log (a report couldn't show what the command did when the app wasn't asked); logging the arguments (they are titles and people's names). Titles and names are written by the people in a space, so what it prints can't drive a terminal: plain output shows each character a terminal acts on (C0 controls but tab and line feed, DEL, C1, the bidi marks) as �, and `--json` escapes them, still exactly what the space holds (the security review, 2026-10-18); the guide says never to follow instructions in them. Release builds refuse the dev-only `--home`, `--space-base` and `SHROOM_HOME` (`tools/release-build.sh` checks) | The token is the one secret that can change the person's Cloudflare account; the device key can only do what this Mac may do in its own space, and the page already enforces it. A command that follows its app needs no flag to find its folder, and a dev build never reads the real `~/.shroom` (B4) | Reading `token.json` and calling Cloudflare directly (the token's powers in every agent's hands); a credential minted for the command (another secret to keep and revoke); asking only the running app (nothing works while it's quit); a `--home` flag in release builds (a way to point the command at another folder's key) |
| B216 | **The app's control socket: the command records through the app, behind General's switch** (spec N62, ratified 2026-10-18; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: an agent may start and stop recordings only once a Settings switch is on, off by default) | `app/src-tauri/src/runtime/control.rs`: `control.sock` in the flavor's folder (the folder 700, the socket 600, every connection's peer uid checked with `getpeereid`), opened once the core has handled `Booted` — an ask before then would be answered from an empty World, and the command that just opened shroom asks the moment the socket is there (the hosted Mac caught it, 2026-10-18) — one JSON line each way, at most 1 KB in: `{"v":1,"op":"record.start"}` (`.pause`, `.resume`, `.stop`) → `{"ok":…,"take":"none\|countdown\|recording\|paused\|stopping","error"?:<code>,"link"?:…}`. The runtime posts `Event::Cli{id, op}` and holds the connection; the core answers each ask exactly once with `Effect::Cli` (codes `recording_off`, `not_set_up`, `take_open`, `no_take`, `busy`, `not_ready`, never words). The run log has each ask and its answer by number — the op, a start's `no-count`/`no-label`, then ok or no, the take or the code, and the milliseconds — and a connection from another user, an ask it couldn't read, an answer the command had gone before; never the link a stop answers with. **General's switch** “Let it start and stop recordings” (`Prefs.agent_recording`, off by default, kept in `prefs.json`) gates all four. A Mac with no space hears `not_set_up` before that: Settings needs a space (B160). **The refusal's link** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: it says recording is off, that the switch turns it on, and gives a shroom link that opens Settings there): the command words `recording_off` in three lines, the last `shroom://settings/agent`, which `--json` also gives as `settings_link` (exit 10 as before). The app takes it as it takes a join link (`RunEvent::Opened`, held until the core is up, so a click launches shroom too) and posts `Event::SettingsLink`: Settings opens on General, the switch's row scrolled into view and lit: the core holds the light 6 s (`SettingsFields.lit`: cleared on the Tick, with the window, by a flip of the switch or another tab), long enough for a window opened cold to draw itself, and the window shows it for its own first 2.4 s, then fades it (the hosted Mac caught a cold launch drawing after a 2 s light had gone; edge `idle-ready → settings-general`, rule `settings-link`). It flips nothing. Mid-take it opens nothing (law 4); a sheet open in Settings stays, and Settings comes forward as it stands. Otherwise each ask is its click: start is the popover's Start, open or not (the grants re-probed first, then the count and the permission beat; edge `idle-ready → countdown`), pause the tray's, Resume and Stop the paused popover's; a stop answers with its link and copies nothing (law 10). The command opens its app when it isn't running and waits up to 15 s for the socket (`--no-launch` doesn't); a socket left by a crash is replaced when the app starts. **Without the count, and the label** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: a flag to skip the countdown, and a small label in the top-right corner saying the agent is recording the screen, with a flag to show it or not): `record start --no-countdown` sends `"countdown":false` and the core fires the count's end at once (the tray goes from idle-ready to recording when capture begins, the machine's own edge); `--no-label` sends `"label":false`. A take the command starts with its label (`World.agent`, the take's opening instant) shows `Scene.label` while it records — on the display it records, top right under the menu bar, in a window made as shroom starts and left out of the video by the recorder's exclusion list as shroom's other windows are (never made late: wanted only once capture runs, it could miss that take's list), and passing every click through; paused, the label goes; a take of the camera only has none. The app's own Start keeps its count and never shows the label. **What keeps a take visible:** the tray's red dot, always, whatever the flags; the countdown unless `--no-countdown`; the label unless `--no-label` — the accepted risk above rests on them. The label is on by default. Bagrat approved the label's board as drawn — its look, its words and the label on by default — 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator | macOS charges a capture to the process that asks, so a command that recorded by itself would ask for the screen as the terminal (history/POC-LEARNINGS.md); through the app, the take is the app's own: its grants, count, tray and stop. A socket in the 700 folder reaches this user only, needs no port, and answers. The link only opens and lights, so a page that fires it changes nothing: the person's own click on the switch is the consent. **The switch is consent, not a wall against code already running as the person** (the security review, 2026-10-18): any process of theirs can write `prefs.json` and reach the socket — as it can read the device key and the takes in the same folder — and so record through shroom's own grants. The count and the tray show every take, as for one started by hand. **An accepted risk:** Bagrat accepted it, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator | A localhost port (any local process and any browser page can reach it); a URL scheme (any web page can fire one, and it can't answer); Apple Events (an automation prompt for each app an agent runs in); XPC (a helper and an entitlement for something no shell can speak). For the link: turning the switch on itself (the person flips it); a `shroom settings` command that opens Settings (the agent would open it unasked; the person clicks); a scheme of the dev build's own (a dev bundle claims none, as for join links); `recording_off` before `not_set_up` (the link would open nothing on a Mac with no space). For the switch: sealed in the login Keychain under the app's signing identity, a `prefs.json` value that doesn't verify read as off (a self-signed identity's keychain asks again on every dev build — history/POC-LEARNINGS.md's walls — and only a release would prove it); asking the person on the first take the command starts each launch (a new prompt in the popover: a design call, Bagrat's). For the label: off by default (with the count skipped it would leave only the tray's dot); the label in the video too (it would sit in every video an agent records); showing it while paused (it would say recording when nothing is) |
| B217 | **The command is always on PATH, from inside the app** (spec N62, ratified 2026-10-18; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: always installed — by the install line, inside the bundle, and with Homebrew) | **The bundle** carries it as a second external binary beside the encoder: `tauri.release.conf.json` → `Contents/MacOS/shroom-cli`, built for the floor (`MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET`) and signed with the bundle; `tools/release-build.sh` refuses a bundle whose command is missing, unsigned, silent on `--version` or open to `--home`. It is `shroom-cli` in the bundle because `Contents/MacOS/shroom` is the app (and the default file system ignores case). **The install line** links `~/.local/bin/shroom` to it, and adds one marked line to the login profile (`.zprofile`, `.bash_profile` for bash) only when `~/.local/bin` isn't on PATH already: `export PATH="$PATH:$HOME/.local/bin"`, after the system's folders, so nothing already sitting in `~/.local/bin` stands in for a system command (the security review, 2026-10-18); `--uninstall` takes back the link (only if it points into shroom), the mark, and the line after it only when that is still shroom's own. **The cask:** `binary "#{Dir.home}/Applications/shroom.app/Contents/MacOS/shroom-cli", target: "shroom"`, for a release that carries the command only: the line waits in `installer/homebrew/shroom.rb` behind `#cli: `, and `render-cask.sh` keeps it when the release's manifest says `cli=shroom-cli` (`tools/package-release.sh` writes that for a bundle carrying the command) and drops it otherwise — the tap renders `main`'s cask for the latest release, and Homebrew fails an install whose binary isn't there, so a cask merged before the first such release would break `brew install` until it shipped. An update replaces the bundle; the link follows it. **The dev build carries it too** (`npm run build:dev` builds it first and bundles it through `tauri.dev-cli.conf.json`), so the full e2e installs and runs it with the real install line | One copy, inside the signed app, updated with it; nothing to install or update apart. A link in a folder of the person's own needs no administrator | A separate download, `npm` or Homebrew formula (a second thing to keep in step with the app); `/usr/local/bin` (needs `sudo`, which the install line never asks for); a shell function in the profile (invisible to agents that run without it) |
| B218 | **Many at once** (spec N62, ratified 2026-10-18; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: batch commands, so an agent asked to invite the engineering team makes every person's link in one call and mails each their own) | `crates/shroom-cli/src/batch.rs`. **Items** as arguments (`--batch`) or `--from <file>` (`-` for stdin): a JSON array of strings or objects, or one per line, blank and `#` lines skipped. **Answers** item by item in the order given, each with a stable `status`, plus `counts`; when any item failed the answer is `partial` (exit 11) with every result. **Safe to run again:** a name already invited is `already_invited` (no link: the space keeps only a hash of it) unless `--renew`, one already in the space `already_member`, a repeated name asked once; a delete of an unknown id is `not_found`; a rename to the same title `unchanged`. **Caps:** 100 items a call, and the space's room for waiting invites (20, WORKER-INTERFACE §4.12), which only the people a call would invite anew count against — both refused before anything is made. `--dry-run` on every change, one item or many. An item of the wrong shape (a `--from` object without an `id`, an invite id that isn't one) is refused before anything is done, never dropped: an id from the command line never becomes a path of its own (`shroom-cloud` sends only an id of the route's shape). **A batch invite asks first:** without `--yes` it makes nothing and answers `needs_confirmation` with each name's plan (`would_invite`); a single `invite "<name>"` doesn't, the person having named that one person. **Names only**, and shroom sends nothing: each person gets their own link through the agent's own mail or messaging tool. The commands: `invite --batch`, `invite cancel <id>…`, `delete <id>…`, `members remove <id>…`, `rename --batch "<id>=<title>"`, `links <video>…`, `list --mine --since 7d` (a digest's data), `members --json` (to check the space against a team list) | One call and one answer for a team, and a re-run after a lost answer or a partial failure does only what's left. **Asking first is the mechanism's, not the guide's:** in the first batch demo the agent skipped the guide's “show the list” and invited and mailed someone on leave; the email couldn't be taken back | shroom mailing the invites (a mail service we'd operate, and the person's own tool knows their people); one call per person (slow, and a failure midway leaves no summary); renewing an already-invited person without being asked (breaks a link that may be in their inbox); a scheduled weekly digest (a service we'd run; `list --since` gives an agent's scheduler what it needs) |
| B219 | **The guide an agent reads, shroom.am/cli, and Settings' “Your coding agent”** (spec N62, ratified 2026-10-18; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: in the app, “here's a prompt for your agent”; the card's words and place left to the build, and the switch drawn on the canvas) | **`site/cli.md`**, an Agent Skills `SKILL.md` (frontmatter `name`, `description`), served at `shroom.am/cli` and `/cli.md` by `site/worker.js` (both in `run_worker_first`) and built into the command as `shroom guide`, so the words an agent reads are the command's own. Its parts: finding the command; what it can do; many at once, with the team-invite flow (look the people up or ask, show the list, make the links in one call with `--yes`, send each person only their own link with the agent's own tool); the rules (ask before anything that can't be undone, hand an invite link over once, record only with the switch); and every error code with what to do. `/agent` points to it. `site/test/cli.test.mjs` holds every command and option it names to `main.rs`, its error table to `out.rs`, its ask-first rules, the team-invite flow, and no secret or dev-only flag in it. **The card** (`derive/settings.rs`, `General.svelte`; the canvas's SettingsGeneral board): “Your coding agent”, the prompt “Use shroom on my Mac: read https://shroom.am/cli and follow it.” in a field with Copy (“Copied”, law 10) and Read it first, the line under them, and the switch | One page an agent can be pointed at in one sentence; a skill's file is the shape agents already load; built into the command, it matches the version installed and works offline | An MCP server (a process to install and keep running, wired differently by every agent); the plugin marketplace (decommissioned); `llms.txt` alone (no rules an agent treats as instructions); the guide inside `--help` only (an agent reads help after it has decided what to run) |
| B220 | **The early channel: signed pre-releases, taken by a Mac that opts in** (in-frame, but for the versions; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: he wants pre-releases on his own Mac to try what's coming while other work waits; users opt in from Settings later, canvas first; his Mac may carry signed pre-releases, never a dev build; pre-release work must never hold up `main`; and early builds carry a suffix — there are no users yet) | **Versions:** an early build is `x.y.z-early.n` (n from 1) on the way to release `x.y.z`, and sorts `0.1.24` < `0.1.25-early.1` < `0.1.25-early.2` < `0.1.25` < `0.1.26-early.1`. The same order in every reader: `shroom_update::Version` (its `Stage`), the page compare `shroom_cloud::wire::may_deploy_over`, `bump-version.sh` (its `key`; it also moves an unreleased `x.y.z` tree to `x.y.z-early.1`), the installer's shape check, and `release.yml`'s pointer step; each has its test. GitHub marks the release *pre-release*. **Branches:** the channel's code is ordinary on `main`. Early builds are cut only from a long-lived integration branch (`claude/early-integration`) that carries `main` plus the held feature PRs and takes `main` by merging it in, never by a rebase; `main` stays free for fixes and features, and releases are cut from `main` as before. **A hotfix while an early build is out** ships from `main` as the release the early build was heading to (`0.1.25`, when `0.1.25-early.n` is out) — an early Mac takes it, it's newer — and the next early build is `0.1.26-early.1`; `release.yml` refuses an early build of a version already released. **The pointer:** a pre-release tagged `early` (never "latest"), made by the first publish that finds none; every publish, early or a release, replaces its `manifest.txt` and `update.txt` with its own unless it already names a newer version. Nothing is uploaded by hand. **The app:** a file in its folder, `~/.shroom/channel`, holding `early` puts it on the early channel; the updater (`shroom_update::check_channel`) then reads the pointer and the latest release and takes the newer, so a release that is out is taken and a pointer that can't be read leaves the Mac on the official channel's. It is read at every check (no restart); `run.log` says `boot: channel early\|official` and each check names the channel; Copy diagnostics and reports say `shroom <version> (early channel)`. **The bootstrap:** `install.sh --early` installs from the pointer when it names a build newer than the latest release (else the latest release, saying so in one line: no early build out yet) and writes the file either way — the official 0.1.24 reads only latest, so the install line, not a release, moves a Mac onto the channel (`curl -fsSL https://shroom.am/install \| sh -s -- --early`); the plain install line is unchanged; removing the file leaves the channel (the next release above the running build is taken as usual). **Publishing one:** Actions → Release → Run workflow on the integration branch, Publish and Early ticked: CI green on it, the version `-early.n` (and only an early build's is), no release notes needed (What's new sends an unknown version to the newest release's), the same certificate and the same pin check — so Screen Recording carries over. **A space's page:** an early app deploys its page to its space as any release does (B50), so the space's viewers see the early build's page; a page only ever moves up, and an older official app on another Mac of the same space leaves it be (`wire::may_deploy_over`). No guard: the channel is opt-in, for the space's own owner, and a broken page is mended by the next build. **Accepted risk (Bagrat, 2026-10-18: no users yet):** an app from 0.1.24 or before compares pages by numbers alone, so it reads `0.1.25-early.n` as `0.1.25`; such an app is on the official channel and never reads the pointer, it leaves a newer-numbered page alone, and the installer installs fresh. macOS is handed `0.1.25-early.1` as the bundle's version string too. | Bagrat asked for the suffix, the integration branch keeps `main` moving, and the pointer keeps the app on two plain `releases/download` fetches like the official channel's (no API, no rate limit, no shroom server) | **Plain next numbers** (`0.1.25` early, `0.1.26` the release; this build's first proposal): no reader to change, but an early build looks like a release and burns a number — set aside for the suffix once Bagrat ruled there's no installed base to protect. **A fourth number** (`0.1.24.1`): every reader refuses four parts. **The GitHub API** (`/releases?per_page=1`, which lists pre-releases): 60 unauthenticated calls an hour per address, JSON to parse, and a second protocol beside the installer's. **A pointer on shroom.am or a branch file**: a push to `main` (a site deploy) or a commit by the workflow for every early build. **Shipping channel support in a release first**: a release to cut before the first early build can reach anyone; the install line moves a Mac today. **Early builds tagged from `main`**: the held features would have to merge first. **A defaults key** for the opt-in: the installer and a person can write a file in the app's own folder as easily, and its tests read it on Linux |
| B200 | **Voice cuts on the owner page: the strip, a press that saves at once, and the cuts' word in the status** (spec N65, provisional; Bagrat asked for voice cuts 2026-10-18 and tried the cue with his own voice the same day, relayed by the orchestrator; the same day he said to build everything now and decide the release later, relayed by the orchestrator; under his standing ruling for this work the build's calls are made on judgment and listed here for him to overturn) | **(a) The strip** (`owner-page-cuts`; canvas “The page — cut where you said “shroom cut””, “The page — a voice cut's row after Put back” and “The page — the unsure cut”): under the player on the owner page only (an owner-class browser or the video's maker, as the title, N40), a heading with the scissors, “Only you see this.”, then a row per voice cut in time order: its time (m:ss), what was said, one button. What was said sits between a few words of what plays around it, quiet and never struck (the planner's `leads` and `resumes`, up to six either side, “…” where more was said; Bagrat's go on the rows, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator, asked for it, drawn first on both boards), so the owner sees where each cut joins. An applied cut's words are struck through, with Put back; one put back reads plain, with Cut again; a cue with nothing said again after it shows the line before it and then the cue's own words, each struck while its cut holds, under a note and with Put back (see (i)). Hand cuts get no row: they're the editor's (N63). `cutsStrip(edits)`. **(b) A press saves at once:** `POST /_shroom/edits/<id> {cut, applied}` flips that one voice cut, by its id, in the record's edit list (`meta.edits`, N63's), by a read-modify-write under `If-Match` as the title (B21), and moves the list's `rev` on, so a final copy cut before it reads as stale and the page plays the original with the cuts skipped. Refused (403) without an owner-class cookie or the maker's, from another site, for a member's press on someone else's video and for a hand cut; 404 for an unknown cut or no list; 409 once removed. It answers what now plays (the trim less the applied cuts), which the page hands its player (`shroom:keep`); a press that doesn't save puts its row back and says “Couldn't save that…”. **(c) A page already open sees them land:** the status adds `cuts`, `"coming"` while a space that listens (N66: Cloudflare's AI bound to its page) hears a take that went live under five minutes ago and the Mac hasn't marked the plan (`edits.voice`, N63's), unless its words wait for the next free day; then a short tag of the cuts as they stand, which any press changes. The owner page polls on while cuts are coming, and on a new tag fetches the strip from `GET /_shroom/edits/<id>` (owner-class or the maker, no-store). **(d) Viewers get the cut video, nothing more:** no strip and no word that was cut on the share page, the embed or a member's view of someone else's video; the status names no word. **(e) Reviewed in both places** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator): the strip here for a quick check, and the editor's timeline (N63, the editor's build; canvas “EditorVoiceCuts”) for fine-tuning, the voice cuts as red parts, a cut put back dashed, to be cut again. Both read and write the one edit list (`meta.edits`) by cut id, so a Put back in either place shows in the other. **(f) The hint** (`owner-page-cue-hint`; canvas “The page — the voice-cut hint”; Bagrat OK'd its line 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator, with whichever cue word wins, B203): in a space that listens, the owner page of a take with no voice cuts and none coming carries one quiet line under the player, where the strip would be, with the scissors: “Slipped? Say “shroom cut”, then say the line again.” and Got it (proposed). It is rendered hidden and shown by the page itself unless this browser has put it away (Got it) or seen a strip of voice cuts, either of which puts it away for good there (`localStorage`, `shroom:cue-hint`; where that can't be kept, for the page view). Never beside the strip, while cues are coming, in a space that doesn't listen, or for a viewer. Tests: `worker/test/voice.test.js`. **(g) A cut changed in the editor** (canvas “The page — a voice cut changed in the editor”; Bagrat approved it as drawn, words and all, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator): once a voice cut's edges are moved in the editor (N63; the cut's `adjusted`, which #61's resize sets and nothing clears), the words it carries are the ones first heard and may no longer be what it cuts. Its row then gives how long it is and where it now ends — “4.6 s, to 0:09 — changed in the editor” — in place of its words and the words around it; the unsure cut's note stays above it, and Put back and Cut again work on it as before. Tests: `worker/test/voice.test.js`. **(h) One finishing line** (`uploading`, `hearing`; canvas “The page — finishing your video” and its moments and endings; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: one indicator on the owner page from the upload until the voice cuts land, “Finishing” his pick of its first word): the owner page's upload line says “Finishing your video — the link already works, share it anytime.” from go-live, with a small spinner in front of its words and no bar (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, on the board: a spinner in front of the text), turning while the take is finishing; the words keep no percent. A stalled upload still says “Waiting for connection — your link already works.”, its spinner resting, dimmed. While the status says the voice cuts are coming, the line stays after the upload, its spinner still turning; it fades once: when the cuts land (the strip, waiting empty above it, takes its place), when the status stops saying they're coming with none (the hint, now waiting hidden above it, shows while it's due), or when the wait's five minutes are up by the page's own count from the take's go-live (`listenFor`), whatever the status last said. In a space that doesn't listen it fades when the upload drains. B142's “Ready ✓” is retired on the page: one line, nothing after it. It never speaks of cuts: the same listening names the video and makes its captions and chapters (N66). The config popover keeps its own status line. Tests: `worker/test/voice.test.js`, `page.test.js`, and `npm run drive:page` (the line, then the strip; the line fading with no cuts, the hint in its place; and by its own count). **(i) The unsure cut** (canvas “The page — the unsure cut”; Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator, after a take of his own on the early build, whose strip offered the line before a cue with nothing said again after it and explained why: say instead that it wasn't clear how far back the slip went and ask to check, strike “shroom cut” too, cut the likely slip at once, and offer Put back in place of Cut it too): the line before such a cue is cut with it (B201 (c)), and its row shows the line and then the cue's own words, both struck, under “Not sure how far back the slip went — check this one.” in amber, with Put back. Put back brings the line back and leaves “shroom cut” out: the cue has a cut of its own, which stays, so viewers never hear it; the row then reads Cut again, the line plain and the cue still struck, the note kept. Putting the cue back too is the editor's. Bagrat picked both on the board (2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator): the note's words, of three ways, and Put back bringing the line back while the cue stays out, of two. A take planned before (the line offered, not cut) reads as one put back: the note, the line plain, Cut again. “Cut it too” and “Nothing was said again after it, so only “shroom cut” went. Cut the line before it too?” are retired from the strip; the editor's chip on a dashed suggestion says what a selection's does, Cut <its length> (N63; Bagrat, 2026-10-19). Tests: `worker/test/voice.test.js`, `npm run drive:page`. | The owner page is where the owner is after Stop, on any browser they use: the cuts are reviewed there, with no new window. A press that saves at once is the title field's way. The words stay in the record for the owner and never reach a viewer, N63's rule that viewers get only what plays. A plan with no cues still marks the list, so a page stops waiting as soon as the Mac is done. | **A review window in the app after Stop** (the stop popover's Review, as first proposed: a window per take, for what the page already opened at Stop can say). **The cuts listed for viewers** (the slip's words are the speaker's). **A switch of their own** (N65 (4): the space's listening is the one switch). **The Mac marking a take “listening” at its go-live** (one more write per take, and a list made before its length is known). **Waiting with no limit** (a Mac gone quiet would keep the page polling). |
