| B201 | **Voice cuts on the Mac: the take's words and its quiet, planned once, landed in its record** (spec N65, provisional; Bagrat asked for voice cuts 2026-10-18 and tried the cue with his own voice the same day, relayed by the orchestrator; the same day he said to build everything now and decide the release later, relayed by the orchestrator) | **(a) The words are the space's** (N66's `rec/<id>/ai/transcript.json`: Whisper turbo's `[text, start, end]` on the recording's clock, written once, whole): the Mac that made the take reads them over the take's own credential (`cred.json`), so nothing new goes up and nothing is heard twice. **(b) The quiet is the take's own,** read on the Mac from its single-file copy, in Rust (`shroom_encode::sound`, symphonia, the reader the editor's waveform uses, B195 (e); `quiet::silences`: under −38 dBFS for 0.12 s, the level the planner was tuned on): the bundled encoder carries no filter for it (B82), and adding one would change the shipped binary. On the same speech it matches ffmpeg's `silencedetect` to one constant 21 ms, the AAC encoder's delay, which the space's hearing carries too. The same pass reads the take's loudness every 10 ms (`quiet::read`): where no silence lies under an edge (a cut word run straight into a kept one), the edge goes to the latest step within 3 dB of the quietest between the two words, just before what follows, since word times drift and a cut word's tail must not play (Bagrat on the tester, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator; `crates/shroom-edit/tests/edges.rs`). Where no one silence covers the gap after a cut's last word, the cut runs through what still sounds there (the word's release, a breath) to the first pause beginning within a second of the word, and ends in it as in any silence: his third take (2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator) let a breath play after a cue whose cut had ended in the short dip right after the word. Measured (`dev/proposals/voice-cut/poc/joins.py --breath`, `poc/results/joins-breath-2026-10-18.json`), on stock-voice takes with a breath out after each cue and a breath in before the next line: as a browser hears them (its noise suppression leaves true silence between words, as his did), the breath out played after all 27 cut cues before (and in 7 more, the cue word's own end), after none of 33 after, and no breath in was cut before a line that plays; with a room's noise, whose silence already covers the gap, nothing changed; of the 287 cuts in the planner's 96 fixture takes, only his take's moved. Tests: `tests/tester_takes.rs` on his take rebuilt (`tests/takes/hand-bagrat-tester-3`). **(c) The planner** (`shroom-edit`'s `plan`, the proposal's: the cue by sound, mentions left alone, the retake lined up against the words before the cue, the edges in the quiet) names its cuts `v1`… in the order of their cues, each with up to six words either side for a review list (`leads`, added to the editor's `Cut` as the mirror of its `resumes`). Two rules came from Bagrat's reworded redo on the tester (2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: the right cut the default). A cue that breaks off a sentence takes the sentence with it, back to its start, when it began within 20 s and no restart was said again word for word (one that was still wins, so a restart mid-sentence keeps its sentence's start). A sentence ends cleanly only on a full stop, a question or an exclamation, never an ellipsis, and never on a word no sentence ends on (an article, “and”, “or”, “but”, “because”, “than”, “to”, “of”, “from”, “into”, “with”, a modal, a possessive), since Whisper puts a full stop wherever a speaker stops; words that often do end one (“it”, “that”, “on”, “in”) are left out, so no finished sentence reads as broken. His next two takes (2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator) added three more: “is”, “are”, “was” or “were” ends a sentence only after a pronoun (“that's what it is.”) or under a question word in its clause (“where your files are.”), so “…and your link is.” was broken off; the broken-off sentence begins after the nearest one that ended cleanly, with or without a pause after it, so a good sentence the speaker ran straight on from stays (it began after a sentence end with a pause, or a long pause, before); and a hesitation right before a restart (“Um, today I'll…”) goes with the cut. Tests: `crates/shroom-edit/tests/tester_takes.rs`, on his two takes rebuilt from his words (`tests/takes/hand-bagrat-tester-1`, `-2`). How far back a line said again in other words reaches is weighed above the planner (B202). When the sentence before a cue ended cleanly and nothing was said again after it, the line before goes with the cue (`line-before-cue`, low confidence, beside the cue's own `cue-only` cut), though how far back the slip went isn't clear, and the owner page asks to check it (B200 (i); Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: cut the likely slip at once rather than offer it). Tests: `tests/redo.rs`, `judge.rs`, `land.rs`, `tester_takes.rs` (his first take: the line he cut by hand on the tester now goes by itself). **`land`** puts them beside what the editor saved (hand cuts, the trim and the copy kept; N63's list laid over the record's `edits`, its fields this build doesn't know kept), sets the plan's mark (`edits.voice = {planned_at, cues}`, a take with no cue too), and moves the revision on only when what plays changes. A record already marked is never rewritten, so a cut put back stays put back. **(d) When:** `runtime/voice.rs` starts once the take is live and its single-file copy made (`uploader::publish`), beside the second-class files' upload, on its own thread. It asks the space once (`GET /_shroom/status/<id>`, `cuts: "coming"`, B200 (c)): a space that doesn't listen ends it unasked. Otherwise it reads the words after 3 s, the wait doubling to 15 s, for up to 15 minutes; a take still without them is marked waiting (`takes/<dir>/voice.json`), and the sweep looks again two minutes after launch and every six hours awake, for two days after its go-live. A take removed, baked (B197) or heard on a bake's clock, or whose record or list this build can't read, is left as it is. **(e) What a report shows** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: enough in the log after release to report a problem from daily use): each step of the lane leaves one run-log line under the take's short id, never a word said — a take planned leaves what was heard (the words' count and length, the looks, the quiet stretches or why its sound was unread, the cues, the cuts landed and how long landing took) and one line per cue (where, how many starts, the judgment's scores and time or why it didn't answer, each cut's id, kind, edges and whether it plays cut or is offered); a take not landed says why (`landing failed (status 403)`, `no words yet after 7 looks`, `left as it is (…)`); and the sweep, for the two days it looks, notes a press on the owner page since (`v2 put back`), from the record. About one line a take and one a cue, so a day of takes is a small part of a report's 1,000 lines (#64). Tests: `crates/shroom-edit/tests/land.rs`, `redo.rs` (the broken-off sentence, Bagrat's words among them), `crates/shroom-encode/tests/quiet.rs`, `crates/shroom-upload/tests/voice.rs` (the fake bucket, the record read back by the real Worker's strip and status; the lines a take planned, a judgment that didn't come, a landing refused once and a press on the page leave), `shroom-cloud`'s `cuts_word`. | The Mac holds the take's sound and the planner, and the space already hears the take for N66, so the words cost nothing more. Landing once, by the record's own mark, is what lets the owner's Put back hold. Reading the quiet in Rust keeps the shipped encoder as built. | **The Worker plans the cues** (it has the words but not the sound: the edges would hug the words, and the planner would live twice, once in JavaScript). **The Mac hears the take itself** (an on-device model to download, or a new permission for Apple's speech; N66 already hears it in the person's own account). **ffmpeg's `silencedetect`** (not in the bundled build, B82; adding it rebuilds the shipped binary). **A Clef gate on each cue** (the rules alone made no wrong cut across 140 transcripts and Bagrat's own take; full Clef stays the option the proposal measured). **A cut's end at the first dip after its word** (as before: a dip between a word's release and a breath is no pause). **A pause by an absolute level** (below −90 dB for 100 ms, as read off his take): a room's noise never gets that quiet, and the take's own silences already mark a pause, against its own floor in the tester and under −38 dBFS on the Mac. **Waiting with no end** (two days covers a free day used up, N66). |
| B202 | **Voice cuts: how far back a redo reaches, weighed by meaning on the space's own account** (spec N65 (2), provisional; Bagrat's reworded redo on the tester left a broken sentence, 2026-10-18, and he asked that the right cut be the default and that a line said again in other words be caught by meaning, chosen by measurement; the same day, that the code find the places and the judgment only weigh them, the code's own plan standing when it can't; both relayed by the orchestrator) | **(a) The code proposes** (`shroom-edit`'s `questions`): for each cue with words after it, the places behind it where the stretch it redoes might begin, nearest first — each sentence start, and each pause of 0.4 s or more (a gap between words, or a silence inside one) inside the cue's own sentence that leaves at least three of its words (fillers aside) before it, up to three sentences or 20 s back, at most six, never a stretch of fillers alone — each as the words from there to the cue, beside what was said after it (up to two sentences or 30 words). A finished sentence before the cue's own is redone whole or not at all, so nothing begins inside one, and no start strands a word or two of its sentence (his second take: “Today [pause] I'll show you…”). **(b) The judgment weighs them** (`POST /_shroom/api/cue`, WI §4.22): one call per cue to Clef (`@cf/cloudflare/clef`) on the space's own Cloudflare account, N66's binding; one yes-or-no question per start (is everything from there said again after the cue, in the same or other words?), answered as a score from 0 to 1. The words are its state, never its questions; it never names a time or a word. **(c) The code picks** (`JUDGED`, 0.4): the farthest start scored 0.4 or more, when the answer has one score per start, each from 0 to 1; anything else is refused. A pick only reaches farther back than the words alone would cut for sure, and makes the line before a cue with nothing said again in the same words a sure cut, with no note to check it (B200 (i)); the edges stay in the quiet. **(d) When it can't answer** (a space that doesn't listen, the free day used up, a busy model, no key for the space, past the hour's budget), the cue keeps the code's own plan (B201 (c)). The Mac asks once per question of a take's plan (`shroom_upload::voice::after_go_live_judged` and `look_again_judged`, through `Space::judge`); the route keeps nothing, and the app's log holds the scores, never the words. **(e) Who:** the take's maker or an owner-class Mac, by device key, never a browser; 120 a device an hour (`rate/<did>`'s `c`, beside B125's page keys), then 429. **Measured** (`dev/proposals/voice-cut/poc/judge.py` on the test account, every score in `poc/results/judge-2026-10-18b.json`; the first run, before (a)'s two rules, in `judge-2026-10-18.json`): 78 starts in 32 takes (the POC's seven scripts, Bagrat's words, three new scripts, in stock voices, and his two tester takes rebuilt). The 39 starts said again scored 0.17–0.95 (median 0.72), the 39 others at most 0.18 (median 0.04; before the two rules, at most 0.31); at 0.4 Clef takes 32 of the 39 and none of the 39, and the seven it leaves are redos the words already cut. With it, the five takes the code alone left short are cut whole (a line said again in other words and a two-sentence stretch, each in two voices, and his first take's “You press stop and the link is pasted.”, which he cut by hand), and no take is cut into a kept line. Tests: `crates/shroom-edit/tests/judge.rs`, `takes.rs` (each Workers AI take with the judgment it got, and with none), `land.rs`; `crates/shroom-upload/tests/voice.rs` (asked once; no answer leaves the code's plan); `worker/test/cue.test.js`; `shroom-cloud`'s `scores_of`. | Whisper writes what was said, and a redo in other words shares too few words to line up: meaning tells a redo from the next line, and the words alone can't. The code keeps every time and every edge, so a wrong score can't cut where no sentence started or nobody paused, and can only move a cut back over a stretch said again. Clef on the space's own account adds no service, no key and no new place the words go (N66 already hears the take there), for one small call per cue. | **Word overlap** (the share of a start's words said again after the cue), measured on the same 70 starts: with no wrong start taken it takes 27 of the 34 at best (at two thirds of the words), and misses Bagrat's own take, where a third came back. **Clef names the start** (a time or a word it names could be anywhere; the code's places and its check keep it to where a stretch can begin). **Each stretch between two starts weighed on its own**, the cut reaching back over a run judged said again (proposed after his second take): on that take rebuilt, Clef already scored the stretches reaching over his good sentence 0.05 and 0.31; the cut came from the words' own rule, which (a) and B201 (c) now fix. **The nearest start above the threshold** (a two-sentence stretch said again would keep its first sentence). **A threshold of 0.3** (takes a wrong start, at 0.31) **or 0.6** (leaves the first sentence of a two-sentence stretch, at 0.51). **Clef-flash** (it missed spoken cut cues in the AI build's measurements). **A gate on each cue** (B201: the rules alone made no wrong cut). **A model on the Mac** (a download, and the words are already in the space). |
| B203 | **Voice cuts: what to say — “shroom cut” only; plain “cut” and a relaxed set measured and set aside for now** (spec N65 (1), provisional; Bagrat asked to try plain “cut” with Clef as the gate, and more relaxed phrases, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator; the same day he approved the first-use hint and the privacy line, “whichever cue word wins”, and then picked “shroom cut” only, both relayed by the orchestrator) | **(a) Built, never set by the app** (`shroom-edit`'s `CueWords`): `Shroom` (as shipped: “shroom cut”), `Plain` (“cut” too) and `Relaxed` (also “cut here”, “cut that”, “cut cut”, “scratch that”, “redo”, “let me redo that”, “let me say that again”). Any phrase but “shroom cut” is proposed only when said apart from the speech (after a sentence's end, a comma or a 0.25 s pause, and before a sentence's end or such a pause), the longest that fits; the judgment then says whether the speaker meant it for whoever edits the video (`intents`, `MEANT` 0.5), asked to answer no for what a viewer is meant to hear (a button, a menu item, a key, a command, an action described). With no answer, only “shroom cut” counts. On the tester as “Listen for”, kept there for experiments. **(b) Measured** (`dev/proposals/voice-cut/poc/cues.py`, stock voices on the test account; `poc/results/cues-2026-10-18-first.json`, `-sharp.json`): said apart, no phrase was proposed in the 93 transcripts the planner is tested on; of 16 cue takes Whisper wrote 13, the three dropped being a lone one-word cue (“Cut.”, “Cut, cut.”, “Redo.”) in one voice of two, primed or not; of 7 phrases said apart but meant for the viewer, the sharp question let in none at any bar from 0.3 to 0.7 (at most 0.29), while every cue Whisper wrote scored 0.81–0.98. **(c) Recommended:** teach “shroom cut” (the hint, the privacy line): it works when the judgment can't answer and needs none, and Whisper wrote it in every take; let the relaxed set ride behind the judgment if he wants the leeway, one small Clef call per phrase proposed. **(d) Ruled** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator): “shroom cut” only, as built, and no second kind of question on the cue route (`POST /_shroom/api/cue` weighs how far back a redo reaches, B202, and nothing else). | A person says the cue mid-take, so it must be heard every time and never fire on its own; “shroom cut” is both, and “said apart” plus a judgment that names what viewers hear keeps the others honest. | **The judgment on every “cut”, said apart or not** (“cut the start”, “the voice cut” are everywhere in speech about video). **The question as first asked** (4 of 7 phrases meant for the viewer let in, at any bar). **Plain “cut” as the cue taught** (Whisper dropped it alone in one voice of two; a short lone word reads to it as a disfluency). **Priming Whisper with the mode's words** (it brought back none of the dropped one-word cues). **Plain “cut” or the relaxed set, for now** (Bagrat, 2026-10-18: “shroom cut” only). |
| B221 | **Edit in shroom: the owner's page opens its video in shroom's editor** (spec N69, ratified as built by Bagrat on 2026-10-24, with its four lines; Bagrat asked for it 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator, and approved the boards “The page — Edit in shroom” and “Editor — opened from the page's Edit” as drawn, with their lines, option A and the tooltip “Edit in shroom”; the build's calls below are made on judgment and listed for him to overturn) | **(a) The link** is `shroom://edit/<host>/<id>`: the page's own host and the video's id, beside the invite's `shroom://join/<host>/<code>`, which the release bundle already claims (B66). It carries nothing secret: any page can make one, so it only asks, the browser asks first, and the Mac acts within its own scope only. **(b) The page** draws it in the owner page's title row, the last quiet icon after Download: a plain link (no script), the scissors, “Edit in shroom” as its tooltip and its name. It renders only on the owner page (the browsers that see the title field) and only once the upload has drained, as Edit… needs a live row; the open page's poll brings it in at the drain. A recipient's page and the embed never have it. There's no test for an app that takes the link (option A). **(c) The Mac** reads the link where it reads the invite's and posts `EditLink { host, rec }` to the core, which decides in this order: not set up — the welcome window, as a tray click; a take open — held (one; a later press replaces it) and acted on once the take ends (law 4); another space's host — the popover's line; the editor open — forward as it is, with its line when it holds another video (as Edit… in the Library does too: found saying nothing there, 2026-10-19); a live row in this Mac's Library — the editor, by Edit…'s own path; no such row — one Library refresh, then the same check, and still none: on an owner-class Mac (which lists every video) “That video isn't in your space anymore.”, on a member's (which lists only theirs) “Only whoever recorded this video, or the space's owner, can edit it.” A refresh that fails (offline) opens nothing and logs it. **(d) The lines**: the popover opens unasked (the app you were in keeps the keyboard), the line said once in the status line, first in its order as what just happened, until the popover closes; the editor's line takes the footer line's place until the next change. **(e) The run log** says each answer in words of its own and never the link, the host or the video (B199 (t)). | The boards above; the invite page's Open shroom, the precedent for a page that opens the app; the Library's Edit… and its rules (B195 (a)) | A web editor on the page (B195); a test for whether an app takes the link, or a line after a press that seems to do nothing (option B, not chosen: browsers can't tell, and a timer would fire behind their own question); swapping the editor's video under unsaved changes, or asking first; a window of its own for the Mac's answers (the popover's status line is where a fact lands, law 7); asking the space about one video by a new address (the Library's refresh is the door it already has) |
| B222 | **The hero goes live: shroom, recorded with shroom, in the landing's box; the walkthrough behind a button** (spec N70; Bagrat approved the board “shroom.am — the voice-cut hero takes the box”, 2026-10-18, and asked for it to go live, 2026-10-19) | **The page:** `site/index.html` is what waited at the hidden `/next` (#69, its still from #71), now at `/`: indexed and counted like the landing before it. **The hero** `site/hero-2.mp4` (2:05, 1920×1080, H.264 High 4:2:0 + AAC, 8.5 MB, `+faststart`; first published as High 4:4:4, which Safari and Chrome won't play, so the re-encode took a new address instead of waiting out caches; `landing.test.mjs` now reads each video's profile) is copied by `site/build.mjs` to `/_/hero-2.mp4` and answered at `/hero-2.mp4` by `site/worker.js` (named in `run_worker_first`) exactly as `/how-to.mp4` is (B194): whole or in the bytes a `Range` asks for, not counted. Its address is set as the block nears the screen, `preload="metadata"`, and a click on the still plays it in place (B190's script). Its still `site/hero-poster.webp` (1280×832, 47 KB) is the editor after the take's cuts with his bubble and the cut row. The button reads “Play shroom, recorded with shroom, 2 minutes”; the length “2 min”. **The walkthrough** opens from the Setup walkthrough button in a `<dialog>` lightbox, its address set on first open; opening it pauses the hero. **`/next`** answers a permanent redirect to `/`, uncounted, so a kept link still lands; `site/next.html` and its test are gone, their checks folded into `site/test/landing.test.mjs`. Tests: `site/test/landing.test.mjs` (the hero's label, length, still, own file, no remote address, the walkthrough's button and lightbox, both videos under the 25 MiB limit), `site/test/worker.test.mjs` (both videos whole, in ranges, 416, HEAD, uncounted; `/next` a 301 to `/`) | The board he approved, as drawn; the hero is the same file kind and size class as the how-to, so it rides the same serving, ranges and limits with nothing new | Leaving `/next` to 404 (a link he may have shared would break); keeping both videos in the box (the board puts one hero there); a new storage place for the hero (B194's reasons hold) |
| B223 | **Closing the editor with changes not saved asks: Save, Keep editing or Discard** (spec N71, **ratified by Bagrat as drawn, 2026-10-19**; Bagrat asked 2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator, that ✕ stop saving without a word; on 2026-10-19 he approved the board “Editor — closing with changes not saved” as drawn, and ruled that Quit and Update save first, as now, without asking, both relayed by the orchestrator; the build's calls below are made on judgment and listed for him to overturn) | **(a) The question.** ✕ while the editor holds changes not saved (`editor`, or `editor-save-failed`: changes a save didn't take) asks in the footer's place, as “Delete the original?” does (B197 (b)): “Save your edit?”, “What you've changed isn't saved yet.”, then Discard (red outline), Keep editing and Save (filled). The window stays open while it asks (`EditorFields::closing`). **(b) The keys.** Return is Save and Esc is Keep editing; Discard is a click only, never Return (law 9). **(c) While it asks** the edit is held: no step is taken (Undo, Redo, a drag or Revert is dropped), the preview pauses, and ✕ again leaves the question as it is. **(d) The answers.** Save is Done: the save, then the final video, as after Done. Keep editing puts the question away with every change still there, back to the face it asked from. Discard closes the window and drops what wasn't saved: nothing is written, and the video stays as it was last saved. **(e) Unchanged**: ✕ with nothing to save closes at once; while the edit saves, ✕ waits; after Done, ✕ is Close (the final video still made, the original kept two days); a refused save closes; Done saves without asking. Quit and Update save first without asking, from the question too (Bagrat, 2026-10-19). Deleting the video, or leaving the space, closes the window as before. **(f) The run log** names the question and each answer: `editor-close-asked`, `editor-close-kept`, `editor-discarded`, and Save as `editor-saving`. | The board “Editor — closing with changes not saved”; “Delete the original?”, the card it reuses (B197 (b)); macOS's document windows, which ask before closing unsaved work | A sheet or a dialog over the window (the editor's questions sit in the footer's place, B197 (b); a sheet since B238, 2026-10-25); Discard on Return (law 9); asking on Quit and Update (Bagrat, 2026-10-19: save first, as now); asking on Done (Done is the save) |
| B224 | **Cloudflare's Web Analytics stays on shroom.am** (Bagrat, 2026-10-19: keep it) | The zone's Web Analytics, a setting in Cloudflare's dashboard and not in this repo, adds its beacon (`static.cloudflareinsights.com/beacon.min.js`) to each HTML page as Cloudflare serves it; found on the live landing the day the hero went live (B222). It sets no cookies and keeps nothing that names a visitor, so the footer's “no cookies, and nothing that tells us who you are” and the privacy page's “Cloudflare's Web Analytics also counts visits on our behalf, without cookies” already say what's true; only `site/index.html`'s comment, which said nothing loads from anyone else, and `site/test/landing.test.mjs`'s notes changed to name the exception. The landing's test still holds the file in the repo to loading nothing remote | Its counts sit beside the site's own (`site/worker.js`'s `STATS`) at no cost and with nothing in the repo | Turning it off (his call: keep); loading it from the page ourselves (it would put a third-party script in the repo for what the edge already does) |
| B225 | **`shroom edit`: the command saves an edit itself; the app makes its final video** (spec N72, **ratified by Bagrat as built, 2026-10-19**; the orchestrator, 2026-10-19: build `shroom edit` now the editor has shipped, its words provisional for Bagrat's next look) | **(a) The save is the command's**, through the door a rename goes (B215): a `manage` credential for that one video, the record read, the change checked by the editor's own `shroom_edit::edit::check` on the list the record holds (made safe against its `durationSec`), and saved by `library::save_edits_over` as the editor's Done saves it, guarded by what the record played when read (`Plays`): an edit that became the video meanwhile refuses it (`refused`, nothing written). The last save wins, as between two editors (B195). **(b) Two socket asks**, answered whatever the recording switch says (an edit records nothing): `edit.check {id}` before any change — `editor_open` while that video is open in an editor window on this Mac, so that window's Done never silently undoes the command's change; a Mac whose app isn't running has no editor open, and the check is skipped — and `edit.copy {id, rev}` after a change that leaves something out: the final video of that revision owed (`library_ops.copies`), cut as after Done, one at a time and never over a take, a newer revision already owed kept. The app is opened for the copy as `record start` opens it (`--no-launch` leaves it); not there, the answer is `"final": "later"`, and `shroom edit <video> finish` asks again. The socket reads a recording id's shape only; the run log names it by its short id. **(c) The surface:** `shroom edit <video>` (what plays, the ends, each cut by its id — `m1`…, `v1`…, or its number in a list from before ids), `trim <in> <out>`, `cut <start> <end>`, `put-back <cut>`, `apply <cut>`, `resize <cut> <start> <end>`, `revert`, `finish`; times in seconds or minutes:seconds on the recording; `--dry-run`. A refusal is `cant_edit` (exit 12) with the editor's reason in `reason` (`out_of_range`, `leaves_too_little`, `too_short`, `nothing_changes`, `no_such_cut`, or `editor_open`). It waits while a take is open (law 4). No “Delete the original?”: the original is kept two days after the final video lands, then goes (B197's Keep for 2 days). Tests: `crates/shroom-cli/tests/edit.rs` (against the real Worker), `crates/shroom-core/tests/cli.rs` | The save needs no app and no window: the editor's Done is a record write the device key can already make, and the link plays the list at once. The re-encode is the Mac's encoder and the app's lane, so only the app can make the final video | **Every change through the app** (a new editor command without a window): more core plumbing, and an edit would need the app running. **`status.json` naming the video the editor holds**: the seam never carries a link's part (law 13, ARCHITECTURE §11.2). **Making the final video at the app's next launch for any edit the command saved**: the upkeep makes a missing copy only of a video on this Mac (`library::upkeep_plan`), never downloading one unasked; widening it would fetch every such video at every launch |
| B226 | **A pause, resume or stop under way has a minute, not 5 s, before it reads as a capture that died** (in-frame; the hosted Mac's E2E on 2026-10-19, at load 27 with OBS at 100% CPU, took 15.3 s to end a take for a pause, and the core called the capture dead 5 s in, keeping the take as an interrupted one; the orchestrator asked for the fix, 2026-10-19) | `Op::deadline_ms`: `RecPause`, `RecResume` and `RecFinalize` 60 s; `RecStart` and `Prime` stay 5 s. Nothing anyone sees or reads changes: the popover already waits for a pause to be confirmed, and a stop already shows its own wait; a slow one now waits longer instead of losing the take. A recorder that never answers still ends as an interrupted take, a minute in. The running take's own watchdog (`STALL_AFTER`, 5 s without a frame) is untouched. Tests: `region3.rs` (a pause and a stop confirmed 15 s late land; a pause never answered ends interrupted at 60 s). | Ending a take for a pause joins the frame writer, which drains its queue into ffmpeg (`Recording::end_take`): a starved encoder makes that slow, not dead, and the recorder answers every command in order, so a late answer is the normal answer. 60 s matches the cloud calls' and is four times the slowest seen. A start keeps 5 s: a late start is already kept as a saved take (`started-unasked`), so it loses nothing. | **A late `Paused` taken as success after the deadline**: by then the take is abandoned and the recorder told to end it, so it would mean not abandoning, which is this. **A keep-alive from the recorder while it drains**: a timer thread beside the blocking join, through the record crate and the app, for the same outcome as a ceiling. **No deadline for these steps**: a recorder that hung would leave the take open forever, Resume and Stop refused. **A longer start deadline too**: nothing to save. |
| B227 | **One editor window per video** (spec N73, **ratified by Bagrat as drawn, 2026-10-19**; amends B195 (a) and B221 (c); Bagrat asked 2026-10-19, relayed by the orchestrator, whether each video could get an editor window of its own, and the same day chose the board “Editor — one window per video”, to be built after the close prompt as its own change, relayed by the orchestrator; the build's calls below are made on judgment and listed for him to overturn) | **(a) One editor per video, each under its own number.** The core keeps every open editor (`Fields::editors`) under the number its window got as it opened (`EditorId`, never given again while shroom runs). Its window, its surface and the lane's work for it carry that number (`WindowKind::Editor(id)`, `Surface::Editor(id)`, `Op::EditorOpen(id)`, `Op::EditorSave(id)`), so each window's open, save, deadline and failure are its own. What the lane says back still names the video: there is one window per video. **(b) Edit on a video.** Its window open, from Edit… or the page: that window comes forward as it is (`editor-forward`, `edit-link-forward`). Otherwise a window of its own opens for it, in front, by Edit…'s path (`open-editor`, `edit-link-open`). While a take is open nothing new opens (law 4): Edit… greys, and the page's press waits, as before. **(c) The windows.** Each is a webview of its own, labelled `editor-<number>`. A window's clicks, keys, steps and scene are its own editor's, by that label, whatever its webview says, and `shroom-media://` gives a window only its own video and its peaks. A new window opens as the first does (B199 (e)), then AppKit's own cascade puts it down and to the right of the newest editor window open, as the Mac stacks documents, as far as its screen has room. A window that closes is destroyed, not kept hidden for the next. **(d) One at a time, where it was.** The final videos are still made one at a time on the lane's copy thread, in the order they're owed; a window whose turn hasn't come reads “Making the final video… 0%”. The upkeep waits until every window has closed, and the cleanup sweep while any is open, so neither bakes a video a window holds. **(e) Quit and Update** save every window's changes not saved, as its Done does, and bring those windows forward. What was asked waits (`Fields::leaving`) while any window saves, and goes on once none does. A save that doesn't land drops it: shroom stays, and that window says why. ⌘Q with an editor window in front is that window's ✕, which asks with changes not saved (N71). **(f) Closing.** Deleting a video closes its window only; leaving the space closes them all, stopping what each open asked for. An update's relaunch never brings an editor window back: its steps lived in the process that quit. | Bagrat's ask and the board; macOS's document windows, each its own, none waiting on another; the lane already ran each open on a thread of its own (B199 (b)) | The one window switching to the other video, asked or not (the board “Editor — Edit on another video”: what changed in it would have to be saved or dropped first); tabs in one window (new on screen, and not how a Mac keeps documents apart); a limit on how many open (each holds only its video's file on this Mac and its steps); keying a window by its video's id (the key is copied all through the core, and an id is text: with one window per video, the window's number is as good a key); keeping a closed window hidden for reuse, as the one was (a webview and its player for every video opened since launch); a lane per window (saves are quick, the opens already have a thread each, and the encoder is one) |
| B228 | **Our own traffic carries a tag; installs and updates are counted through shroom.am** (in-frame; Bagrat asked 2026-10-19, relayed by the orchestrator, that our own tests be marked so the stats can leave them out, and that updates be counted and told apart from ours) | **(a) The mark is the link tag the Worker already keeps** (`?ref=`, blob 6): `ci` on every fetch a GitHub Actions job makes of shroom.am (`site.yml`'s check that the deploy serves this commit, the one source of most of the untagged US `/install` fetches), `test` on anything else of ours (a session by hand, a live drive, the hosted Mac by hand; the `shroom-cloud` skill says so). An untagged fetch counts as a person's. **(b) `/download/v<version>/<file>`** on shroom.am answers a `302` to that file in the GitHub releases, `no-store`, and counts one row: the page row's six blobs (answer `redirect`) plus `via` (`install`, `update`, or empty for anything else), the version and the file name — the same for everyone who gets that release. Only a version in the release shapes (`1.2.3`, `1.2.3-early.4`) and a plain file name pass, so it can only ever send someone to one of our releases' files; anything else is a 404. HEAD isn't counted. Nothing in it names a person or ties two requests together. **(c) The collector** (`dev/stats/collect.mjs`) leaves `ci` and `test` out of every count by default, keeps them as `ours` (the funnel's shape), and reads `/download` by day, country and version (`downloads`), ours as a line of their own; the dashboard shows installs and updates beside the funnel. **(d) What downloads through it.** The installer's zip, from the public releases: `$DOWNLOADS/v<version>/<file>?via=install` (`DOWNLOADS` is shroom.am's; a test's own releases are fetched where they are), with `&ref=$SHROOM_REF` when that is a tag's shape; `mac.yml` sets `SHROOM_REF=ci` for the hosted Mac's installer runs. No fallback: the installer was just fetched from shroom.am. The app's updater (`shroom_update::sources`): from the public releases, shroom.am's `…?via=update` first, then GitHub's own address when it doesn't answer (15 s to connect) or gives something that doesn't match the fingerprint; a dev build's stand-in, only where it is. Nothing else rides along: no ID, and not the version running. The privacy page and the FAQ say so, in words Bagrat approved as drafted (Bagrat, 2026-10-19). Tests: `site/test/worker.test.mjs` (a release's download), `dev/stats/collect.test.mjs` (the tags, `downloads`), `installer/test/install.test.mjs` (the download, counted), `crates/shroom-update` (`a_public_update_is_counted_on_its_way_and_a_stand_ins_never`, `an_update_goes_on_when_the_counter_is_down_or_wrong`) | The tag is already counted and validated, costs no new data, and rides on any URL; GitHub's per-asset totals can't say when, where, which version or whose, and a query string on GitHub's URL isn't counted apart (B136) | **A distinct client kind** (a test User-Agent): curl's and the updater's User-Agents can't be set from outside without the installer's help, and a browser's can't at all. **A `.curlrc` in `CURL_HOME`** setting the User-Agent for the installer's own curls: invisible magic, and nothing for the updater. **The network the request came from** (GitHub's runners are Azure's): new data about every visitor, a person's provider included. **Routing the manifest (and the app's checks) through shroom.am too**: a check every few hours per running copy for numbers the downloads already give, and the installer's first fetch made to depend on the site twice. **A per-release counter file** (a second zip per kind): doubles every release. **A ping after install or update**: telemetry, parked ([PRINCIPLES](PRINCIPLES.md)). **The Homebrew cask through shroom.am**: its tap is its own repo, and Homebrew's audit wants the download's host to match; brew installs stay in GitHub's totals |
| B229 | **The Library and Settings close for good; no memory hand-back after a take** (in-frame; Bagrat asked 2026-10-21, relayed by the orchestrator, for two fixes from an earlier memory study (debug build, Activity Monitor: 60 MB at rest, 110 MB after Settings opened and closed, 176 MB idle after a take), measured before and after on the hosted Mac; B106's overlays kept as they are, a native countdown out of scope) | **(a) The Library and Settings are destroyed when they close**, as the editor windows are (N73): `presenter::closes_for_good`. The setup window and Send feedback are still hidden. **(b) Nothing opens either without a click.** A page made late brings shroom forward (B106), so a window made afresh may only appear when the person reached for it. The core's tests hold it as a law at every step of every walk (`common/laws.rs`): the Library or Settings appears only when its door is asked, or at a launch (the update relaunch's restore, N41). `a_closed_library_or_settings_comes_back_only_when_reached_for` (region5) covers both ways in that need no fresh click: asked mid-take and shown as the take ends (law 4), and nothing reopening either once it's closed. A mutation (an unasked door inserted mid-run) trips the law. **(c) The measure.** The take drive `memory` reads each process's physical footprint (Activity Monitor's Memory column, `proc_pid_rusage`, with `top` beside it as a cross-check) for shroom and the WebKit processes started since it. It reads at rest, after the Library and then Settings opened and closed (20 s to settle), and a minute after a 30-second take finishes uploading to the fake. The hosted Mac's `memory` scenario runs it in a release-profile build (`tauri build` without `--debug`, harness built in), first as before (a harness-only `memory-as-before` file in the home keeps the windows hidden), then as built, on one Mac. **Run 39564004595, before → after, MB:** at rest 65.5 → 66.7 (5 processes each); after the Library 94.3 → 75.1 (6 → 5); after Settings 119.6 → 74.6 (7 → 5); idle after a take 123.1 → 78.5 (7 → 5). **(d) No hand-back after a take.** Built and measured, then dropped: `malloc_zone_pressure_relief` over every zone, once at the upload's end and once debounced to 30 s after the last take or upload, handed back 0 MB. Read a minute after the take, shroom's own process is back to about 27 MB without it. The study's ~40 MB was shroom read too soon after the take (at 20 s it still held about 54 MB, with or without a hand-back) | Each hidden window kept a web page process, and the Library's and Settings' pages held about 20 and 24 MB, plus a few MB in shroom, for the rest of the run. Opening one again costs a page load (the editor's already do), against about 45 MB and two processes for as long as shroom runs. The law makes B106's bring-forward impossible to bring back without a red test. The measure is a release-like build because a debug build's own footprint isn't what anyone runs | Hiding as before (the 45 MB stays); closing the setup window and Send feedback too (rarely open, and not what was measured); keeping the hand-back anyway (code that frees nothing measurable, with a lock walk over every zone each time); a hand-back on a timer (the same nothing, more often); measuring the debug build only (the earlier study's numbers didn't hold in release); reading memory as resident size (counts shared pages in every process; Activity Monitor shows footprint) |
| B230 | **The space's name is the owner page's title, not its placeholder** (amends B209 (c); Bagrat's bug report, 2026-10-24, relayed by the orchestrator: after the AI named a new video, its title stayed an empty, focused box with the name greyed in it, and a click put the caret at the start with no way to move it; he asked that the name become the accepted title, shown plain, with only a click opening the edit box) | **The field holds the name as its value.** A video the space named (not typed over) renders with the name in the field and no `autofocus`, so the page's own style draws it as plain text until it's clicked; the placeholder stays the dated “Untitled — Oct 2”. The page's save takes the name as what's saved, so an untouched field writes nothing and the name stays the space's (`titled` false: the early name can still give way to the full one, and Copy thumbnail still draws only a typed title). **A name arriving while the page is open** (`GET /_shroom/named/<id>`) goes in the same way when the field still holds what it was given: the field lets go of the keyboard if the page had opened in it, or, if the owner clicked in before a key, takes the name selected, ready to retype. A field being typed in, or one already saved from, is left be (a typed title wins, as N35). The page moves to 0.1.28 (B61). | **Proved by:** `worker/test/ai.test.js` › the page (B209) — the field as drawn, and the naming poll run against a stand-in field (arriving untouched, early then full, mid-typing, clicked before a key); the Worker suite. | Keeping the placeholder and only dropping `autofocus` (the field would still be an empty box and its caret still stuck, the placeholder not being text); writing the name as a typed title from the page (it would stop the full name replacing the early one and put an unseen name on Copy thumbnail, B209 (c)) |
| B231 | **The setup's waits say what's happening, and wiring finishes on its own** (spec N74; a tester's finding on a fresh Cloudflare account, 2026-10-19: the card step and the last step looked stuck; the boards “Setup — the card step shows its check as it runs…” and “Setup — the last step shows each part of wiring as it lands…” ratified by Bagrat as drawn, 2026-10-25, relayed by the orchestrator; the build's calls below are made on judgment, for him to overturn) | **(a) Parts are said as they begin.** The wizard's lane posts `CloudNews::Part { op, part }` before each part's first call: the probe pass's token proof, the email gate's throwaway write, R2's list (the card gate), the look around (`sweep::email_gate` and `sweep::card_gate` split out of `gates`); wiring's storage, page, name (before the route is switched on and the page awaited), the self-check, then the enroll (`wire::wire_parts`, `wire_with` its quiet form). A part is ticked only when the next one begins, so nothing is ticked that hasn't answered. Each part is logged with its time (`wizard sweep: part n`, `wizard wire: part n`), so a full e2e run's log measures every part. **(b) Never stored (law 1).** `sweep.check` is the part underway of this pass, gone when the pass answers, and a pass begun again starts over. `sweep.wiring` keeps the furthest part and when it began: a retry re-runs the idempotent parts before it in a blink, so they stay ticked and the clock counts from the first try; gone when wiring answers. **(c) Clocks come from the core**, as lengths (`copy::length`): a spinning part's from its first second, the card watch's from the first Add credit card, the working row's in its aside. The window wakes each second while one runs; the webview composes nothing (the dot before the row's clock is drawn by CSS). **(d) The long lines** at 45 s after Add credit card (the card counted ~25–30 s after it landed in the stranger runs) and at 3 min on the name (~3.5 min measured on a fresh name). **(e) Wiring needs no window.** `due_wire` fires while a token is saved, the name row has ticked and no space is set up, window open or not (never on the way out of a relaunch); with the window closed `due_sweep` runs one full pass a process, so a launch (the login item's included) knows where the setup stands. A gate still up waits for the window: no poll, no wiring. It lands opening nothing. **(f) No done form for a run's last part**: the step moves on the moment it answers, so the boards' “Looked for your link name and any earlier space” and “This Mac is in your space” never show and aren't lines. The forms the boards didn't draw (a part still to come, one underway that the board drew done, the working row's other parts) are provisional. | Measured from the last four full e2e runs on the test account (2026-10-19): the check took 2.5–5.7 s and wiring 7.8–10.6 s (that account's name long switched on). Core tests: `onboarding.rs` (the check's parts, the card watch, wiring's parts and clock across a retry, the working row, wiring with the window closed and after a quit); `wizard_fake.rs::wiring_says_its_parts_in_order`. The setup gallery's new faces (`card-check`, `gate-card-watching`, `gate-card-long`, `wiring-parts`, `wiring-long`, `done-wiring-part`). | A progress bar or a percentage (the waits have no honest length: the name can take ten minutes); a part ticked on a timer rather than its own answer; the parts stored to disk so a relaunch resumes them (law 1: the launch's pass says where things stand); polling the card gate with the window closed (a stopped setup is the person's to resume); a notification when wiring lands with the window closed (no dialog to an empty chair) |
| B232 | **Auto-cleanup's deletes run in the space's own page, on its one schedule** (spec N75; Bagrat, 2026-10-24, relayed by the orchestrator: off by default; Off / 15 / 30 / 60 days from the last watch, or from the upload if never played; a pinned video is never deleted; a warning a few days ahead with a one-tap Pin; and the page, not a Mac, does the deleting, so a closed Mac never misses one and the bill never grows for it) | **(a) The setting** is `.shroom/autoclean.json` = `{v, days: 0\|15\|30\|60, at, since}` in the space's bucket. The Mac that holds the setup key sets it from Settings · General (`POST /_shroom/api/autoclean`, owner-class), because turning it on gives the page its schedule, the way turning AI on does (B206 a). Every Mac reads it (`GET`, device key; a member sees their own videos going). It needs Count plays: with counting off, nothing is due and nothing goes. **(b) One schedule, shared.** The page keeps the single Cron Trigger B206 already uses, every minute, wired while AI *or* auto-cleanup is on: `wire::set_schedule` puts or takes it, and `set_ai` keeps it while auto-cleanup is on. Turning auto-cleanup on wires the schedule first, then tells the page the days; turning both off takes it away. **(c) What's due.** A video is due `days` after its last counted play (the owner's own never count, B210), or after its upload if never played, and never before counting began. The page keeps `.shroom/autoclean/state.json` = `{v, scanAt, cursor, going: {<id>: {warnedAt, deletesAt}}, last}`. Once a day it walks the records ten at a time, one step a scheduled run, listing each live, unpinned video due within three days with `deletesAt` the later of its due date and `warnedAt` + 3 days, and dropping any that were watched, pinned or deleted since. So nothing goes without three days' warning, also when it's turned on or shortened (`since` restarts the clock). `POST /_shroom/api/cleanup` gains `deletesAt` per item and `autoclean: {days, last}`, which the Library, the popover and the command read. **(d) The delete.** A run that doesn't walk looks at up to three due videos, reading each fresh, and deletes at most one. It skips a video that is pinned (`keep`), isn't live or hasn't finished uploading, has a fresh editing mark (`.shroom/editing/<id>.json` = `{until}`, from `POST /_shroom/api/editing`, written by the Mac with its editor open every 5 minutes and good for 15), or was played since `warnedAt`. A skipped video is looked at again later. The delete goes exactly as a Delete does: the tombstone first (with `by: "auto"`), then the bytes under `rec/<id>/`, then the seal (`gone/<id>`, B125), then its plays record; the round's ids and bytes go in `last`, which each Mac says once. **(e) Timing:** on the date, or within a day of it. **(f) Cloudflare's limits** (Workers limits page, read 2026-10-24; free plan): 5 Cron Triggers per account, 10 ms CPU and 50 subrequests (R2 calls included) per scheduled run. The shared trigger is one of the five; a delete is about 5–10 R2 calls; the walk's ten records keep a run inside 10 ms. **Proved by:** `worker/test/autoclean.test.js` (the setting, the walk, the warning, each guard, the delete, the round); `crates/shroom-cloud/tests/ai_fake.rs` › `auto_cleanup_shares_naming_s_one_schedule…`; WORKER-INTERFACE §4.24. | A closed Mac can't delete, so the page has to; the one trigger AI already has costs nothing more, and a step a run keeps inside the free plan's CPU and subrequests | **The token Mac's cleanup sweep** (B91, every 6 h of awake time): a closed laptop misses the date and the space keeps paying for what the person asked to have gone, and a member's videos would wait on the owner's Mac. **A second Cron Trigger for auto-cleanup alone**: another of the free account's five, for no gain. **Scanning every record each run**: past 10 ms and 50 subrequests on a real space. **R2 lifecycle rules**: they expire by an object's age, not by the last watch, and can't skip a pinned video |
| B233 | **Pin is the record's `keep`; Delete all is one sheet and one Undo** (spec N75 (1)–(2); Bagrat approved the boards as drawn, 2026-10-24) | **(a) Pin** writes the record's `keep` (`shroom_upload::library::set_pinned`, under the token Mac's pair or a `manage` credential, as a rename) — N68's Keep, renamed so one idea has one name. The row reads pinned at once while the write runs; a refresh begun before it landed doesn't undo it (`LibraryOps.pinning`), and a write that fails puts the row back and says so above the rows (law 12). A pinned video is never suggested, whatever a stale read says, and the page's walk never lists it. **(b) Delete all…** asks with its own sheet (the count, and what it frees), then runs each delete as a single Delete does and holds them all under one Undo line for a single delete's few seconds (`LibraryFields.undo`); a video pinned elsewhere since the list was read is skipped. **(c) The going rows.** What auto-cleanup will delete leads Suggested to clean up under its own heading, each row's date apart in amber with a Pin, and the Library's amber line above the rows says how many and when. **(d) The popover's banner** ranks after Let shroom record and before an update; Pin both pins all of them, Review… opens Suggested to clean up, Not now hides it on that Mac until the list changes (`Prefs.going_dismissed`); after a round it says once what went (`Prefs.cleaned_seen`). **Proved by:** `crates/shroom-core/tests/pin.rs`, `autoclean.rs`, `cleanup.rs`; the region 5 inventory; `app/test/windows-gallery.mjs` (every new face). | One name for one idea; one ask for many deletes; Undo as forgiving for a batch as for one | A sheet for Pin (it deletes nothing, and Unpin is one click away); an Undo per row after Delete all (several lines for one act); keeping the word Keep beside Pin |
| B234 | **N75 as built: what the core reads and when, and the command** (in-frame) | **(a) Reads.** The Library's entry reads the suggestions when Suggested to clean up is on *or* auto-cleanup is (so its warning shows with the suggestions off). The popover reads them when its tray icon is clicked, at most once an hour (`SUGGEST_FROM_POPOVER_MS`), so its banner is current without a timer of its own; that read timing out says nothing (`Op::Suggest` is background, law 12). **(b) The editing mark.** While an editor window is open, the core asks the space to mark its video every 5 minutes (`LibCmd::Editing`, `EDITING_MARK_MS`); a failure is only logged: the mark is a guard, not a promise. **(c) A failed Pin** reuses the Library's ratified “Couldn't change that just now — try again in a moment.” (`library.pin-failed`). **(d) The command.** `shroom pin` / `unpin` take ids or links (never title words, as a delete), many at once, each `pinned`, `unpinned`, `unchanged` or `not_found`, `--dry-run` first, no `--yes`; `list` and `info` say “· pinned”, or “· deleted Oct 27 unless pinned”, and carry `pinned` and `deletes_at` in `--json`. `shroom autoclean` reads the setting and what goes next and never changes it (the schedule is the setup Mac's to give). `cleanup-candidates` lists what's going first, soonest first, never a pinned video, the by-age fallback included. **(e) The version** is 0.1.29: the page changed after 0.1.28 was fingerprinted for B230, and rows are never edited (B61). **Proved by:** `crates/shroom-cli/tests/pin.rs`, `cleanup.rs`; `crates/shroom-core/tests/editor.rs` (the mark). | The warning has to show whatever the suggestions switch says; the popover has no other moment to read; the command can read anything a Mac can, but only the setup Mac can give the page its schedule | A timer on the popover's read (wakes for nothing); turning auto-cleanup on from the command (it would need the setup key); new words for a failed Pin (the ratified line says it) |
| B235 | **AI is on by default, and said once** (spec N76; Bagrat, 2026-10-25, relayed by the orchestrator: on by default for new spaces and existing ones, with a banner that says so and a Turn off; the boards “Popover — AI features are turned on…” and its states ratified as drawn, nothing in the setup and nothing in the Library; reverses his 2026-10-18 off by default, N66; amends B206 (e); the build's calls below are made on judgment, for him to overturn) | **(a) New spaces:** the setup's own wiring (`CloudCmd::Wire { fresh: true }`, the runner's `wire::wire_fresh_parts`) puts a page up with the `ai` binding and its schedule where there was none; a page already there keeps what it has, and a repair's wiring (`fresh: false`) never changes it — so a space whose page was lost isn't turned on behind its owner's back. The setup Mac's AI is settled once wiring lands (nothing said: Bagrat asked for nothing in the setup). **(b) The one remembered fact** is the old offer's `once.ai_offer_answered`, read as “the AI is settled on this Mac”: the old Not now, the switch in Settings · Space (either way: a choice), the banner's OK, a Turn off that went through, and a setup on this Mac all set it; nothing else does. No new prefs version and no on-update hook: the old flag already holds the one thing a migration needs to know — whether this Mac ever said no. **(c) Existing spaces:** the turn-on is the banner's — `apply` turns the AI on (`AiTurn::TurningOn`, `SetAi(true)`) in the step where the setup Mac's `config` would show `ai-turned-on` over AI known off (`derive::popover::ai_default_due`: config up, no higher banner, not mid-take, online, nothing re-wiring), never from a background task: nothing is heard before the person has read that it's on. Rejected: turning it on at launch or after the page refresh (silent until the popover opens); a banner that asks first (Bagrat chose on-with-Turn-off). **(d) Failure:** once said, a turn-on that didn't go through is tried again quietly at the old offer's backoff (ten minutes, doubling, at most an hour), with the popover shut too, until it goes through; OK lets it go on. Turn off greys while a turn-on's few seconds run (both re-wire the same page). A Turn off that didn't go through says so under the banner's line (`ai-turn-off-failed`) and is tried again quietly until it goes through or OK. **(e) Members' Macs** learn the space's AI from the ping their standing re-probe already makes (`StandingAnswer::Fine { ai }`), and say once whose account hears their new videos (`ai-on-in-space`, OK only); owner-class Macs without the token say nothing (the setup Mac speaks for the owner). **(f) The banner's second line** is a new optional `Banner.detail`, drawn under a bold first line with the buttons beneath; every other banner keeps one line. **(g) Gone:** N66's offer, its confirm (`Confirm::Ai`), turning-on, failed and on faces, and the actions `AiAsk`, `AiOn`, `AiNotNow`; the switch, its cost line and what the AI does are unchanged. | B237 | **A cue heard in the take's quiet is never cut** (spec N65 (1), in-frame; Bagrat's hand test of 0.1.30-early.1, 2026-10-25: a take with no speech came back with its first 6 s cut) | The planner counts a cue only where the take on the Mac sounds: with its quiet known (the single-file copy's silences at −38 dBFS, the level its edges use), a cue whose words and 0.5 s either side (`CUE_REACH`: word times drift) hold under 0.15 s of sound (`CUE_SOUND`: shorter than a spoken syllable) was written into silence and is left alone: no cut, no question to the judgment. | B239 | **The app links the `shroom` command once, for a Mac installed before it** (spec N62, B217; DEVIATIONS #57, ratified by Bagrat 2026-10-25; found in Bagrat's hand test of 0.1.30-early.1, 2026-10-25: `shroom` not found on a Mac installed around 0.1.2x and updated in-app since) | Only the install line and the cask made the link, and an update replaces the bundle without running either, so every Mac installed before the command shipped had no `shroom` on PATH, though the bundle carried it. **(a) Once per Mac, at launch** (`runtime/cli_link.rs`, `boot`): the app installed at `~/Applications/shroom.app` makes what `link_cli` makes, under its rules — the link `~/.local/bin/shroom` only, never over a file or link of the person's own (then nothing else either); nothing when Homebrew's bin already links it; the marked line (the install line's `CLI_MARK` and `CLI_LINE`, a test holds them equal) appended to `.zprofile` for zsh or `.bash_profile` for bash, only when no shell start file already names `.local/bin` and the mark isn't there; any other shell, no profile. The app's own PATH is launchd's, not the Terminal's, so it reads the start files rather than its environment. Then `cli.json` in shroom's folder records the pass (it outlives Disconnect), so a link the person removes stays removed; the install line still links on every install. Nothing is said: the install line and the cask say nothing about the command either (B217: always installed). A dev build never links (its command would stand in for the real one). **(b) Uninstall shroom… takes it back** (`cli_link::unlink`), as `install.sh --uninstall` does: the link only if it points into this app, the mark, and the line after it only when it's still shroom's own. Before this, the app's own uninstall left the install line's link dangling | Reading the login shell's PATH by running it (`$SHELL -l -c`) runs the person's profile from a background app, and still misses `.zshrc`; linking on every launch (re-makes a link the person removed); only after an update relaunch (misses a Mac that updated before this shipped and then just launches); a one-time line or a Settings row saying the command is ready (a new surface for what the install line does silently) |
| B240 | **A cue with nothing said again never has more than itself cut unasked** (spec N65 (2), in-frame; amends B201 (c) and B202 (c); Bagrat's hand test of 0.1.30-early.1, 2026-10-25: he said “shroom cut” with no slip, carried his sentence on through it, and the strip didn't ask him to check the cut) | The sentence a cue broke off is cut sure only when the judgment hears all of it said again: a start scored 0.4 or more at the sentence's own start or before it. Otherwise, with no answer, all starts low, or only a pause inside the sentence scored, nothing was said again: the planner cuts the cue (sure) and the broken-off sentence as the line before it (low), which the strip asks to check; the finished sentence before it stays, as before. The page asks on until `done` (a title typed meanwhile no longer stops it: the chapters still come), and, when the name lands before the player does, until the player is there. A page that waits for a name carries the player's chapter and caption code from the start. **After an independent review** (four agents and their refuters, before merging): `done` also waits for the take's voice cuts, which the Mac plans from the finished transcript and so land after the name; the page redraws the bar whenever the chapters it is handed change, and asks once more whenever what plays changes (`shroom:keep`: a cut landing, a press on the strip), on any owner page that shows chapters, so the bar always comes to what a reload draws. And the captions' edited clock (`&e=1`, B209 b) is taken only when the saved edit's own copy plays, on the page as served and in place: a baked recording's own `edit-<n>.mp4` with a newer edit still to be copied plays on its own clock, cuts skipped, and its captions ran early before. **The line is gone** from the page, the spec and the canvas board (“The page — the owner's, named from what was said”); the free day's line stays. The page moves to 0.1.31 (B61). | **Proved by:** `worker/test/ai.test.js` › the page (B209) — the poll's answer equals what the reloaded page draws, and the poll run against a stand-in player (chapters and captions wired once, the edited clock's flag for an edit's copy and not a baked file, a typed title, a player still to come, chapters changed after they were drawn, a change of what plays after the asking stopped), the route's `done` waiting for the voice cuts and its `copy`; `tests/e2e/pages-gallery.mjs` › `owner-named-in-place` — real Chromium, the page playing as the video is named under it: the bar's four sections and the tip, the Captions button and a caption at 1:10, the same page, still playing; then a cut of 0:30–1:00 lands under it and the bar comes to the reload's. | **Reloading the page when the name lands** (loses typing and the place in the video). **Swapping the player** (restarts playback). **A second poll for chapters** (the name's poll already runs until naming is done). |
| B242 | **The editor seeks from its ruler; a part cut at an end trims; a marker's flag stands on its line** (spec N63; Bagrat, 2026-10-25, relayed by the orchestrator: drag the playhead's head and click the ruler to seek, as the board “Editor — seeking” draws; of the yellow ends, A+ from “Editor — the yellow ends, A or B”; and two fixes he asked for the same day: the playhead's line never above its circle, a marker's pole the exact continuation of its dashed line) | **(a) Seeking.** The ruler (24 px, a quiet strip that lights under the pointer, a faint line and the time following it) is the seek target: a click seeks as a click on the sound does (snapped to where play goes on); a press there, or on the playhead's circle (`grab`, then `grabbing`), scrubs: the picture follows the raw time, through cuts, and the let-go seeks. The circle held doesn't jump to the pointer. What was playing pauses while held and plays on (`Editor.svelte` `hold`). The waveform keeps its drag-to-select. A cut's scissors, which sit in the ruler's strip, still pick their cut. **(b) Keys:** ← → a second, ⇧ five, ⌥ a frame (1/30 s, shroom's frame clock), Home and End the edit's start and end; never while a field holds the keyboard or a sheet asks. No J/K/L: reverse playback isn't reliable in WebKit. **(c) A+.** A selection's edge within 6 px of a yellow end goes to it (`SNAP`), and such a part, cut (chip or Delete), is sent as `trim`, the same op the ends send, so the edit list, the page, the embed, `shroom edit` and the voice-cut strip see what a dragged end makes; one that reaches both ends is sent as a cut, for the core to refuse. Rejected: B, no ends at all (a new hint for a ratified one, and trimming hidden until found); storing an edge part as a cut (it would list as `m` cuts and put back differently). **(d) Pixels.** The playhead's line starts at its circle's centre (`top: -22px`: the 10 px gap and half the ruler), in every state; a marker is one 1 px column at a whole-pixel left, dashed through the sound and solid above it as the flag's pole (no svg pole), checked at 1× and 2× in WebKit: the same device columns, no gap. |
| B243 | **A Library row is always its record's key** (in-frame hardening, 2026-10-25) | A record read from `meta/<id>.json` is that recording's only when it names the same id: `library::refresh` skips one that names another (so every row, and every Rename, Pin and Delete sent from it, goes by its key), and every by-key read for a change (`read_meta`, `try_update_meta`, the editor's look, the copy's look, the progress beat) refuses one, through `upload::its_own`. The device door already took the key's id. | `crates/shroom-upload/tests/library.rs` › `a_row_is_always_its_records_key` | **Forcing the key's id onto the row** (`Row { id, ..Row::of(&meta) }`): the row would still show a record that isn't that recording's; skipping it is plainer. **Checking in the Meta deserializer**: it doesn't know the key. |
| B244 | **Voice cuts make the edited copy at once** (in-frame, amends B197 (d)'s cadence; 2026-10-25) | When the voice lane lands cuts on a video, or reads back a press on its owner page that changed them, it tells the core (`EditorNews::CutsLanded`), and the editor's upkeep is due at once (`library_ops.upkeep_asked`, read by `derive::next_upkeep`) rather than at its six-hour turn, under its usual guards (never over a take, never offline, never while an editor window is open or the lane is busy). An upkeep that answers or fails after the cuts landed has answered them; a failure keeps its usual wait. So what viewers get leaves the cuts out within minutes of them landing, not hours. | `crates/shroom-core/tests/editor.rs` › `landed_cuts_make_the_upkeep_due_at_once` | **The voice lane making the copy itself**: it would bypass the core's guards (the take's encoder, the editor's lane). **A shorter upkeep cadence for everyone**: more passes over the whole space for one video's need. |
| B245 | **Plays: a ceiling per viewer a day, and a beacon must come from a page** (in-frame hardening of N68 (1) and B210; 2026-10-25; the page moves to 0.1.32) | (a) One viewer (the day's stand-in hash) counts at most `VIEW_PLAYS_PER_VIEWER` = 5 plays a day on one video; past it the beacon writes nothing. Replays under it, another viewer and the next day count as before; ends still land. The day's per-viewer counts sit in `today.p`, dropped with the day as `today.h` is. (b) `POST /_shroom/view/<id>` refuses a beacon carrying neither an `Origin` nor a `Sec-Fetch-Site` header (every browser sends one or the other); WebKit's `Origin: null` page-leave beacon is still taken. | `worker/test/views.test.js` › the ceiling, and the beacon with no browser headers | **Counting a play once per viewer a day**: replays are real plays, and the owner's numbers would change meaning. **A signed token embedded when the page renders**: more moving parts (a cached page would hand everyone one token) for what the ceiling already bounds. **A Workers rate-limiting binding**: a new binding for every space to wire. |
| B246 | **The marker toast: ⌥⌘M answers on screen, left out of the video** (spec N78; Bagrat, 2026-10-25, trying 0.1.30-early.1, asked for it; he approved the board “The bubble — ⌥⌘M answers: the “Marker dropped” toast” as drawn, its words and the numbering, the same day, relayed by the orchestrator) | **(a) A fourth overlay panel**, `toast` (`runtime/overlay.rs`), born at start-up with the popover, the count and the label, on the list of what a take leaves out from the moment it exists, passing every click through, never key. **(b) The core holds what it says** on the open take (`OpenTake.toast`: the words and the press's instant), so it goes with the take; `derive` shows it for `MARKER_TOAST_MS` (2 s) on the take's display (Camera's: where its preview sits), never once the take finishes, and marks it `under_label` when the agent label is on that display. A press while it shows replaces it and starts its two seconds again. `update` puts it away for good whenever the popover or an unfired count is up — so paused with the popover open the row answers and no toast is left for later, and one the popover covered doesn't come back when it goes. Put away: the toast, and a second press at the same pause sets “Already marked at this pause” with no marker. Amends B196 (b)'s “nothing on screen answers it”. **(c) Where:** the pill's top and right edge the label pill's (12 points in, 8 under the menu bar); under the label, the pills 6 points apart (`presenter::toast_frame`, one move per Scene and none when it's there). The panel is 470 × 62 points, the pill at its top right inside a 16 / 12 / 20 margin (right, above, below): room for “Already marked at this pause” with its hint, which the gallery found too wide for the label's 380, and for the pill's shadow. **(d) The fade and the shadow are the page's** (a 2 s CSS animation, restarted on a new `pressed`; the board's `0 4px 14px` shadow on the pill, the panel casting none): macOS casts a panel's shadow once, from what the page held then, so its shadow would neither come with a toast drawn after it nor fade with one. Law 4 and the overlays' surface now name the toast beside the label, as shown to the person and left out of the recording. |
| B247 | **Settings gains a Videos tab and grows to 600 × 720; the galleries hold Settings to its page, and CI checks it** (spec N79; Bagrat, 2026-10-25, relayed by the orchestrator: the tab named Videos, and Space used in the Space tab, on the board “Settings — General outgrew its window”; the window 600 × 720, his 👍 on the tasks page) | **(a) The tabs.** General · Videos · Space · Members. The galleries said 620 because their size reader matched nothing in presenter.rs and kept their fallbacks, and they run only by hand. 0.1.24, the agent card alone and Your videos alone each still fit within the gallery's 620; together, in 0.1.25, they reached 682, and auto-cleanup (N75) took it to 820. | B248 | **A page's version moves only when a build carrying it goes out** (amends B61; Bagrat, 2026-10-25, relayed by the orchestrator: he took the recommended answer on his version card, yes from the next release on, with this round staying 0.1.33) | (a) **Re-stamp until it's out.** While no build of the tree's `VERSION` is published (no `v<VERSION>` tag, release or early), a page change re-stamps that version's row in `worker/page-version.json`; `page-version.test.js` prints the new row. A page fix merged before the next build reuses that build's number. (b) **Frozen once out.** Once `v<VERSION>` is tagged, its row is what installed apps and spaces run: `tools/page-version-out.sh` (CI's web lane) fetches the tag's row and fails when the tree's differs, and the next page change moves the tree up (`tools/bump-version.sh`). B61's other rules stand: VERSION is the newest row, equal to the app's version, and a space takes only a newer page (B50). (c) **No move down to an early build.** The tree always holds its own version's row, so an early build of an unreleased x.y.z would sort below it: `bump-version.sh` no longer makes one, and an early build leads to the version after the tree's (0.1.33-early.1 from an unreleased 0.1.32). An official release after an early build ships the early build's version (RELEASING step 1).
