| B249 | **Turning AI on or off shows its parts as they happen** (spec N80; Bagrat's hand test of 0.1.30-early.1, 2026-10-25: it took long enough to worry and showed little; the boards “Settings — Space: the AI switch while it works…” and “Popover — … Turn off while it works…” approved with the plainer words, 2026-10-25, relayed by the orchestrator; the build's calls below are made on judgment, for him to overturn) | **(a) Three parts, said as they begin.** The cloud lane posts `CloudNews::Part { op: Ai, part }`: 1 before the page's version is asked (then the setup key proved, auto-cleanup's days read, the page read as shroom's), 2 before the script PUT, 3 before its schedule (`wire::set_ai_parts`, `AiPart`). Each is logged with its time (`naming: part n`). **(b) Done is the page's word.** After the schedule the lane asks the page's ping every 0.5 s until it says its AI is on (or off), for at most 150 s, inside the re-wiring's 3 min deadline (N80 (5)); a page that never says so is a didn't-go-through, never a done. **(c) Never stored (law 1).** `space.ai_run` holds which way, the part underway, since when and how it ended; every re-wiring asked (the switch, the banner's Turn off, the quiet retries, the turn-on by default) starts it over at part 1. Settings shows a run while it runs; a done one from the moment it is done until the window closes or the tab changes (a tab change ends the run; a window opened after it was done never shows it; a refocus changes nothing); a stopped one while its failure is this visit's (law 12's existing rule), crossed at the part it stopped at — the first part's stop is the build's form, “Checking your space” (provisional). **(d) The switch shows where it's going** while the run runs (its `on` is the run's), greyed; only the switch greys, the parts stay readable. **(e) Clocks** come from the core as lengths on the part underway, from its first second; the core wakes each second while a run runs. **(f) The long line** at 20 s on one part (the slowest part measured was 6.5 s). **(g) Shared look.** The parts list is one component (`app/src/onboarding/Parts.svelte`, its styles in `app/src/lib/parts.css`) drawn by the setup, Settings and the popover; a stopped part's cross is the theme's red. | Measured on the test account (2026-10-25, six turns, the real page: `tests/e2e-live`, `the_ai_switch_parts_are_timed`): checking the space ~1 s; the script PUT 0.8–1 s, 6.5 s the first time; the schedule ~0.25 s; the page saying so 0.04–0.3 s after. Core tests: `region5.rs` (the parts as they land, done, stopped, long, the clock's wake, the banner's Turn off, and the inventory's rows); `ai_fake.rs` (the parts in order, the confirm). The window gallery's new faces (`space-ai-turning-on`, `space-ai-on-done`, `space-ai-off-stopped`, `space-ai-long`, `ai-turning-off`, `ai-turning-off-long`). | A progress bar or a percentage (no honest length); done as soon as the calls answer (the page is what records; it says so within a second); the parts ticked on a timer; keeping the done list until the window closes regardless of tab (a tab change is an entry, as everywhere in Settings); giving the banner's turn-on by default the parts too (Bagrat, 2026-10-25: no preference; left as it is) |
| B250 | **The owner page's quiet icons are there from the first paint, greyed until ready; the seek head travels inside the bar** (spec `owner-page`, `live`; amends B164, B166, B198 (a) and B221 (b) on the owner page; Bagrat, 2026-10-25, relayed by the orchestrator: Edit and Download coming in only once the video is processed makes sense, but the row flickers, so every icon should always be there, disabled until it's available; and, the same day, at the end of a video the head's right edge must meet the player's edge) | **(a) The icons.** Copy link, Copy thumbnail, Download and Edit in shroom are drawn from the first paint, in that order (#112's Edit chapters goes before the scissors), each in its place. One not ready is greyed as Copy thumbnail already was (`aria-disabled`, the icon dimmed, no hover): Copy thumbnail until the video plays and its picture is stored; Download until the single-file MP4 exists, with no `href`, so it goes nowhere; Edit in shroom until the upload drains, its link kept aside in `data-href`. The status poll wakes each where it stands, and greys Download again if its file goes while the page still asks. No new words: a greyed icon keeps its name as its tooltip, and Copy thumbnail its “Preparing the thumbnail…”. The viewer's page doesn't change: it never polls, so nothing comes in under a viewer's pointer, and what a viewer can't use isn't drawn (B166). **(b) No jump at the go-live or the drain.** The waiting frame keeps the room the player's control bar will take (`--bar`), and a page that opened with the upload line keeps the line's room once the line has gone (`.upl[data-room]`), so in a window short enough to size the column, the column and the icons stay where they are both when the player comes in and when the line goes. An open page keeps the box it opened with: a take whose shape isn't 16:9 is letterboxed in it until the page is reloaded, and nothing moves for it. **(c) The head.** The bar was a range input with `step="0.1"` and the video's length as its `max`: at the end, a length that isn't a whole number of tenths held the value under it, so the head stopped 3–12 px short of the edge while the played part ran on past it. Now the bar's `max` is the length rounded to a tenth and the end sets the value to it, so the head reaches the edge; the step stays a tenth of a second (`step="any"` would make Safari's VoiceOver adjust do nothing). The played part is drawn on the head's travel from the bar's own value (`calc(var(--th)/2 + var(--p)*(100% - var(--th)))`, the head 14 px), the loaded part on to the bar's end once it's all loaded; the chapters' first and last sections reach the bar's ends, padded by the head's radius, so each boundary falls where the head's centre stands at that time; the tip under a pointer and above a finger place time as the head does. **(d) A change to what plays** (a press on the cut strip, cuts landing) greys Download at once and starts the poll again if it had stopped, so a stale copy never answers 404 from an awake icon; the poll wakes it when the new file is up. **After an independent review** (three reviewers and their refuters, before merging) the step, the loaded end, the chapters' ends, (b)'s drain and (d) were added or corrected; a Copy thumbnail opened while an edit waits for its copy still waits for a reload to make its picture (older than this; a follow-up). The page carries 0.1.32 (B248). | **Proved by:** `worker/test/owner.test.js` › the title's quiet icons (order, greyed markup before the go-live and before the record, awake when ready, the poll's wake and rest, the viewer's page unchanged); `worker/test/page.test.js` and `ai.test.js` (the step, the track's mapping, the chapters' inset and the tip's); `tests/e2e/pages-gallery.mjs` › `owner-icons-in-place` (Chromium: opened while it uploads, every icon there and greyed, a greyed Download goes nowhere, then each wakes with nothing moving) and the `seek-head-*` faces (the head's edges read from the bar's pixels at 0:00 and at the end, a loaded bar's last pixels, the chapters' boundaries against the head's travel, on the viewer's page, the owner's, the embed and a chaptered bar, at 900 and 390 px, WebKit and Chromium); the icons face reads the row again once the upload line has gone; `worker/dev/page-drive.mjs`'s owner checks follow. All failed on main. | **Hidden until ready** (what was there: the row moved under the pointer three times). **A reason as the greyed icon's tooltip** (drafts went to Bagrat through the orchestrator; until he picks words, none). **The picture's box as the head's box in fullscreen and the embed** (the picture is letterboxed there; the head stays inside the player's box). |
| B251 | **Upload credentials live an hour, minted again as an upload goes on** (spec N81, amends N32's line; Bagrat, 2026-10-25, relayed by the orchestrator: one-hour keys, and the Remove sheet's and the FAQ's lines as drafted) | The Worker's mint (`TTL.upload`) and the token Mac's local mint (`TTL_SECONDS`) issue an upload credential for 3600 s. A credential with under `CRED_LEFT_MS` (15 min) left is minted again for the same recording before use, budget-free: by the uploader every pass, during a take too (`LiveUpload::renew`); by the voice lane, through `uploader::take_client`; and a stale one refused mid-upload on the token Mac is dropped and re-minted, as a member Mac's is. A removed Mac's re-mint is refused, so its upload ends within the hour; the core's `can_send` counts a credential while more than `CRED_LEFT_MS` is left. The member mint's golden vector moves to the hour on both signers. | `crates/shroom-core/tests/truth.rs` › `once_removed_a_take_sends_only_while_its_credential_has_time_left`; `crates/shroom-cloud/tests/member_vector.rs` with `worker/test/region6.test.js` (the same JWT, computed independently); `app/src-tauri/src/runtime/uploader.rs` › `a_take_holding_a_credential_with_time_left_can_still_finish` | **Seven days as before**: a removed member's credentials outlived Remove by up to a week. **Fifteen minutes**: a re-mint every few minutes of every upload, for little more. **Per-member storage limits now**: after launch, with the owner seeing each member's usage (design first). |
| B252 | **Copy thumbnail follows the first frame it draws from on an open owner page** (N46, N63; the orchestrator, 2026-10-25, relaying Bagrat's rule that a button that never gets ready is a bug, after the review of B250 found it) | An owner page opened while an edit waits for its permanent copy has no first frame to draw the picture from (`thumbSource` is the copy's), and the page never learned of one later, so Copy thumbnail stayed greyed until a reload; and a press that made the copy stale left the old copy's picture awake. The status now names what Copy thumbnail draws from now, `drawFrom: {from, psrc, tsrc, teaser} \| null` (the same the page is served with), and the open owner page follows it on every answer: the copy landing, the edit put back, a press making the copy stale (greyed, nothing to draw from yet; B250's press already starts the poll again). A new first frame starts the drawing over; a picture says the first frame it began from (`x-shroom-from`), and one begun before the drawing started over is never stored, so a picture of a part an edit cut never goes up under the copy's name. | **Proved by:** `worker/test/thumb.test.js` (the status's `drawFrom` as the page as served would draw, null while the copy is on its way; the drawing's `source`; the frame a picture began from, and the superseded one not stored), `edits.test.js` (the status with `drawFrom`), and `tests/e2e/pages-gallery.mjs` › `owner-thumb-after-copy` and `owner-thumb-after-undo` (Chromium: the copy lands, or the edit is put back, under the open page; the button wakes with no reload, the stored picture's `from` the copy's first frame or the recording's own). | B253 | **A release is built with no key on the runner and signed only from main, v\* tags and the early branch** (in-frame hardening of B102 and B220; 2026-10-25) | (a) **Build, then sign.** The release job runs the one release build `--adhoc` first, so every build script, proc-macro and package runs before the identity is imported; then it imports the identity (non-extractable, `security import -x`; the partition list Apple's tools only) and signs the finished bundle, its encoder and its command with `tools/release-build.sh --resign` — the bundler's options (hardened runtime, the app's entitlements, default identifiers), checked as an `--identity` build is, now including the runtime flag and both entitlements on every binary. CI's ad hoc job rehearses `--resign` with a throwaway identity. (b) **Who may sign.** An early build is published from `claude/early-integration` only (`EARLY_BRANCH`); the check job decides whether a run signs (a tag, `main`, or an early publish from that branch), and any other run — a rehearsal off `main` — runs outside the `release` environment and keeps its build ad hoc. (c) RELEASING's one-time setup makes the environment's required reviewer and its deployment branches and tags (`main`, `claude/early-integration`, `v*`) required. | The 0.1.27 release's signatures (runtime flag and entitlements on all three binaries), matched by `--resign`; CI's Release build (ad hoc) with the throwaway identity's `--resign`, and the Signing rehearsal | **Signing in a separate job** (the bundle carried between jobs as an artifact: a second runner and an upload for what one step after the build does). **`tauri bundle` after the build** (the CLI's own rebuild rules, untested here). **Re-signing with `--preserve-metadata`** (whatever the ad hoc build set, rather than what a release needs). |
| B255 | **The Mac app checks what it's handed: updates by their own version, storage by its host, takes by their names, windows by their label** (in-frame hardening of B31, B125, B26, B32 and N62; 2026-10-25) | (a) **Updates.** After `same_signer`, `apply()` reads the staged copy's own `Contents/Info.plist` (`shroom_update::check_staged`): its `CFBundleShortVersionString` must be exactly the version offered, newer than the one running, and on the official channel no early build; anything else is discarded as a signature that doesn't match is (`FailClass::Refused`). A dev build's stand-in may serve the tree's early version. (b) **The email probe.** It writes `shroom-probe` only when no Worker has the name (`worker_fact`), else a fresh `shroom-probe-<8 hex>`, and with neither free leaves the gate to wiring's own classification; one already there is never written or deleted, and the probe it made goes by a plain DELETE (`Cloud::remove_worker`), never `?force=true`. (c) **Storage.** A release build uses a credential only when it names `https://<32 hex>.r2.cloudflarestorage.com` and a bucket of 3–63 `[a-z0-9-]` (`device::r2_store`, `Runtime::store_ok`), where a mint answer arrives and where `cred.json` is read back; dev builds keep the fake, the local space and the e2e run's R2. A listing stops at a token it already followed or past 10 000 pages; a download to disk stops past the length the store said, else 64 GiB, and its `.tmp` goes. (d) **Takes.** `takes::dirs` keeps the real folders named as take folders are (`posters::valid_dir`), never a link; the upload reads an init only by the encoder's names (`files::is_init`) and refuses a playlist naming another. (e) **Windows.** `commands::surface` binds every surface to its window's label, as an editor's already was; `paste_secret` answers only the setup and Settings, `field` only those and the Library and Send feedback; the capability grants the pages only `core:event:allow-listen`, `allow-unlisten` and a debug build's devtools key, for `core:default`. (f) **Who asked.** Each `control: ask` line carries the calling process's pid (`LOCAL_PEERPID`) and program (`proc_pidpath`); how a start goes is unchanged (N62). | `shroom-update` › `reads_a_bundles_own_version`, `a_staged_copy_fits_only_as_the_offered_newer_version_of_this_channel`; `shroom-cloud/tests/wizard_fake.rs` › `a_worker_already_named_as_the_probe_survives_a_gates_pass`; `device` › `a_credential_names_r2s_own_store_or_none`; `s3` › `a_listing_that_doesnt_move_on_stops`, `a_body_past_its_ceiling_is_an_error_and_leaves_nothing`; `shroom-upload/tests/inits.rs` › `a_playlist_naming_any_other_init_is_refused`; `playlist` › `an_init_is_named_as_the_encoder_names_it`; on the Mac: `commands` › `a_surface_is_only_its_own_windows`, `pastes_and_fields_come_only_from_the_windows_that_have_them`; `presenter` › `the_pages_may_only_listen`; `takes` › `the_takes_are_real_folders_named_as_takes_are`; `control` › `the_log_says_which_process_asked`; `runtime` › `a_release_build_stores_only_on_r2`; `updater` › `a_staged_copy_of_another_version_is_refused`. Tauri 2.11.6's own permission sets (`core:event:default` holds `emit` and `emit_to`; `core:default` the tray's and menu's): the pages use `listen` alone. | **A signed manifest** (a key kept apart from CI, checked before `offer()`): the stronger proof of a release, but a key and a release step of its own — its own decision. **Checking the version at `offer()`** only: the manifest says what to fetch, not what was fetched. **Probing under a random name every time**: a Worker per pass in the account's list while it lives, where the plain name is almost always free. **Pinning the endpoint to the space's own account id**: a member Mac doesn't always hold it; the shape and https keep the bytes on R2. **A partial listing on a stuck token**: a caller would act on a view that isn't whole. **Following links and checking where they lead**: a take is never a link, so none is followed. **The Tauri app manifest and a capability per window** (`AppManifest::commands`): a build-time change of its own, for what the label binding already gives each command. **A mandatory agent label, or a first-start notice**: the spec intends `--no-label` and a start without the count (N62); a notice would be new words. |

## Deviations from the spec

*(Cited as DEVIATIONS #n — numbered #1 on, a new entry is the next number. Until
2026-10-14 they were their own file, `DEVIATIONS.md`.)*

> Where the build departs from the spec (`spec/machine.json`; before B133, `spec/UX-STATEMACHINE.md`), or settles something the spec leaves
> to the build in a way Bagrat should see. Each entry: what, why, the evidence, and how
> to reverse it. **Nothing here changes the visible behavior of a ratified state** — that
> would stop the build and ask (TESTING-PLAN §7). Bagrat ratifies or rejects each entry at
> a checkpoint; a rejected entry is reverted, not argued.

### From the core's design (ARCHITECTURE §3.8, B22) — 2026-10-02

| # | What | Why | Reverse by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | “Set up my space” is remembered across a relaunch (`prefs.setup_chosen`), cleared by Later/✕, by provisioning, and by exit | Makes N19's “relaunch mid-signup re-enters at `account`” reachable after a quit; it records a choice the person made, never a screen | Dropping the field: a relaunch then always opens `welcome` |
| 2 | An invite that arrives is saved to `~/.shroom/invite` at once, not only on Later | A crash while it waits out a take (law 4) must not lose it | Saving only on Later |
| 3 | For the quiet-take law, “a take is open” starts at the countdown | Stricter law 4: nothing opens over the scrim | Starting at capture |
| 4 | The recording id is minted when capture starts, as the bucket prefix uploads need; it becomes a **link** only through Stop or Get link (`publish.json` + the meta claim) | Upload while recording (`e3c70a8`) needs the prefix before Stop; law 3 holds at the link level. TESTING-PLAN §2.3's wording “a link id is minted only by Stop or Get link” becomes “a recording becomes a link only by Stop or Get link” | Uploading only after Stop (slower Stop → live) |
| 5 | A Mac in a space whose permissions chapter (row six) isn't done: the tray click opens the plan at row six, not `config` | The chapter is per Mac and lives in the plan; `config` would offer Start before macOS allows recording | Opening `config` with the recording-off banner instead |
| 6 | Capture dying mid-take (no frames past a deadline, a finalize failure) is a stop the person didn't make: a saved take, and the `config` popover opens by itself with the recovered-take banner | Law 2 (never lose a take) and no tray stuck red with Stop unreachable; N39's unasked-popover rule covers stops you didn't make. *Built 2026-10-02 (DECISIONS B47 b): the recorder's watchdog — the encoder failing or taking no frame for 5 s, the source saying it stopped, a camera silent for 5 s; a stall under switched-off screen recording is `permission-lost` instead* | — (the alternative is a stuck recording) |
| 7 | ~~A confirmed tombstone removes the local copy~~ — **not adopted**: the build follows §5.5/N35 — the take stops uploading and its row turns “Removed from the space” with Save to Movies and Discard… | Law 2 as the spec wrote it | — |
| 8 | New numbers: the stop's reachability probe capped at 600 ms; a restore window honored for 2 min; the monthly re-confirm primed after 25 days (run at `config`, `settle::due_reconfirm`, B45 f); backoff 5 s doubling to 2 min; deadlines 5 s (a recorder's start), 60 s (a pause, resume or stop under way, B226; cloud calls), 3 min (wiring); “Copied” for 1.5 s; the disk (B47 a): a 1 GB margin, `disk-low` under 30 minutes, the floor at one minute (the spec’s “about a minute”) plus the single-file copy still to come, looked at every second mid-take; a stall after 5 s (B47 b); the grants re-read every 2 s mid-take (B47 c); the page’s upload no-progress window, 120 s (`STALL_SECONDS`, the Worker) | Scaffold flags the spec left open | Changing the constants |
| 9 | macOS's own quit-and-reopen during `perm-screen` is recognized by a `restore.json` written on the way out and honored for 2 min | Launch provenance isn't visible to the app (scaffold flag, to be probed). B45 a now reads a login launch from its launch event and the install script's launches from their flags; macOS's quit-and-reopen wasn't probed for a mark, so the file stays | Probing a real signal and replacing this |
| 10 | The paused popover after a click-pause counts as asked (it may take the keyboard), though the click came before the pause was confirmed | The person clicked; Return = Resume is the ratified path | — |

### From Bagrat's hands on the preview — the popover, the bubble and the count on a real desktop — 2026-10-13

| # | What | Why | Reverse by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 39 | **The popover follows you: switching apps or Spaces no longer closes it.** The spec closes a tray popover when it loses focus (“Tray popovers dismiss on focus loss”, SM:404; `config`'s “click-away or Esc dismisses it”; the “click away or Esc” edges). From here on, losing the keyboard is not a dismissal: turning to another app or moving to another Space — by ⌘-Tab, the Dock, Mission Control, a swipe — leaves the popover — and with it the bubble, born at `config` (N22) — on screen, on the Space you went to. Hiding shroom (⌘H — shroom's key whenever a popover has the keyboard, whichever app is in front) is the same: the Library and Settings go, the popover, the bubble and a count that is playing stay (B112). It closes on Esc, a click on the mushroom, its own buttons, a **click anywhere outside shroom's own overlays** (another app's window, the desktop, the menu bar, one of shroom's own windows — “click away”, the spec's words, taken literally), and whatever the core closes it for. A click that is itself the switch is not a click away: on the Dock, or on a window or a Space in Mission Control — the popover is still there when you arrive. **As built, and as far as it was measured** (macOS 15.7.7 and 26.6.2, one display; each place put to the popover's watch from inside shroom, since nothing may click in another app): a click on the Dock closes nothing, and neither does any click while Mission Control is up. What tells shroom Mission Control is up can't tell it from Show Desktop or App Exposé, so while either of those is up no click closes the popover either — one on bare desktop included, which on a plain desk does close it. **Not measured:** a click in the app switcher (⌘-Tab held, an icon clicked) — it is a switch only if it lands on something of the Dock's, which nobody has read, and otherwise it closes the popover; Stage Manager's strip (taken to be another process's, not the Dock's — which would make a click there a click away); a second display; macOS 14; ⌘-Tab from a real keyboard and a click from a real hand (another app was brought forward and the Space changed without either). Every face the same: `config`, `paused`, the questions, the banners. Two things stand as they were: a popover that opened by itself and that you haven't touched is not closed by a click somewhere else (it never was — it had no keyboard to lose — and a pause you didn't make must still be there when you look); and a click in it, or on the mushroom, makes it yours (law 5: only then may it take the keyboard) **For Bagrat to ratify:** that losing the keyboard no longer closes the popover, and three calls inside it — a click on the Dock or in Mission Control closes nothing; with Show Desktop or App Exposé up a click on the bare desktop closes nothing either; a popover that opened by itself and that you haven't touched is not closed by a click somewhere else (BUILD-STATUS items 33 to 35). **Ratified by Bagrat** (2026-10-14, BUILD-STATUS item 35: all ratified) | Bagrat, on the preview (2026-10-12): the popover and the camera bubble stayed on top when opened over a full-screen window, but opened on a desktop they vanished when he switched to another full-screen app. The spec's reason for the old rule is the wizard's browser round-trip (“the user would come back holding a token with nowhere to paste it”) — and the wizard is a window. `config` has no round-trip, and “you see and place yourself before Start” (N22) can't survive a glance at another app if the glance closes it. The click away keeps a popover from lingering once you've turned to something else for good | `Popover.svelte` dismissing on the webview's `blur` again, and the click watch removed (`runtime/overlay.rs`) |
| 40 | **Every overlay can sit on any Space — another app's full-screen Space too — and showing one never moves you.** The spec doesn't say where the popover, the bubble, the countdown and Camera's preview go when the app in front is full screen. From here on each of them joins every Space and sits above a full-screen window; showing one never changes the active Space and never brings shroom forward. The countdown plays over whatever you are looking at on the chosen display and takes the keyboard there (its Esc “must work without any extra macOS permission”, SM:1720); when it ends you are where you were. A popover you asked for has the keyboard at once (law 5 as written, law 8's *click the mushroom, press Return*); and a click on any of its controls acts the first time, whether or not the popover has the keyboard at that moment — that click also gives it the keyboard. **For Bagrat to ratify:** every overlay on any Space and above a full-screen window, the count taking the keyboard where you are and leaving you there, and a first click that acts (BUILD-STATUS items 33 and 35). **Ratified by Bagrat** (2026-10-14, BUILD-STATUS item 35: all ratified) | Bagrat asked for the popover always on top, so it works over full-screen windows; a take started in a full-screen window had its countdown move to a desktop and stay there, where it should count down over the active window; and the popover didn't take focus when opened, so his first click on Show yourself only focused it and only the second checked the box. No ratified sentence bends: the overlays are “never windows” (SM:1720) — as built, two of them were | Each overlay staying on the Space it opened on (no Space behaviour on the panels), and the countdown an ordinary window that brings shroom forward |

Now Show yourself lists every camera here, then **Camera off** — picking a camera turns the bubble on with it, Camera off turns it off and keeps the pick for next time (`prefs.camera`, the first-ever default still off, N22); the microphone lists every microphone, then **No mic** — picked, the take records silently and macOS is never asked about a microphone (`prefs.mic_off`); and the source picker's camera choice reads **Camera only**. With Camera only as the source, Show yourself stays live as the pick of the camera the take records, and Camera off isn't offered (picking that camera never turns the bubble on for later display takes). What stands: shroom never picks an iPhone by itself (N39) — an iPhone is listed, and is used only when the person picks it; a picked camera that leaves falls back to the one shroom would pick, the pick kept for its return; a denied camera shows Camera off with “Camera is off for shroom —” [ Open System Settings ], the wish kept (N39); a denied microphone shows No mic, nothing to pick, with its hint; no microphone at all shows No mic and “No microphone — your video will be silent.” The words “Camera only”, “Camera off” and “No mic” are Bagrat's (copy.rs, outside the registry until the spec takes them). **Ratified by Bagrat in the asking** (2026-10-13: Show yourself a picker too, listing the cameras and Camera off, with No mic for the microphone; the displays list with Camera only in place of Camera, which keeps the camera picker enabled and hides the no-camera choice). ### From the heartbeat's move into a Tart clone — two bars no VM holds — 2026-10-14

| # | What | Why | Reverse by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 49 | **In a Tart clone, two of the heartbeat's bars are judged against the clone's own alarm, not the product's bar: skipped frames (product ≤ 0.5 %, clone alarm ≤ 2.5 %) and Stop → the owner's keyed page (product ≤ 1 s, clone alarm ≤ 3 s).** A miss of the product's bar is said; only a miss past the alarm fails (`MAC_CLONE_ALARMS` in `tests/l5/heartbeat-vm.sh`). The alarms are regression alarms, set about twice the clone's worst run, so they catch a gross regression (the stop path's network work doubled, capture falling badly behind) and miss a small one. The other five bars are judged as they are. The product's bars are unchanged. Bagrat's Mac, the one machine that held them, no longer runs dev builds (`dev/proposals/heartbeat-in-vm.md` §3), so for now no machine judges these two at the product's bar: a small change there is decided from the figures, by eye (Bagrat's call, 2026-10-14: alarms over leaving them unjudged) | Five clone runs on 2026-10-14 (`main` at `b4cd764`), every one missing both. **Skipped:** 0.96 / 1.27 / 0.96 / 1.27 / 0.63 %, all at capture (`skippedByCapture` = `skipped`) on the clone's virtual 2048×1536 display; the hosted runner, also a VM, skips 1.26 %; Bagrat's Mac the same day: 0. **Keyed page:** 2,112 / 2,106 / 1,643 / 2,083 / 1,486 ms, against 950 ms on his Mac; the slow step is the storage round trip (1,069–1,706 ms against 577 ms), and uploads after Stop take 11–14.5 s against 5.8 s. Tried: bridged networking instead of Tart's NAT (2,083 ms, no change) and 6 cores / 12 GB instead of 4 / 8 (1,486 ms, still over, and repeated frames rose to 1.58 % at load 10). The guest's own network stack and virtual display are the cause, not the app: the clean link (213–345 ms), take end (56–68 ms) and finalize (0 ms) hold in every run | Judging both at the product's bar again in the clone (drop `MAC_CLONE_ALARMS` in `heartbeat-vm.sh`) once a clone holds them — a faster guest network or display, a newer Tart — or a judged run on real hardware if Bagrat allows one |

Three more lines, each with its own next step: a release that's ours to fix (a manifest or file that answers 4xx, a malformed manifest, a zip with no shroom.app — DEVIATIONS #24 had routed these to the checksum line) reads “This release isn't all there on our side, so nothing was installed. Run the same command in Terminal — if it repeats, tell us: …”; and somewhere restricted reads “This looks like it's running somewhere restricted, so nothing was installed — run the same command in Terminal.” — said before anything is fetched when the environment says it's a coding agent's sandbox (`SANDBOX_RUNTIME=1`, Claude Code's; `CODEX_SANDBOX`, Codex's), and in place of `install-no-room`'s line when a write fails with “Operation not permitted” (a Seatbelt deny; a full disk or a plain permission still reads no-room). Exit 1 for all, as before; the update lane still adds “Nothing was changed.”. **Awaiting Bagrat:** the three lines (copy table) | Bagrat's field report, 2026-10-14: Claude Code ran the 0.1.9 install line inside its macOS sandbox and got the checksum line though the zip matched manifest.txt (`6cab47da…`). Under Claude Code's sandbox (code.claude.com/docs/en/sandboxing; sandbox-runtime's Seatbelt profile) writes are allowed only in the working directory and its temp folder, the network goes through a proxy that answers a host it doesn't allow with 403, `open`'s Apple events are denied, and `com.apple.trustd.agent` is reachable only with `enableWeakerNetworkIsolation` — the docs' own note that `gh` and `gcloud` fail TLS verification under Seatbelt is the same trust service. A blocked host gives curl `http_code` 000 (CONNECT 403 shows only in `http_connect`; reproduced against a refusing proxy), so it reads the connection line; the download was fine, so the fingerprint passed; the step left that says the checksum line after a good fingerprint is `codesign --verify`, whose Developer ID chain needs that trust service — the most likely cause. One line for five checks couldn't tell them apart, and “Run it once more” could never fix a sandbox *Corrected the same evening:* the cause was neither — the pin's line (`-R="=…"`, B125) hands codesign `==…`, a requirement syntax error, on every Mac (the hosted Mac, no sandbox, the real 0.1.9: [run 38630403548](https://github.com/bagrat/shroom-archive/actions/runs/38630403548); `-R "=…"` exit 0, a wrong leaf exit 3). The installer and the updater (`shroom_update::same_signer`, same form) now pass `-R` and its text apart, each held by a test on the real codesign. And the environment check missed Claude Code's sandbox (sandbox-runtime sets `SANDBOX_RUNTIME` with its proxy environment), so a signature that fails where Calculator's fails too now reads as somewhere restricted | Back to one line: route the release, signature and restricted cases to `install-checksum-fail`'s and `install-no-room`'s lines again and drop the `restricted` check (`installer/install.sh`) |

### From Bagrat's hands on 0.1.10 — the popover's “Fully live ✓” — 2026-10-14

| # | What | Why | Reverse by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 52 | **No “Fully live ✓” in the popover either**: when the last upload finishes, config's status line just goes, as the page's upload line does (#46 a). The spec's status line said it once after the queue drained, until the popover closed (N38; `config`'s said-line, now removed from `spec/machine.json`). The status line keeps its other facts: uploading, waiting for connection, the update's one-shots. The state that only it needed (`World.last_live`, set when a video finished uploading) is gone. **Ratified by Bagrat** (2026-10-14, in the orchestrator session, after using 0.1.10 on his Mac: he would remove the popover's “Fully live” footer) | The row pushed the popover's footer down 36 px for a fact the person can't act on (`dev/mac/MANUAL.md`), and the page had already dropped the same line | `copy::fully_live`, `World.last_live` (set in `apply.rs` when a video's upload drains) and its arm in `derive/popover.rs`'s `status_line` back; `config.fully-live` back in `spec/machine.json`; the canvas's status-lines board gets its row back |

### From Bagrat's hands on 0.1.10 — a Camera-only take lost in silence — 2026-10-14

| # | What | Why | Reverse by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 53 | **A take whose capture died with nothing to keep is said at `config`.** When the recorder's watchdog calls capture dead (`FrameStall`, a mid-take `Failed`, a failed finalize), the recorder lets it go (`Abandon`) and now, still holding the take's lock, runs the scan's own repair: if the folder holds nothing a repair can save (no frame ever reached it), it answers `NothingKept` instead of `Abandoned`. `config` — already open by itself after a capture dies — then shows a status line, once, until the popover closes (a new take clears it too): “Your camera didn't send a picture, so nothing was recorded — try again.” for Camera only, “Your screen didn't send a picture, so nothing was recorded — try again.” for a display. The face is `take-lost`; Start stays the default, so Return tries again. A take that kept anything still comes back as the recovered take's banner (N25, DEVIATIONS #6), and a capture nobody asked for (Started after the count was cancelled) says nothing. The folder stays where it is. **Ratified by Bagrat** (2026-10-14, relayed by the orchestrator: the wording OK'd); drawn on the canvas board “Popover — a take that kept nothing” | Law 2 (“Nothing shroom does ever loses a take”) can't be kept for a take that recorded nothing, but it was being broken in silence: Bagrat's 0.1.10 checkpoint, Camera only — started, five seconds, `FrameStall`, `capture-died`, `abandoned`, and the popover back at ready with no banner, no take and no word (twice). The spec has no state for a capture that dies before anything is kept; N25's banner needs a saved file | The core's `capture_died` going straight back to a silent `config` (the build before this entry); `RecNews::NothingKept`, `Session::take_lost` and the two lines gone |

### From the first two-VM stranger run (CHECKPOINT §10, 0.1.12) — wiring's offline line — 2026-10-15

| # | What | Why | Reverse by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 54 | **Inside wiring, an unanswered call is a calm retry, not `wizard-offline`.** The spec sends wiring to `wizard-offline` on a transport failure. Wiring runs only after the token's proof has just answered, so a call inside it that goes unanswered (Cloudflare's API or the storage endpoint) now retries under wiring's calm line (`FailClass::Timeout`, `runtime/cloud.rs` `unreached`) and is logged as such. A network that really dropped fails the next attempt's proof, which is still a transport failure: the offline line shows then. No said-line changes | Bagrat's stranger run on a brand-new Cloudflare account: ~1 min of transport errors after the first workers.dev claim, then two 90-s waits for the page, while the VM was online; the window flipped between the wiring line and “Everything so far is saved — I'll pick up right here when you're back online.” The new name's certificate went live ~3.5 min after the claim. A slow first certificate is not offline (Bagrat: never show the offline line while the network is fine) | Mapping both transport arms of `wire_space` back to `FailClass::Transport` |

### From the editor build (N63, B197) — 2026-10-18

| # | What | Why | Reverse by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 55 | **`editor-keep-original`: Yes, delete is filled and leads, but Return does nothing.** B197 (b) and N63 had Yes, delete as the question's Return default (filled, Return), since Bagrat asked for deleting the original soon to be the choice shroom encourages. Law 9 keeps destruction one deliberate act away: a destructive question has no Return default. The build keeps his look, Yes, delete filled and leading, and makes it a click only: on the question Return does nothing, and Esc or ✕ is Keep for 2 days (`derive/editor.rs`: no Return action for `editor-keep-original`, Esc `KeepOriginal`; `crates/shroom-core/tests/editor.rs`; the editor drive on the hosted Mac presses Return there and finds the record unchanged; `spec/machine.json`'s state and its Yes, delete, `return_default: false`). A filled destructive button is itself a bend of law 9's “never filled”, kept as he asked. **Ratified by Bagrat**, as built (2026-10-18) | Deleting the original can't be undone (the bake deletes its files and the edit is final), and the question appears by itself when the copy lands, so a Return meant for the window a moment before (Close, while the final video was being made) would land on it | Making Yes, delete the Return default again: `Action::DeleteOriginal` as `editor-keep-original`'s Return in `derive/editor.rs`, `return_default: true` in the spec, and the tests' asserts turned round |

### Local diagnostics for daily use — a report's log reaches back a day — 2026-10-18

| # | What | Why | Reverse by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 56 | **A report carries the last 1,000 lines of shroom's log, not 200** (N53, N60 say “the last 200 lines”). The app and the page keep the same count (`report::LOG_LINES`, `site/clean.js`); the Worker takes a log of up to 120,000 characters (was 48,000) in a body of up to 512 KB (was 64 KB). Each line of `run.log` now opens with its UTC wall clock (`2026-10-18T17:52:03Z`), and the boot's first line, and the first line of a fresh log after its 5 MB roll, name the build: `shroom <version> · macOS <version> · <chip>`. What the report window shows and says is unchanged; its log is longer. N53's and N60's summaries in `spec/machine.json` now say 1,000. **Ratified by Bagrat** (2026-10-18, relayed by the orchestrator: 1,000 lines) | Bagrat asked (2026-10-18) for enough on his own Mac to report a problem from a day's use. A day of lines in the app's shapes (`core` per event, the recorder, uploader, power and net lines; one progress line per few segments): six 5-minute takes ≈ 360 lines (≈ 21 KB), fifteen ≈ 830 (≈ 50 KB). 200 lines held about three takes. 1,000 holds a busy day today, or a normal one once the features log about twice as much, at ~60 characters a line. The bound is the Worker's CPU: it cleans the log once more on the free plan's 10 ms; measured in Node, a cold clean of 200 lines took ~2.5 ms, 1,000 ~6 ms, 2,000 ~12 ms | `LOG_LINES` back to 200 on both sides, `LOG_CHARS` and the Worker's `log` back to 48,000, `MAX_BYTES` to 64 KB |

Cloudflare's $5/month plan lifts the limit.” (*was:* “…about 1,800 times a day in total…”), for the single-file playback that ships in 0.1.17's page (B177). *The privacy page's AI point ends with voice cuts' sentence (Bagrat, 2026-10-18, approved with the hint; B203; on the canvas board “AiPrivacy”):* “When you say “shroom cut”, the slip is cut from what viewers get; the words cut stay in your space, and only you see them.” *The voice-cuts sentence, the FAQ's “What do I need?” and its removal answer (Bagrat, 2026-10-25, approved as drafted; B251, N81):* “When you say “shroom cut”, viewers skip the slip at once, and it’s gone from the video itself, usually within minutes, once your Mac has saved the edited copy; the words cut stay in your space, and only you see them.” (*was:* “…the slip is cut from what viewers get…”); “…and a Cloudflare account (setup walks you through making one; if you already use Cloudflare for other things, a separate free account just for shroom keeps them apart).”; “…though a video already on its way has up to an hour to finish.” (*was:* “…may still finish.”) |
